Measured with `--sections`, `--profile` (a sampling profile of the audio thread by program counter, `scripts/bench-profile.py` maps it to functions) and `--micro` (the kernels one by one, microseconds per call); the base is commit 2952f44e.

**Causes (file:line in 2952f44e).** Convolve is not one of them: `kFxConvolve` is not in the tier `low` mask, `fx_chain.cpp:330` keeps the module from running, no `fx: convolve` section appears in the Key 61 report, and `core/tests/test_own_convolve_bypass.cpp` shows the render with Convolve on equal to the one with it off at tier low (and different at high). Sample voice (`sample_voice.cpp`): per sample region and loop bounds in doubles, the pan `cos`/`sin` (465), `log2` for the mip level (103), `floor` and 64 bit conversions of a double (25: `__aeabi_d2lz` / `l2d`, library calls on 32 bit ARM), the loop crossfade `cos`/`sin` in double (452), `pow` twice per control period (390). Granular (`granular.cpp`): `log2` per grain sample (79), `windowValue` with two divisions per sample (334), `floor` and 64 bit conversions in the Hermite read (67), a test per sample for the spawn (299). Spectral: the analysis is already made once at load (`content_set.cpp:115`) and shared; the stall is the note start, which synthesises 3 frames (543) plus the first hop, each a full length scalar complex FFT (755; its time on the Key 61 is in the observations), 1025 `cos` + `sin` (747), `fmodf` per bin (716), scalar shaping loops with `cos` / `%` in the warps (281, 307). Shepard Ascent: the morph cycle is rebuilt every control block per voice (scalar `inverseFft`, `morph.cpp:54`), 4 builds a block on the XL (its share of the block is in the observations).

**Fixes** (each with a scalar form of the same bits and a test, `--scalar-content` runs the old code with the same binary; `--scalar-warp` also covers the morph transform): block kernels for the sample voice (identical samples; the loop crossfade weights in float lanes, 3e-6), the granular voice (reciprocal window, at most 3e-5 of a block's peak), the spectral voice (half length NEON transform, polynomial sine / cosine, the exact old phase wrap, vector shaping loops, the priming frames sharing one shaping (since round 10 only frames 1 .. 3 of the priming, deferred as their weight asks: engine-spec 07 section 4a), cached band and comb gains), the morph transform (NEON, identical bits). On the Key 61's Cortex-A17 a Q-register NEON operation costs about two cycles and 64 bit integer / double conversions are library calls: kernels count instructions and use 32 bit integers there.

**Render equality** (`scripts/content-kernel-compare.py` on `synthyboy_preset_render --wav` with and without `--scalar-content`, all 160 presets, tiers low / medium / high, on the Mac; `--verify --tolerance 3e-5` for 160 presets x 2 profiles on the Mac and for six content presets on each device): 150 of 160 presets give identical samples; the ten granular / spectral ones differ by -120 to -140 dB of their RMS (level difference 0.0000 dB, third octave bands at most 0.001 dB), except Spectral Gong Pad (Phase Lock, Add Subharmonics): -98.6 dB, level 0.0000 dB, bands 0.0008 dB. The cause is isolated (docs/engine-spec/07 section 7): the oscillator alone differs by -127 dB there; the Mono Bass crossover of the preset's Utility lifts round-off noise to -98.6 dB, constant over a 60 s held note. On XL arm64 the compiler no longer fuses multiply-add in these files, so XL renders differ from round 9 at the 1e-7 level.

**Waste removed from the content engines without changing a sample (round 11, `docs/release/CPU-REVIEW-CONTENT.md`).** Each item keeps its old code path selectable for its test and is proven bit for bit by it: SP1 (2a402953) a note start no longer clears the 150 KB of player buffers (`SpectralVoice::reset` clears flags only: `noteOn` clears or writes what it reads, proven by notes started on buffers filled with a pattern; `GranularVoice::reset` skips its 48 grain flags while no grain was spawned); SP2 (b2a0f581) the start schedule is a table made in `prepare` instead of up to 654 double cosines per note; SP3 (52fc6cdf) the real inverse transform, the overlap-add window, the start gains and the schedule table are built in `SpectralVoice::prepare` (`SpectralVoice::tablesBuiltForTest`), not at the first Spectral note; MK1 (c2d3accf) the bypassed Convolve's makeup caches the meter constants, the weights of the two program means (rebuilt only when the program's shares move), the impulse key (two `powf`), the level gain and the smoothing constant by their inputs (`makeup::setLegacyCachesForTest`); GR1 (f01a6a63) a scanning cloud makes the two divisions and the loop bounds of its head once per call; SA1 (e53de443) the sample kernel keeps the `log2` of the pitch ratio and the pan gains until their inputs change; LU1 (9946509c) `LibOsc::update` keeps the pan law, the detune curve, the spread pan and the pitch ratios until their inputs change; SP5 (2a36556d) Phase Lock advances only bin 0, the peaks and the kept half regions (gathered, advanced by the same kernel, put back) instead of every bin; SP6 (7fce2783) the priming frames are overlap-added straight into the ring (`overlapAdd` has a `first` sample); SP10 (25488aa2) the Spectral and Granular output stages (gain, mask of non finite values, clamp) and the library oscillator's lane mix (`simd::rampLerp` + `simd::mulAddTo` on the ARM builds) run in NEON lanes with the operations of the scalar code. The one caveat of every vector form of the engine applies to SP10: NEON on 32 bit ARM flushes subnormal numbers, so a sample or product below 1.2e-38 may be 0 instead of the subnormal value (the Mac tests run the aarch64 form, which does not flush). Not done: CS1 (the analysis only for Spectral oscillators): a live switch of an oscillator to Spectral would have no analysis for the 0.1 to 0.2 s that the background build takes on the Key 61, where it is instantaneous now; keeping the old type until the analysis is there means deferring the type switch in `voice.cpp`, which that round did not touch. Not done on purpose (rounding level or audible, owner decides): GR3, SA2, SP13, SP14, every D item. Tests: `synthyboy_test_spectral`, `synthyboy_test_granular`, `synthyboy_test_sampleosc`, `synthyboy_test_own_content_opt`, `synthyboy_test_own_convolve_bypass`, `synthyboy_test_own_mix_lanes` (arm64 Mac only).

The measurements of these kernels on the devices (median block times before and after, the presets still over the rule, the cost of a Spectral note strike) are listed under "Observed on the device" (entry "Content oscillators, round 10").

## Benchmark switches

The benchmark `synthyboy_bench` (`bench/bench_engine.cpp`) and `synthyboy_noteon_bench` run on the device as they are (built by `scripts/build-mpc.sh --bench`, or by `cmake` for the armv7 / aarch64 toolchain with `-DSYNTHYBOY_BUILD_TESTS=ON`). The switches used for the measurements above:

- `--preset FILE` and `--preset-dir DIR` play `.sbpreset` files through the same loading path as the shell (parameters by `setParam`, then the content); the directory mode prints one line per preset, the presets over the block budget in the worst block and the top 15 by average. `--csv FILE` writes name, average, 99th percentile and worst block of every preset.
- `--voices K` plays K notes (default: the voices of the tier); `--tier low|medium|high`, `--seconds N`, `--sr 44100`, `--retrigger S`.
- `--sections` prints the exclusive time of each part of a block, and the same for the slowest hundredth of the blocks with the number of voice ticks and render calls. It needs a build with `-DSYNTHYBOY_SECTION_TIMING=ON`; the clock of a scope costs about one microsecond on the device, so small sections are inflated.
- Legato and the voice control (`work/fix-legato-env`): a legato note (`Voice::legatoTo`) takes the envelope time scaling of its voice control step also when nothing of the voice is modulated (the all envelope settings cache is cleared there, in the old `--no-voice-savings` code too); no factory preset changes (renders identical, 960 files), `core/tests/test_own_voice_legato_env.cpp` is in CTest.
- `synthyboy_tier_bench` also: `--scalar-content` (the content oscillators run their reference code), `--no-voice-savings` (the voice control and the matrix without the round 13 caches and skips; `--verify` includes it), `--profile FILE`, `--micro`, `--tolerance X` (with `--verify`) and, with `--sections`, the sections of the longest block.
- `--unison-cap N` (0 = none), `--morph-spread 0|1`, `--kill-fade MS` set the tier low options of the engine described above. `--ftz` sets flush-to-zero like the plugin; `--idle` plays no notes.

## LFO pictures

The tabs `LFO 1` to `LFO 4` (sub-tabs of the function key `LFO`) have the same layout as the Env and Osc tabs: a large picture of the selected shape in the upper two rows, with a cursor that follows the cycle of the voice that was played last, and the eight main controls of the LFO in the lower two rows, which are Q-Link 1 to 8 (the rows on the screen are the rows of the hardware): Shape, Sync, Rate, Division / Trigger, Phase, Fade In, Delay. The Q-Link frames cover the lower two rows (`x 301 288 323`, see "Q-Link frames and alignment"). `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/30_lfo.py` builds the tab.

- **Two layers.** The host keeps film strips and pictures decoded (width x height x 4 bytes per frame), so one strip of 33 frames per shape at this size would not fit. (a) The shape: 13 single frame PNGs, 600 x 240 pixels (the 12 shapes of the engine's `lfoShape()` and CUSTOM), one `Image` each, valid only while `lfoN.shape` has that choice (`IndexedEnabling/<choice>/13/Parameter <shape>`, bounds version 2), centred in the tab at `340 30 600 240` (y 30 to 270, above the frames). (b) Over it ONE transparent film strip, `lfo_cursor.png`, 33 frames of 600 x 240 (7920 pixels tall): frame 0 is empty (no voice sounds), frames 1 to 32 have only a vertical line, 3 pixels wide, in one cell of the cycle; the cell centres are at x = 24 + (k - 0.5) / 32 x 552, the curve of layer (a) uses the same x. The same file serves the four LFOs and every shape; it is bound to the display parameter "LFO N Cursor" and is shown for every shape (also for CUSTOM).
- **Source and size.** The curves are the engine's: `scripts/gen-mpc-lfo-pictures.py` compiles `scripts/lfo-shape-dump.cpp` against `core/engine-own/env_lfo/lfo.h` and plots its samples (red line, faint fill, grid, the shape name at the top left, like the oscillator pictures); the result is committed in `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/lfo-pictures/` (`lfo_shape_00.png` .. `lfo_shape_12.png`, `lfo_cursor.png`; run the script after a change of the LFO code, a test renders them again and compares; needs numpy, Pillow and a C++ compiler, the build only copies the files). Decoded: (13 + 33) x 600 x 240 x 4 = 26 496 000 bytes, under the budget of 32 MB (a test); the files are 0.2 MB on disk. All PNG files of the skin together are 105 709 408 bytes decoded (the wavetable pictures 66 355 200, these pictures 26 496 000, the kit 12 825 568, the spectrum columns 32 640; before the large LFO pictures the skin had 96 267 744 with 12 551 552 for the 208 x 38 strips); a test keeps that number. The 208 x 38 strips of the earlier version (12.5 MB decoded) and the tabs `LFO 1-2` and `LFO 3-4` are gone.
- **Transparency.** The cursor strip is an RGBA PNG whose background is fully transparent; the host has to draw the picture below it through it. Evidence: the knob, fader, toggle and button images of the SDK kit are RGBA PNGs with fully transparent corners (`knob_uni.png`: pixel 0,0 is 0,0,0,0) and every skin built from the kit uses them over the dark page background, including the skins loaded on the Key 61 (no screenshot of that was examined for this document), so the host is expected to blend the alpha channel of an `Image` / film strip. That the host blends it over another `Image` (not over the page background) was not measured separately; it is the same drawing call, and the cursor frames carry the light line colour also on their partly transparent edge pixels, so that a host that treated them as opaque would show a bright line and no dark box.
- **Sync.** The rate in Hz matters only when `lfoN.sync` is "Free (Hz)", the division only for the three tempo modes, "Key follow" uses neither (`lfoRateHz` in `core/engine-own/env_lfo/lfo.h`: 0 free, 1 tempo, 2 dotted, 3 triplet, 4 key follow). A Q-Link is one parameter, so Rate and Division cannot share one place in the Q-Link map: they keep their two places and the one that does not apply is hidden. Rate is valid for sync 0 (`IndexedEnabling/0/5/Parameter <sync>`); the Division exists three times at its place, each copy valid for one of sync 1, 2 and 3 (the same `IndexedEnabling`, which is the proven mechanism; the copies are named `LFO N Division`, `LFO N Division 2`, `LFO N Division 3`). With "Key follow" both places are empty. The knob name of Rate has the same visibility (85_names.py); a hidden control cannot take focus but its Q-Link still moves its parameter.
- **Controls and Q-Links.** The eight controls are those of the Kick page `lfoN`; the selectors Shape, Sync, Division and Trigger have the host header, Rate, Phase, Fade In and Delay are knobs. `Q-Links.json` of the tab: row 3 (Q-Link 5 to 8) Shape, Sync, Rate, Division; row 4 (Q-Link 1 to 4) Trigger, Phase, Fade In, Delay.

The frame follows a display-only plugin parameter, "LFO N Cursor", at the end of the parameter list (index: number of engine parameters plus N minus 1), holding the step divided by 32. The values are not saved in the state or a preset, the parameters are not automatable and no index before them moves. Because the host redraws only on `audioMasterAutomate`, a thread of the shell (`shell-mpc/synthyboy_mpc.cpp`, `shell-mpc/lfo_live.h`) reads the engine's live values (`LiveValues::lfoPhase`, the voice played last) and calls `audioMasterAutomate` only for a cursor that changed cell.

Thread model of the display (the reason is the host: `audioMasterAutomate` runs synchronously on the host's interface thread, and a continuously moving display was reported by the community wrapper "Open MPC" (`wrapper/vst2_wrap.c`, a comment there; measured by its authors on an MPC Live III) to hold up the host's audio threads for about 20 milliseconds every few seconds; that wrapper therefore reports from a separate thread of normal priority, every 100 milliseconds, changed values only):

- One display thread per plugin instance, created in `VSTPluginMain` (the host's loader thread, not the audio thread; whatever that thread is, the display thread sets its own scheduling first, see below). Tick rate `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_HZ`, default 10: a tick every 100 milliseconds. A tick reads the cursor positions at that moment (the cursor is always where the cycle is now, not a queue of old positions), and calls `audioMasterAutomate` for each of the four parameters whose cell changed, in index order, at most once per parameter and tick. With a fast LFO the 32 cells are therefore stepped over (the cursor jumps), which is accepted. A tick that comes late (the host call blocked) is not made up for: one tick, then the grid goes on from that moment. With no voice sounding the thread looks every 100 milliseconds and does not call the host.
- The thread is a normal thread: `shell-mpc/thread_policy.h` sets, from inside the thread, policy `SCHED_OTHER` with priority 0, nice 0 and the affinity of all CPUs. Nothing is inherited from the thread that created the plugin: a real-time policy or a single-core affinity of the creator would otherwise carry over to a `std::thread`. Nice 0 (not lower) because the thread holds host locks while it is inside the host call, and a starved holder makes the host's own threads wait. Lowering a nice value needs the privilege `CAP_SYS_NICE`; a host process without it keeps a nice value inherited from a creator with nice above 0 (policy and affinity are always reset). The thread is named `sb-lfo-display` (`top -H`, `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/comm`). The host test (`shell-mpc/tests/lfo_live_host.cpp`, Linux) creates the plugin from a thread that is pinned to one CPU and, where the process may, nice 5 and `SCHED_FIFO`, and checks policy, nice and affinity of the display thread; it ran on aarch64 Linux in a container, with and without `CAP_SYS_NICE`.
- The audio thread (`pluginProcessReplacing`) does nothing for the display: no call to the host for it (its one host call is the transport query `audioMasterGetTime`, as before the display existed), no allocation, no lock. The only connection is that the engine writes its readout (`OwnEngine::writeReadout`, relaxed atomic stores) at the end of a block, and the display thread reads those atomics (`copyReadout`) and the parameter mirror. The cursor values the host reads (`getParameter`) are atomics too.

Callback statistics (diagnostics, off by default). To find out on the device whether the display holds up the audio callback, the shell can measure its own callback: build with `-DSYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS=ON` (compile switch `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS`, default 0; with 0 the source preprocesses to exactly the text without the statistics code). In `processReplacing` it measures with the monotonic clock the time between two calls (the gap) against the nominal block time of the previous block (frames divided by the sample rate), and the time spent inside the call. On the audio thread this is arithmetic and relaxed atomic stores into storage that exists from the start: no allocation, no lock, no file access (`shell-mpc/callback_stats.h`). A low priority thread (`sb-cb-stats`, nice 10) writes a text file every 5 seconds and when the plugin is closed, through a temporary file and rename. Environment variables of the host process: `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS_FILE` (default `/tmp/synthyboy-callback-stats.txt`; a second plugin instance writes `<file>.2`, and so on) and `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS_INTERVAL_MS` (default 5000). The gap after mains off and on is not counted. Since format 2 the file also tells what reaches the plugin and what the host asks (below the sample). The file:

```
synthyboy-callback-stats 2
build: LFO_LIVE=ON LFO_LIVE_HZ=10 LFO_LIVE_NOTIFY=ON LFO_LIVE_SELFTEST=OFF params=2641 arch=arm64
written: shutdown at_ms=722
block_frames_last: 480
nominal_block_us: 10000
callbacks: 11
gaps_counted: 10
gap_max_us: 104863
gap_buckets (gap / nominal block time): <1.5x=2 <2x=3 <4x=3 <8x=1 >=8x=1
process_max_us: 7521
process_avg_us: 699.397
largest_gaps: 10
  1 at_ms=367 gap_us=104863 ratio=10.49x block_frames=480
  2 at_ms=262 gap_us=67531 ratio=6.75x block_frames=480
midi:
  window_start_ms=0 resets=0
  window_ms=722
  vst_events: midi=3 sysex=1 other_types=0
  status_totals: note_off=0 note_on=1 poly_at=0 cc=1 program=0 chan_pressure=0 pitch_bend=1 system_or_other=0 note_on_velocity0=0
  channel 1: note_off=0 note_on=1 poly_at=0 cc=1 program=0 chan_pressure=0 pitch_bend=1
  cc_numbers:
    cc=74 count=1
  pitch_bend (14 bit, centre 8192): min=8192 max=8192
  channel_pressure: none
  poly_at (value): none
  cc74: min=10 max=10
  last_events (of 3, oldest first; at_ms since the plugin was created, delta in frames within the block):
    1 at_ms=301 delta=5 status=0x90 ch=1 d1=60 d2=100
host_can_do:
  questions=2 distinct=1 not_stored=0 (texts longer than 63 bytes are cut)
  "receiveVstMidiEvent" asked=2 answer=1
dispatcher_opcodes:
  total=9 out_of_range=0
  op=25 name=effProcessEvents count=2
  op=50 name=effVendorSpecific count=1
  vendor_specific_first_calls (effVendorSpecific): seen=1 shown=1
    1 opcode=50 index=1 value=1000 opt=0.5 ptr=null
  unknown_opcode_first_calls: seen=0 shown=0
audio_master_calls:
  total=12 out_of_range=0
  op=1 name=audioMasterVersion count=1
  op=7 name=audioMasterGetTime count=11
```

(The sample is from the host test, `shell-mpc/tests/callback_stats_host.cpp`, with calls spaced on purpose; "at_ms" is milliseconds since the plugin was created, the ten largest gaps are listed, largest first.) The sections after the callback lines (added in format 2; the sample above is shortened and its numbers are illustrative) answer two questions on the machine itself. `midi:` counts every message that arrives in `effProcessEvents` by status type (note off, note on, polyphonic aftertouch, control change, program change, channel pressure, pitch bend; everything from 0xF0 on, and a first byte below 0x80, as `system_or_other`) and per channel (channels with no message are not listed), counts each control change number (non zero rows only), keeps the smallest and largest pitch bend (14 bit), channel pressure, polyphonic aftertouch value and CC 74 value seen, and lists the last 64 messages (time since the plugin was created, position in the block, status and data bytes). `vst_events` also counts the events that are not MIDI messages: sysex events (type 6) and events of any other type. `host_can_do:` lists every different `effCanDo` text the host asked (at most 32 texts of 63 bytes, how often, and the answer the plugin gave last); `dispatcher_opcodes:` counts every call of the dispatcher by opcode with the name from the ABI header (opcodes the header does not know are named `unknown_to_the_ABI_header`) and shows the index, value, opt and whether `ptr` was set for the first 8 calls of `effVendorSpecific` and of the first 8 calls with an unknown opcode; `audio_master_calls:` counts the plugin's own calls to the host by opcode. All of it is fixed size atomic storage: the audio thread only adds and stores. To mark the start of a measurement, create the file `/tmp/synthyboy-stats-reset` (environment variable `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS_RESET_FILE` changes the path): before its next write the writer thread zeros the MIDI counters, the ranges and the ring (not the callback lines or the host questions), deletes the file and counts `resets` up; a message that arrives in the very moment of the zeroing may be lost or counted once more. With the switch off none of this is compiled. A healthy callback has nearly all gaps in the first bucket and a largest gap of about one block time. The variants for the comparison on the device are built with `scripts/build-mpc.sh --cmake-arg ...` (see its usage): `LFO_LIVE=OFF` (no display parameters), the default (10 a second), `LFO_LIVE_HZ=25`, and the self-test builds (`LFO_LIVE_SELFTEST=ON`, cursors move without a note) at 10, 25 and 50 a second. A variant without `LFO_LIVE` has 2637 parameters, one with it 2641 (the four cursors are indices 2637 to 2640): a skin that refers to those indices needs a build with `LFO_LIVE=ON`.

Known limits of the cursor, from the engine's readout: swing and direction are not drawn; in free-run mode and for mono envelope LFOs the engine does not advance the voice's own position, so the cursor stays at the start; an envelope-type LFO that has finished jumps back to the start; with several voices only the voice played last is shown.

The skin refers to the display parameters, so the MPC plugin must be built with them. Build options of the MPC shell (CMake): `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE` (default ON; OFF builds the plugin without the parameters, only for comparisons, because then the host hides the pictures), `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_HZ` (default 10, ticks a second), `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_NOTIFY` (default ON; OFF updates the values without telling the host, a baseline), `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_SELFTEST` (default OFF; the cursors sweep without a note, for measuring on the host), `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS` (default OFF; the statistics above). The compile switch of the same names in the source defaults to 0, which makes the shell identical to one without the display. The acceptance script `shell-mpc/accept.sh` builds the variants (also HZ 25 and the statistics) and runs the ON build, with the statistics, under ThreadSanitizer.

A `Plotter` bound to a parameter does not draw on this host (see the device observations), so the display is a film strip and not a plotted line.

## Spectrum

The bottom row of the `Macros` tab (the fourth row of the grid is empty there) shows a live spectrum of the plugin's own output: 32 vertical columns, from about 30 Hz to 18 kHz on a logarithmic scale, 30 pixels wide with 8 pixels between them (32 x 30 + 31 x 8 = 1208 pixels, centred, x 36 to 1244), y 480 to 616. `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/40_spectrum.py` builds them; the Q-Link frames of the tab end at y 474 (rows 1 to 3), so the columns, which cross the four frames, stand below them.

- **Widget.** One native `Meter` per column, `direction "Up"`, with the keys and the key order of the Filterbank's `envfollower_meter1` element (`version 3`, `invert false`, `inactiveImage`, `rmsImage ""`, `rmsHandle "Invalid"`, `peakImage`, `peakHandle`, `peakHoldHandle`, `peakHoldSize 0`, `peakHoldColour`) and the remap `Data -> Parameter N`; `peakHandle` is `Parameter N`, `peakHoldHandle` is `Hold/Parameter N`. A film strip per column was the first plan and is not used: a Meter bound to a plugin parameter works on the Key 61 (see the device observations), it needs two small pictures shared by all 32 columns and not a strip per column, and the level is continuous. The `Plotter` does not draw a parameter, and the `Info/Named` channel that feeds the stock plugins' displays has no known way for an external plugin. `"Right"` was the direction tried first; `"Up"`, the one the stock output meters use, was tried on the Key 61 afterwards and works (see the device observations).
- **Pictures.** `scripts/gen-mpc-spectrum-images.py` (standard library only, deterministic) writes `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/spectrum-images/spectrum_off.png` (dark, 42 41 44) and `spectrum_on.png` (the accent colour, a vertical gradient from 244 63 110 at the top to 211 17 69 at the bottom), both 30 x 136 pixels; a test regenerates them and compares the bytes. Decoded they are 2 x 30 x 136 x 4 = 32 640 bytes, so they add nothing to the 105 709 408 bytes of the skin ("LFO pictures").
- **Parameters.** 32 display-only parameters "Spectrum 1" to "Spectrum 32" at the end of the list, after the four LFO cursors: indices 2641 to 2672 (2637 engine parameters, 2637 to 2640 the cursors). Value 0 to 1 in steps of 1/64 (display text "n/64"). They are not in the mirror, the state chunk or a preset, `effCanBeAutomated` is 0, writes are ignored, no index before them moves (a test checks the names, the indices and that the chunk is the same with and without them).
- **Thread model.** The audio thread (`pluginProcessReplacing`) copies the mono sum (left + right) / 2 of the engine's output, after the engine has rendered the block, into a ring of 8192 samples (`SpectrumRing` in `shell-mpc/spectrum.h`: relaxed atomic floats, the write position released after the samples): no allocation, no lock, no call to the host. The display thread of the LFO cursors (`sb-lfo-display`, normal priority, 10 ticks a second by default) does the rest on every tick: the newest 2048 samples (about 46 milliseconds at 44.1 kHz), Hann window, FFT (`shell-mpc/spectrum_fft.h`, own radix-2, no dependency), 32 bands with equal ratio from 30 Hz to 18 kHz (`shell-mpc/spectrum_bands.h`; a band takes the largest bin inside it, a band narrower than a bin interpolates between the two neighbouring bins, so the lowest columns share bins: at 44.1 kHz a bin is 21.5 Hz wide), to dB (a full scale sine on a bin gives 0 dB), to a level (linear in dB from floor `kSpectrumFloorDb` = -60 dB -> 0 to ceiling `kSpectrumCeilingDb` = -12 dB -> 1, clamped to 0..1: levels above -12 dB show a full column because on the device a single loud note peaked at about half height with the earlier 0 dB ceiling, as a single band never reaches 0 dB), smoothing (the distance to a higher target is covered by 0.7 per tick, to a lower one by 0.15, about 3 seconds from the top to nothing at 10 ticks a second), quantised to 1/64 so that a small flutter makes no report. Every column whose quantised value changed is reported with `audioMasterAutomate` in index order: at most 32 calls a tick besides the at most 4 of the cursors, none while all columns stand on the floor. A ring that did not move since the last tick (the host stopped calling) counts as silence.
- **Build options (CMake).** `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_SPECTRUM` (default ON in `shell-mpc`; the source default is 0, so a build that does not define it has no spectrum code, no parameter and no string of it; it needs `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE`, the thread is shared) and `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_SPECTRUM_SELFTEST` (default OFF; a wave runs over the 32 columns without a sound, for measuring the host side; at `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_HZ=25` it is the worst case, 32 reports every 40 milliseconds). The skin of `40_spectrum.py` refers to the parameters, so the MPC package must be built with the spectrum ON.
- **Tests.** `shell-mpc/tests/spectrum_bands_host.cpp` (the band mapping on a 1 kHz sine, other sines, silence, white noise, the quantiser), `shell-mpc/tests/spectrum_host.cpp` (ring and analyser: silence, a sine, the smoothing, a stale ring, two threads), `shell-mpc/tests/spectrum_live_host.cpp` (the plugin: parameter count and names, silence gives no report, a held note moves the columns to where an offline analysis of the same output puts them, at most 32 calls a tick, the reports stop after the note, the self-test wave), all three also under ThreadSanitizer through `shell-mpc/accept.sh`; `scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_spectrum.py` (the elements, the handles, the geometry) and `scripts/tests/test_mpc_spectrum_images.py`.
- **The curve over the meters (overlay build).** In a build with `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OVERLAY` and the overlay skin, the Macros tab shows the same signal a second way: one smooth, filled, anti-aliased spectrum CURVE (Serum / EQ analyser style) exactly over the row of the 32 meters, the view `spectrum` of "Overlay (experimental)". The meters stay in the skin underneath and are what is seen when the overlay is off, disabled, or has no data (an instance without the spectrum ring); the production skin and the production plugin do not change. Where the picture comes from: nothing new on the audio thread, the curve reads the same ring `SpectrumRing` as the meters; the analysis is `shell-mpc/overlay/spectrum_curve.h` on the overlay thread, and it runs only while that tab is on screen, once per picture (about 30 a second, and not at all in silence).
  - FFT of 4096 samples with a Hann window, hopping by one picture (about 33 milliseconds, so a new analysis overlaps the last by about 65 percent). At 44.1 kHz the window is 93 milliseconds and a bin is 10.8 Hz: the partials of a note from about 40 Hz up are separate peaks (the 2048 samples of the meters would show the lowest octave as one lump), and a longer window would smear the attack of a note. The mean of the window is taken out first (a DC offset of the output is not sound and would show as a hump at the low end).
  - Level: `S[k]` is the power in the three bins of the Hann main lobe divided by 1.5 (the noise bandwidth of the window), so a sine gives its own amplitude whatever its distance from a bin centre (a plain bin read-out loses up to 1.4 dB between two bins), and the dB scale is the one of the meters (`kSpectrumFloorDb` at the bottom to `kSpectrumCeilingDb` at the top, the same constants, a full scale sine is over the top).
  - 256 points, log axis from 20 Hz to 20 kHz. A point narrower than a bin (the low end) is interpolated between the two bins. EVERY point takes the peak of `S` over its stretch, whatever its width (an earlier version took the mean of `S` over the wide points above about 3 kHz and crossfaded: it lowered every tone there and the curve drew visibly under the meters, which show the maximum of the bins of their band); a tone keeps its level wherever it lies between two points.
  - The height of the meters. The meter of a band shows the maximum of the bins of that band in a 2048 point analysis of the newest samples (`spectrumBandDb`); the curve computes exactly that value for each of the 32 bands (the same functions, without the meters' smoothing in time; the mean of that window is taken out as for the curve) and is tied to it: in each band the highest point of the curve is exactly the meter's value, and no point inside a band is above it. The gain needed at the highest point of a band (the two windows differ, a tone between two bins reads up to 1.4 dB low on a plain bin, and the skirts of a loud tone reach farther in the 2048 window than in the 4096 one) is applied to the whole band, so the shape inside a band, the order of its peaks and the valleys between partials are kept, and is blended linearly with the neighbouring band's gain over a stretch of one and a half points on either side of the band edge (no step in the picture). Points under 30 Hz or over 18 kHz take the gain of the first or the last band. Only the level is tied to the meters; the resolution of the 4096 point analysis is not given up.
  - Light smoothing across frequency: a point is raised towards the mean of itself and its two neighbours, never lowered (valleys between partials fill a little, every peak keeps its height). In time, per point: attack with a time constant of 20 milliseconds, release with 350 milliseconds, by the real time between two analyses; a point under 1/256 of the scale with a target at the floor goes to exactly 0.
  - Silence: a window whose largest sample is under -80 dB below full scale (far under the floor of the scale), or a ring that did not move (the host stopped calling), is not analysed (no FFT); the curve falls to the floor and, when it has settled there, the view reports "unchanged": no picture is drawn, the helper sleeps, the thread polls. The 32 display parameters and their reports are not touched.
  - Drawing: an opaque dark background over the whole row (the meters do not show through), faint grid lines at 100 Hz, 1 kHz and 10 kHz, a filled area under the curve with a vertical gradient (accent colour, strong under the line, almost transparent at the bottom) and a line 2.5 pixels thick in the accent colour, 30 pictures a second while the curve moves. The same picture from the GPU (a scene with the gradient fill, protocol version 2) and from the CPU (`draw.h`, `fillToBaselineGradient`); the geometry of the row is `meterRow` in `shell-mpc/overlay/overlay_layout.json`, the one place that both `40_spectrum.py` and the overlay read.
  - Tests: `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/spectrum_curve_host.cpp` (sines at several frequencies and levels, the worst offset of a tone between two points, the curve against the 32 meters of the same signal: twelve frequencies at four levels and a chord of seven partials, in every band the highest point of the curve is the meter's value within one row of the view and never above it, two sines, a DC offset, white noise, silence, a ring that stands still, the attack and release times, other sample rates, the audio thread pushing while the overlay thread analyses, also under ThreadSanitizer through `shell-mpc/accept.sh`), `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/spectrum_view_host.cpp` (the view and the controller: no data without a ring, the scene is the CPU picture, no picture at rest). The measured numbers are in "Known host behaviour".
- **Limits.** The spectrum is the sum of both output channels of the plugin, not the stereo image; the plugin's own effects are in it. A pure tone shows in the column of its band and a little in the neighbours (the Hann window). The audio callback gaps with the 32 columns are in "Known host behaviour" (Key 61 with the self-test wave and with a real chord, MPC XL with the self-test wave); the CPU cost of the analysis on its own (about one FFT of 2048 points per tick) has not been measured, nor has how the peak hold looks or the MPC XL with real sound in the test project.

## Q-Link frames and alignment

Every tab has four equal Q-Link column frames (`qlinkBoundsData`): x = 16 + 320 c, width 288 (c = 0 to 3), so 16 pixels from each screen edge and 32 between the frames. The x and the width are set in one place, `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/90_frames.py` (it runs last). The frame of a plain tab is `x 0 288 624`. On the tabs with a picture in the upper half (Env 1 to 4, Osc 1 to 3, LFO 1 to 4) the frame covers the lower two rows: `x 301 288 323`; the orange "Q" badge of the host (about 38 x 38 pixels, top left corner of the frame) then sits above the lower controls, and the Env curve (`23 6 1238 290`) and the Osc pictures (`384 30 512 240`) end above the frames. The `Macros` tab has its picture at the bottom (the spectrum, see "Spectrum"): its frames are `x 0 288 474` and end above it. Nothing may stick out of a frame horizontally; only the Env curve, the Osc pictures and the spectrum columns cross the columns.

`scripts/mpc-skin-extras/80_layout.py` puts every control on the vertical axis of its frame (x = 160 + 320 c): slots of 278 pixels at x = 21 + 320 c.

- Knob (`knobUni`, `knobBi`) and toggle: slot 278 x 138; the name above (see "Control names"), the 90 x 90 knob (or the 84 x 68 switch) in the middle (y 22, y 33), the value or state text centred below (3 112 272 26, Titillium Web 24, white).
- Fader: slot 278 x 100; name above, the strip in the middle (15 32 248 36), value below (3 70 272 26).
- Selectors (`sliderWithFocus`, `sliderWithFocusMenu`) and radio groups (`radio3`, `radio4`): slot 278 x 100; the content of the selectors and of `radio3` is symmetric already, the four buttons of `radio4` move 2 pixels to the right (their x 15 to 259 was 2 pixels left of the axis).
- Rows: plain tabs y 44, 188, 332, 476 (pitch 144, the 138 pixel rows end at 614); the tabs with a picture: y 343 and 483 (end at 621, below the badge zone that ends at 341). The 100 pixel slots sit in the middle of a row (19 pixels offset).
- Names: one centred line over the inner width of the frame (272 pixels), see "Control names".
- Touch and focus behaviour (Q-Link selection on touch, double tap to the knob or menu overlay, hardware focus) is the generator's: only geometry and text justification changed, a test compares the actions and handles with the generator's output. The Q-Link map of a tab that no module rewrites is the generator's (a test compares by tab name; the sub-tab module renumbers `Tab` and `SubTab`).

## Control names

The tab says which module a control belongs to, so the name over a knob, toggle, fader or radio group is short: "Detune Curve" instead of "Osc 1 Unison Detune Curve". The host draws the name of such a control from the parameter name (a `Label` of type `Name` in the shared component definition); `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/85_names.py` removes that child from the definitions and puts a fixed text (`Label`, type `Text`) over every control, at the place where the host drew the name. The same module gives the label the `additionalInvalidatingHandles` of its control, so a control that is valid for one choice of another parameter only shows its name only then.

- **Where the names come from** (`scripts/gen-mpc-ui.py`, which writes them with the page structure to `skin-meta.json` next to `ui.json`; the build passes that file to `scripts/apply-mpc-skin-extras.py --meta`): the `shortLabel` of the Kick page (`docs/kick-pages.json`), unless `scripts/mpc-short-names.json` has an entry for the parameter key. The table holds the names where the `shortLabel` is cut off at 10 characters ("Unison Ste", "Key Tracki", "Voice Cont") or is ambiguous, and the few that were too wide. If two controls of one tab still get the same name, the generator puts in front of the name the words in front of the longest common word suffix of the full parameter names: "Osc 1 Filt Blend", "Osc 2 Filt Blend", "Filter 1 Dest", "Mod 01 Source". The rule is deterministic; a clash it cannot resolve (no common suffix) stops the generator and asks for table entries. Tests: names are unique inside a tab, none is cut off in the middle of a word, no name is wider than the area.
- **Style**: Titillium Web SemiBold 22 pixels, upper case (`case` `Upper Case`), colour `ff6a6a6c`, the weight of the headers the host draws over the selectors ("OSC 1 MORPH TYPE"); the value below keeps white and 24 pixels. The colour is the SDK's own "muted" grey (`tools/gen_ui.py` `DEFAULT_STYLE`, used by the generated name labels before); the font and size of the header drawn by the host are not in any file (the Slider draws them inside the host), so bold and about 22 pixels follow the description of the header seen on the device. The label is 272 x 26 pixels at the top of the slot (the knob and toggle slots at y 0, the faders at y 4, the radio groups at y 6).
- **Width**: the width of a name is estimated from the advance widths of Titillium Web Bold (`ADVANCE` in `scripts/gen-mpc-ui.py`: the upper case letters, digits and the punctuation of the names, measured with Pillow on `TitilliumWeb-Bold.ttf`, a font file that is not in this repository; the host draws the skin font itself, so kerning and rounding may differ a little). The estimate uses the Bold widths, which are an upper bound of SemiBold. The widest name is "Formant Resonance" with an estimate of 223 pixels at 22 pixels (area 272); a test keeps every name under 95 percent of the area (258 pixels).
- **Measured on the Key 61 (MPC 3.10.1.12): a fixed text label (`Label`, type `Text`) placed on a tab is drawn only if its `Data` handle is bound to a valid parameter**, although ui-reference 6.3 says the handle is ignored for `Text`. With an empty remap nothing is drawn (`showWhenDataModelInvalid` Show and another font style did not help; binding to `Parameter 0` did). So every fixed text of the skin is bound to the parameter of its own control: the names of `85_names.py` and the captions of the radio buttons (the generator's `... seg N` labels, which `85_names.py` binds too). A test fails on a `Text` label with an empty or out of range remap. The style is SemiBold: it was seen to draw on the device, Bold was not tried there.
- **Selectors keep the host header.** The SDK `Slider` (6.1) draws the name of the parameter in upper case over the bar (`GenericLegacy`) and has no key for another text, no way to hide the name and no way to draw the header smaller. A `Label` has no background, so a text over it would stand on top of the host name. The selectors (`sliderWithFocus`, `sliderWithFocusMenu`, the parameters with five or more choices) therefore show the full parameter name as before; this is a limit of the host, not a choice. The full name also stays where the host shows it by itself (the header of the knob and menu overlays).

## Faders and knobs

A continuous (not stepped) control is a knob, except on a Kick page (`docs/kick-pages.json`) whose continuous controls are all levels or mixes (parameter name with level, mix, volume, dry, wet or send): there they are horizontal faders (`faderUni`). The rule is `_is_level_page` in `scripts/gen-mpc-ui.py` and is applied to the Kick page a parameter is taken from (a parameter is placed once). Stepped parameters are unchanged: 2 to 4 choices a toggle or radio group, 5 or more a selector. Before, every parameter with such a word in its name was a fader, so a level among knobs (`Osc 1 Level`) or `Master Volume` on the Macros tab stood alone as a fader.

In the current layout two Kick pages are level pages, so the faders are `Bus 1 Level` and `Bus 2 Level` on the tab `Mixer` (page `mix_bus`, the other controls of the page are switches) and the eight `Chorus Level`, `Compressor Level`, `Delay Level`, `Distortion Level`, `EQ Level`, `Flanger Level`, `Phaser Level` and `Reverb Level` on the tab `FX Level`. Every other level (Osc 1 to 3 Level, Noise Level, Sub Level, Direct Level, Master Volume, the mixes of the effects) is a knob. The Kick pages `mix_sends_osc`, `mix_sends_filter` and `fx_splitter_ms` would be level pages too, they are not on the skin. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_gen_mpc_ui_layout.py` (`test_widgets_follow_the_rules`, `test_faders_only_on_level_pages`, the unit tests of the rule).

## Function keys and sub-tabs

The skin groups its tabs under function keys. Several tabs with the same `fnKeyIndex` and the sub-tab indexes `fnKeySubIndex` 0, 1, 2 ... are sub-tabs of one key; the host steps to the next sub-tab when the key is pressed again, the key shows the `tabName` of the current sub-tab, and `Q-Links.json` has an entry with `Tab` = `fnKeyIndex` + 1 and `SubTab` = `fnKeySubIndex` + 1 for every one of them (observed on the Key 61: sub-tabs work; the SDK allows at most 8 sub-tabs per key and says "at most 32 tabs in total", see below).

- **Layout file**: `scripts/mpc-ui-pages.json` lists the pages (= tabs) in order; `fn` on a page names its function key, the pages of one key follow each other, a page without `fn` is a key of its own. `scripts/gen-mpc-ui.py` checks this (at most 8 per key) and writes the grouping to `skin-meta.json`; the SDK generator still makes one key per page, and `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/95_subtabs.py` (runs last) puts the tabs under their keys, orders the tab list and renumbers `Q-Links.json`. The page of a layout takes one to four Kick pages (`docs/kick-pages.json`) that must fit the 16 slots: every Kick page takes whole rows of four slots (its controls padded to a multiple of 4), so the controls of one Kick page stay together on the Q-Link rows.
- **The committed layout** (`scripts/mpc-ui-pages.json`, `"tabLimit": 34`) has 34 tabs in 10 function keys. Function keys and their sub-tabs, in order: `Macros` (Macros); `Osc` (Osc 1, Osc1 Shp, Osc 2, Osc2 Shp, Osc 3, Osc3 Shp); `Mix` (Sub/Nois, Mixer); `Filter` (Filter 1, Filter 2); `Env` (Env 1 to 4); `LFO` (LFO 1 to 4); `Mod` (Mod 1-2, Mod 3-4, Mod Amt); `FX` (FX Chain, FX Filt, Distort, Comp 1, Comp 2, EQ); `FX 2` (Chorus, Delay, Phaser, FX Level); `Master` (Master, Voice).
- **Measured on the Key 61 (MPC 3.10.1.12): the host loads a skin with 34 tabs in 10 function keys** (also the 32 tab one), so the sub-tabs do not count in the "at most 32 tabs in total" of the SDK (ui-reference 3.5); the sub-tab dots show under the function keys (6 under Osc, 4 under Env, ...). The SDK validator (`tools/validate.py`) still reports `ERROR  TUI.json: 34 tabs; at most 32 are supported`. The build (`scripts/build-mpc.sh`) accepts exactly that one finding, like the `file64` one of a single architecture build (`scripts/tests/test_build_mpc.py`), and fails on every other validator error.
- **Function key labels (overlay variant).** The host draws the `tabName` of the CURRENT sub-tab on the key (observed on the Key 61: the key reads the name of the sub-tab on screen), and the names must be unique. A page of the layout file may have `"label"`: the text the key shows while that page is the sub-tab on screen. The page keeps its unique INTERNAL name (the extras modules, the overlay views `overlay_layout.json`, `skin-meta.json` and the tests refer to it); `gen-mpc-ui.py` writes `labels` ({page name: label}) to `skin-meta.json` (only a layout that has labels: the production `skin-meta.json` is unchanged), and `95_subtabs.py`, the last module, sets the label as the `tabName` of the finished skin and refuses duplicates. The generator checks that a label is 1 to 8 characters and that no two tabs of the finished skin get the same name. The overlay layout labels the first sub-tab of every key: `Play`, `Macros`, `Osc` (Osc A), `Mix` (Mixer), `Filter` (Filter 1), `Env` (Env 1), `LFO` (LFO 1), `Mod` (Mod 1-2), `FX` (FX Chain), `FX 2` (Chorus), `Master`; the other sub-tabs keep their names (Osc B, Sub, F1 Model, Env 2 ...), which the key shows while they are on screen. Limits: the key reads the short label only while the FIRST sub-tab is on screen (after stepping on it shows the sub-tab's own name, and the first sub-tab of a key has no title of its own); a label must not equal the name of another page, so the short label is the name of the first page that carries it (`Osc 1` is called `Osc` in the finished overlay skin, `Osc 2` stays). The production skin has no labels and is byte for byte as before.
- **The 32 tab fallback** (`scripts/mpc-ui-pages-v32.json`, build it with `--skin-layout`) merges two pairs of tabs: `FX Filt` + `Distort` into `Flt+Dist` (14 controls, both tone shaping stages) and `Comp 2` + `FX Level` into `Cmp2+Lvl` (16 controls: the mid and high band of the compressor and the eight effect levels), placed behind `Comp 1`; `FX` is then FX Chain, Flt+Dist, Comp 1, Cmp2+Lvl, EQ and `FX 2` is Chorus, Delay, Phaser. Every control of every Kick page stays in both layouts (a test compares with the parameters of the skin at `7a0d198` and checks that the layouts hold the same parameters). The validator has no finding for it.
- **SDK tool limit**: `tools/gen_ui.py` refuses more than 32 pages. `scripts/run-gen-ui.py` runs it and lifts that limit only when the ui.json has more pages (34 tab variant); the build uses it for all skins. `scripts/build-mpc.sh --skin-layout FILE` picks the layout file (default `scripts/mpc-ui-pages.json`).

## MPC XL pads

The measurement was done on an MPC XL running MPC OS 3.10.1.12, using a diagnostic build of the plugin with callback statistics and MIDI counters (format 2). The owner pressed the pads by hand because pad input cannot be simulated remotely. The plugin was on a plugin track in the `Sbtest` project.

| | Full Level on | Full Level off |
|---|---|---|
| note on / note off | 19 / 19, channel 1 | 16 / 16, channel 1 |
| note-on velocity | always 127 | varied (96 and 127 seen) |
| poly aftertouch | 0 | 112 messages, values 1..127, one stream per held pad (per note), about one message every 6 milliseconds |
| channel pressure | 0 | 0 |
| pitch bend | 0 | 0 |
| CC (including CC 74) | 0 | 0 |
| channel | 1 | 1 |

What this means: the host passes note, velocity and per-pad pressure (poly aftertouch) to an external plugin; the X/Y position of a pad does not arrive in any form (no pitch bend, no CC 74, no other CC) and there is no MPE channel rotation; with Full Level on neither velocity nor pressure passes.

The host asked `effCanDo` only for `"bypass"` (answer 0) and `"receiveVstMidiEvent"` (answer 1), never for `"MPE"`, and made no `effVendorSpecific` call.

Poly aftertouch drives the modulation source "Poly Aftertouch"; the factory preset "MPE Pressure Lead" routes it to Filter 1 Cutoff and Osc 1 Unison Detune, and on the device pressing harder audibly opened the filter (heard by the owner, not measured).

Not measured: the MPCe Q-Link mode from a drum track to plugin parameters, 16 Levels, Note Repeat, pad lighting from a plugin.

## Pad pressure

The MPC pad sends polyphonic aftertouch for every held pad ("MPC XL pads"). The plugin turns it into an opening of Filter 1 by default, for every preset, without changing what a preset contains:

- **What it does.** The own engine (the engine of the MPC package) adds, per voice, an offset to the Filter 1 cutoff: pressure 0 to 1 of that voice (poly aftertouch value divided by 127) times the amount times 36 semitones, added in the control domain of the cutoff (the same domain the modulation matrix uses, `core/engine-own/voice/voice.cpp`). Only the cutoff of Filter 1: not Filter 2, not the frame, the level, the vibrato or the unison. It comes on top of the knob and of any modulation slot of the patch (if a preset's modulation matrix already contains a route from Poly Aftertouch to Filter 1 Cutoff, such as "MPE Pressure Lead", the layer does not add to that voice, preventing a duplicate effect). The pressure of each voice is smoothed with a one pole filter of 12 milliseconds, so the stream of values the pad sends (about one every 6 milliseconds) moves the cutoff in fine steps and does not make it zipper. A note starts with its first pressure value as it is. The layer is a function of the pad pressure only: the knob, the presets and the saved state of the engine are not written to. It costs nothing while the amount is zero or the switch is off (no smoothing, no addition), and a voice does not leave the envelope cache of the engine for it (`core/engine-own/own_engine.h`, `setOwnPadPressureToCutoff`).
- **Polyphonic vs Channel Aftertouch (0xA0 vs 0xD0).** The layer reacts strictly to polyphonic aftertouch (0xA0, per-voice/per-pad pressure). Channel aftertouch (0xD0), which keyboard instruments such as the MPC Key 61 may send from their keys, is currently ignored by the pad pressure layer; measurement from the designer's test rig is pending, and until then the layer reacts strictly to 0xA0.
- **Two parameters.** "Pad Pressure" (off or on, display "Off" / "On") and "Pad Pressure Amount" (0 to 100 percent, display "35") come after the 32 "Spectrum n" columns: indices 2673 and 2674 (2637 engine parameters, 4 cursors, 32 spectrum columns); in every build except the overlay build they are the last two parameters of the list (the overlay build has "Overlay Instance" after them, see "Parameter order"). Both are automatable and have parameter properties (a switch; a step of 1 percent). No index before them moves. A build without the LFO display has them at 2637 and 2638, one without the spectrum at 2641 and 2642; the skin of `45_pad_pressure.py` refers to the indices of the default build.
- **Parameter order (fixed).** The parameter list of the MPC shell is, in this order: the engine parameters (`docs/params.json`, 2637), 4 "LFO n Cursor" (2637 .. 2640), 32 "Spectrum n" (2641 .. 2672), "Pad Pressure" (2673), "Pad Pressure Amount" (2674), and, only in a build with `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OVERLAY` (the overlay block, in this order): "Overlay Instance" (2675), the three Play selectors "Play Osc", "Play Env", "Play LFO" (2676 .. 2678), the 24 Play proxies (2679 .. 2702, group after group: oscillator, envelope, LFO, eight each) and the sixteen Q proxies "Q 1" .. "Q 16" (2703 .. 2718, see "Q proxies and the plugin-drawn pages"), 2719 parameters in all. "Overlay Instance" is the FIRST parameter of the overlay block, not the last of the list (it is not automatable and not in the chunk, as before); the Play proxies and the Q proxies come after it. **The 16 Q proxies (format change before the first public release, 2026-11-16):** the first builds had four, "Q 1" .. "Q 4" at 2703 .. 2706 (2707 parameters). The twelve new ones, "Q 5" .. "Q 16", are APPENDED after "Q 4" (2707 .. 2718), at the very end of the list, so nothing a saved project or preset can reference changed its index: the engine parameters, the display parameters, "Overlay Instance", the Play parameters and "Q 1" .. "Q 4" are where they were. The test `play_host.cpp` writes every one of these numbers out (count 2719, "Q 1" 2703, "Q 4" 2706, "Q 5" 2707, "Q 16" 2718, the first parameters before them) and fails when one moves; the skin generator test (`test_mpc_skin_overlay.py`) checks the same for the Q-Link maps. The indexes of the builds without the overlay never move (the overlay build only adds the block at the very end, it adds nothing to a production build); a build without the LFO display or the spectrum moves the later groups down as described above, and the overlay block is always the last one. Everything that computes these indexes follows it: `PluginInstance::overlayParamIndex()`, `qBase()` and `padPressureBase()` in `shell-mpc/synthyboy_mpc.cpp`, the overlay instance report, `ctx.param_count` of the skin modules (`45_pad_pressure.py`, `40_spectrum.py`, `50_filter_overlay.py`, `82_play.py`, `83_pages.py`: params.json entries + 4 + 32 + 2 [+ 1 + 27 + 16 for the overlay block]), and the host tests (`lfo_live_host`, `spectrum_live_host`, `pad_pressure_host`, `overlay_instance_host`, `play_host`; the first three take the word `overlay`).
- **Pad pressure in the overlay.** The overlay draws the Filter 1 curve from the engine's readout of the modulated cutoff (`copyReadout`, `setReadoutTargets`). The readout of a knob is the value as the voice that sounded last hears it (`Voice::modulatedParam`), and the pad pressure layer adds its offset to exactly that modulation delta, so the displayed cutoff and the curve include the pad pressure contribution of that voice, without changing the sound (the readout only reads; test `pad_pressure_is_in_the_filter_1_cutoff_readout` in `core/tests/test_own_pad_pressure.cpp`). With several held notes the curve shows the voice played last, like for every other modulation. Channel aftertouch (0xD0) is not used by the layer, so it does not move the curve either.
- **Defaults (changed 2026-11-16).** OFF, 35 percent. Pressure is the PRESET's business now: a saw pad wants it, a punk bass does not, so the plugin-wide layer no longer opens Filter 1 on every sound. The factory presets that suit it (held pads, leads, held keys, one drone: 49 of 112) carry the route Poly Aftertouch -> Filter 1 Cutoff in their own matrix (`scripts/propose-pad-pressure.py` proposes it from the category and the shape of Env 1, the data is `scripts/factory-preset-pad-pressure.json`, `scripts/gen-factory-presets.py` writes it, the list for correction is `pad-pressure-presets-proposed.md`; a few demo presets have 70 percent); the layer skips a voice whose preset has that route (no doubling). The layer itself stays for whoever wants pressure on every sound: switch it on (the host's Master tab of the production skin, or PAD PRESS on the plugin-drawn Master page, VOICES block) and set the amount (PAD %, 35 percent: a full pressure opens the cutoff by about one octave, 100 percent three). The two parameters and the chunk members did not move; a project saved with the layer ON keeps it ON (the chunk members win over the default; only a project without them gets the new default OFF). The settings are not part of a preset, a program change leaves them alone. The pad's Full Level must be off (below), or the pad sends no pressure at all.
- **State.** The two settings are saved in the state chunk as two members at the end of the document (`"mpcPadPressure":1,"mpcPadPressureAmount":35.00`, `shell-mpc/pad_pressure.h`) and restored with it. A chunk without them (a project saved before this feature) leaves the settings as they are. The chunk is the same with and without the display parameters.
- **Full Level must be off.** With the pad's Full Level on, every hit has velocity 127 and the pad sends no pressure at all ("MPC XL pads"), so the layer has nothing to follow. The skin says so beside the controls.
- **Skin.** The Master tab has its bottom row free: `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/45_pad_pressure.py` puts the toggle (Q-Link 1) and the amount knob (Q-Link 2) in its first two columns and a two line text in the third ("Full Level must be off for pressure"); nothing is placed at x 1025 or to the right of it, and the skin keeps its 34 tabs (the validator's one accepted finding stays "34 tabs; at most 32 are supported"). The key and the short names of the two parameters are registered with `ctx.add_param_key` (`scripts/apply-mpc-skin-extras.py`), so `80_layout.py` and `85_names.py` treat them like any other control.
- **Build options (CMake).** `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PAD_PRESSURE` (default ON in `shell-mpc`; the source default is 0: a build that does not define it has no pad pressure code, no parameter, no string of it and no chunk member). The skin refers to the parameters, so the MPC package must be built with it ON. The Vital engine of the desktop build has no such layer: with it the two parameters exist and are saved, and the audio does not depend on them (the package is built with the own engine).
- **Tests.** `core/tests/test_own_pad_pressure.cpp` (the layer off is bit identical to a stream without pressure, it opens the filter more with a larger amount, only the voice that is pressed, only Filter 1, the step response of the smoothing, no zipper in the stream of 1/127 steps, the cutoff returns to the knob, no doubling when the mod matrix routes Poly Aftertouch to Cutoff, MPE Pressure Lead audio byte-identical with layer on vs off); `shell-mpc/tests/pad_pressure_host.cpp` (the plugin: indices, names, defaults, properties, writes, byte-identical chunk round trip and an older chunk, a program change, no doubling with mod matrix route, the audio with a pressed note in the own engine and the Vital engine, the build without the feature; also under ThreadSanitizer) through `shell-mpc/accept.sh`; `scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_pad_pressure.py` (the controls, their places, the Q-Links, the 34 tabs).
- **Not measured on a device.** How the opening of the filter sounds from a pad, whether the default amount is right on the MPC XL and the Key 61, and the CPU of the layer with a full pad bank; see "Known host behaviour" for what has been measured so far.

## Output level

Every shell passes the engine's output through ONE output stage, `core/include/synthyboy/output_stage.h` (`synthyboy::OutputStage`): the MPC shell (through `shell-mpc/output_stage.h`, which adds the plugin setting LIMIT), the JUCE VST3 / AU and the CLAP shells, and the offline renders that play what a shell plays (`synthyboy_preset_render --output-stage`, `synthyboy_sound_lab` profile key `stage=on`; without them both tools write the raw engine output, which is what `scripts/measure-loudness.py` measures). The stage is:

- a make-up gain, **`synthyboy::kMakeupGainDb` = 0 dB** (unity), the one value of every shell, smoothed over 20 milliseconds when it changes. The MPC build option `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OUTPUT_GAIN_DB` (CMake cache entry of `shell-mpc`, empty by default) overrides it for that build only (tests, trial packages; range -24 .. 24). **The rule (the owner, 2026-11-20): unity gain staging, stock-like headroom, no limiter, no guard.** With every level control at 0 (the preset's `master.volume` aside, the host fader at 0 dB) the track output carries the engine's output as it is: no hidden gain, no limiter, no latency. The level lives in the presets' `master.volume` and per-profile `tierTrimDb` (`docs/release/OUTPUT-LEVEL.md`, `scripts/level-to-stock.py`): single note C3 at velocity 127 targets stock instrument level parity across all categories (see `docs/release/OUTPUT-LEVEL.md` for the exact measured stock reference numbers and chord headroom targets), and every chord at velocity 127 stays below full scale without limiter on both profiles (`low` Key 61 and `high,voices=16,control=economy` XL). Master volume is set from the XL solve, and any difference on the Key 61 is trimmed by the top-level preset member `tierTrimDb` (applied via `OutputStage::setGainDb` on Key 61 only). Every preset keeps at least 5 dB room up to +6 dB (`master.volume <= +1.0 dB`). Velocity tracking is fully linear for all presets; note that pad Full Level sends fixed velocity 127 from the hardware on every hit (lines 705-717). Existing saved projects do not migrate automatically; users must reload presets to apply updated levels.
- a **stereo-linked look-ahead peak limiter** behind it (section "Output limiter"), **off by default in every shell**: the user's choice (LIMIT on the MPC), or a preset's own (the member `outputLimiter` of a `.sbpreset`, read when the program is loaded). When it is on, the 3 milliseconds of look-ahead are added to the latency each wrapper reports.

Why no limiter and why headroom: stock MPC instruments maintain headroom rather than driving into a ceiling (see `docs/release/OUTPUT-LEVEL.md` for stock instrument measurements; no stock instrument clips). With engine voice-count gain alpha = 0.5 (1/√n), polyphonic chords naturally sum without excessive crest factor, allowing the output stage to run cleanly at unity gain without limiter or guard. The engine's safety stage (`core/engine-own/dsp/master_stage.h` `outputLimit`: identity up to 1.8, tanh knee towards 2.0) sits before the shell stage.

The spectrum of the MPC shell shows the signal after the output stage, that is what the host gets. Tests: `shell-mpc/tests/run_output_stage_test.sh` (the stage alone, the NEON form against the scalar one, the setting), `core/tests/test_output_stage_presets.cpp` (every factory preset through the stage) and `shell-mpc/tests/output_level_host.cpp` (the plugin against a build with 0 dB, limiter forced off and on); `shell-mpc/accept.sh` runs the first and the last.

### Output limiter

- **The design** (`synthyboy::OutputStage`, the comment at the top of the header has the details). After the make-up gain every sample goes into a delay line of the look-ahead, **3 milliseconds** (132 samples at 44.1 kHz, 144 at 48 kHz, 288 at 96 kHz). A detector sees the samples as they enter: the gain a sample needs is 1 under the ceiling, else the ceiling over its peak (the louder of the two channels: one gain for both, the stereo image does not move); the minimum of that over the look-ahead window (a streaming van Herk / Gil-Werman minimum, constant work per sample); a two-stage release; then two moving averages that together span the look-ahead window (W = D + 1 samples: B1 + B2 - 1 = W), so the gain falls along an S-shaped ramp that ends **exactly** at the peak when the peak leaves the delay line: no overshoot, no step. The output is the delayed sample times that gain; a hard clip at the ceiling behind it is the guarantee for anything the design does not cover (rounding, input above 2^60): in the tests it never acts.
- **Constants.** Ceiling **-0.5 dB full scale** (0.94406): close to full scale, half a decibel of room for the inter-sample peaks a DAC reconstructs and for a host stage after the plugin that rounds or adds a little (the MPC's track and master stages). Look-ahead 3 milliseconds: the attacks of the factory presets (velocity 127 chords) take from under a millisecond (Fast Arp Bass, Pitch Drop 808, Tine Glass Keys) to about 30 milliseconds from the onset to their crest; 3 milliseconds is the upper end of what is inaudible as latency for a played instrument and makes the gain ramp long enough that it does not click on a low note, and the overloads that come sooner than that come from silence, where the ramp has nothing to change. Release in two stages: the fast stage releases with **30 milliseconds** but only down to the slow stage; the slow stage is the reduction a lasting overload needs: it follows the needed reduction up at a rate that grows with how long the overload has lasted (the weight `s` rises with 300 milliseconds while overloads come within 25 milliseconds of each other, and falls with 300 milliseconds when they stop; at full weight the slow stage follows with 20 milliseconds) and releases with **300 milliseconds**. So a drum hit or a pluck recovers fast (the slow stage has hardly moved), a held chord or a bass held over the ceiling brings the slow stage to the reduction its crests need and the gain does not move at the rate of the waveform, and its release after the overload is slow; a pluck followed by a held sound recovers from its attack down to what the held part needs, so the loudest 400 milliseconds of a sound never fall as the make-up gain rises. The make-up gain is smoothed over 20 milliseconds; a switch of LIMIT crossfades over 10 milliseconds.
- **Off and idle.** While nothing needs a reduction the detector is not run (the "idle" path, the NEON form on ARM) and the output is the gained input delayed by exactly the look-ahead, to the bit. With LIMIT off the output is the gained input **without** the delay (latency 0), to the bit; the detector keeps running so that switching on finds the reduction already computed. A switch crossfades the two paths (no click; switching on, the crossfade is clipped at the ceiling).
- **Cost.** No division (the reciprocal of the peak is an integer seed and three Newton steps from below) and no libm call per sample; the coefficients are made in `prepare`. The idle path costs a gain multiply and the delay line: NEON on ARM, four samples at a time, the same samples as the scalar loop to the bit (`output_stage_lanes_host.cpp`, also with flush-to-zero). Measured figures: "Observed on the device" has none yet; the Mac figures are in the report of the branch `work/loudness-fix` and in the output of `output_stage_host` (TIMING line).
- **Latency.** The look-ahead is reported to the host while the limiter is on, where the host has a field. MPC shell: `AEffect::initialDelay` is the engine's latency (Bode's frequency shifter) plus the look-ahead while LIMIT is on; set when the instance is made and at every `effSetSampleRate`; any change (the switch, a chunk, a program with its own `outputLimiter`, the engine's latency) marks it stale, and the next dispatcher call that is not `effProcessEvents` (so never on the audio thread) writes the field and sends `audioMasterIOChanged` (`PluginInstance::latencyDirty`, `latencyNow`). Whether the MPC compensates `initialDelay` for an instrument track is not known; 3 milliseconds are inaudible for a played synth. JUCE: `setLatencySamples` (engine + stage), updated in `processBlock` when it changes; CLAP: the latency extension, with a restart request when it changes; the JUCE shell's `reset()` restarts the engine and the stage (the host's flush).
- **The switch.** Master page, OUTPUT block: the LED toggle **LIMIT** under the voice override (a touch target, no Q slot: it is a setting of the plugin, not an engine parameter, so there is no table index a Q knob could point to). Default **OFF**: the constant `kOutputLimiterDefaultOn` in `shell-mpc/output_stage.h` is the one place to flip it.
- **Where it is stored.** Like the pad pressure layer it is not a host parameter (the host's parameter count, 2719 in the overlay build, does not change) and not part of a preset: it rides in the state chunk as the member `"mpcOutputLimiter":0|1`, written before the pad pressure members (which are read from the very end), read from the last 240 bytes. A chunk without the member (a project saved before) leaves the setting as it is, that is the default (OFF). A program (`effSetProgram`) sets it from its preset: the member `"outputLimiter":1` switches it on, without the member (and for Init) it is off; `scripts/gen-factory-presets.py` writes the member for the presets named in `scripts/factory-preset-output-limiter.json` (empty today). `PluginInstance::outputLimiter` / `applyOutputLimiter()` in `shell-mpc/synthyboy_mpc.cpp`; the page reaches it through `IInstanceView::outputLimiter` / `setOutputLimiter` (`shell-mpc/overlay/curve_source.h`).
- **Forcing it for tests.** `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OUTPUT_LIMITER` in the environment (read when the instance is opened): `1` forces the limiter on, `0` off, anything else leaves it to the setting; a build with `-DSYNTHYBOY_MPC_OUTPUT_LIMITER=ON` still forces it on. `output_level_host` sets the variable for the state it measures. While it is forced the toggle shows the state the stage runs.
- **Tests.** `shell-mpc/tests/output_stage_host.cpp`: the gain alone when off (to the bit, no delay), transparency when on (to the bit, delayed by the look-ahead, also after a limited passage), the ceiling (bursts, steps, noise, a single spike, NaN, infinities, huge values, mono, one buffer for both channels, the first samples after a reset, 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, any block size), the attack (the crest comes out at the ceiling with exactly the needed gain, the gain falls over the look-ahead before it), the gain curve (its second difference bounded: no zipper; a held chord's gain does not rise by more than a third of a decibel in any 5 millisecond step), the two release stages measured from the gain trace, the distortion of a held sine 3 dB over the ceiling (100 Hz and 1 kHz), the loudness order over make-up gains (tested over 0 to 18 dB, historic sweep), latency and reset, switching on and off while playing (no step larger than the signal's own plus the crossfade), the chunk member and the environment; `output_stage_lanes_host.cpp`: NEON = scalar to the bit; `core/tests/test_output_stage_presets.cpp` (`synthyboy_test_output_stage_presets`, label `slow`): the 160 factory presets, a velocity 127 chord and a single note, the Key 61 and XL profiles, at make-up gains 2, 4, 6, 9 and 15 dB: no sample above the ceiling, every sample finite, no safety clip, no loudness inversion beyond 0.15 LU; it prints the gain reduction per category. `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/mastervoice_ui_host.cpp` `testMasterOutputLimiter` (LIMIT shows the view's state, touch target, inside the OUTPUT block, a tap writes, not an engine parameter).

There is no separate parameter for the level of the instrument: that is Master Volume (first knob of the second row of the Macros tab), which is saved with the project.

### Unattended device level sweep (`scripts/device-level-sweep.py`)

To verify and record level calibration across the 160 factory presets (program indices 1..160; index 0 is the empty Init program and is not swept) on real hardware without interactive operator intervention, `scripts/device-level-sweep.py` automates program selection, note hits (C3 = 48), peak readout, and track mixer screenshot capture.

#### Modes of operation

- **Run on device (`run`):**
  ```bash
    # Key 61 (note 48 via seqnote 129 0, like the XL):
    python3 scripts/device-level-sweep.py run --device k61 [--limit N] [--resume] [--outdir DIR]

    # MPC XL (uses seqnote 129 0 MIDI input):
    python3 scripts/device-level-sweep.py run --device xl [--limit N] [--resume] [--outdir DIR]

    # Dry-run preview (prints planned shell/SSH commands without touching hardware):
    python3 scripts/device-level-sweep.py run --device k61 --dry-run --limit 3
  ```
  Default output directory: `/Users/szabi/Documents/Projects/Safian/.hw-local/mpc-perf/level-sweep-<device>-<date>/`.

- **Offline verifier (`verify`):**
  Checks that every preset index 1..160 has exactly two images (`-full.png`, `-half.png`), no other images, that `-full` was written before `-half` by file mtime (and both are the two newest files of that preset), that `sweep.csv` has 160 lines (`--limit N` verifies presets 1..N of a partial trial), and that peak levels match the target thresholds (`--full-target -10.0 --full-tol 2.0 --half-diff 11.9 --half-tol 3.0`, owner target: factory-like level, full hit near -10 dB full scale, half hit about 12 dB lower). Writes `verify-report.md` with failing presets listed first, and exits non-zero if any check fails:
  ```bash
    python3 scripts/device-level-sweep.py verify <outdir> [--full-target -10.0] [--full-tol 2.0] [--half-diff 11.9] [--half-tol 3.0]
  ```

- **Selftest (`selftest`):**
  Runs the verifier against synthetic test suites (pass case, out-of-tolerance peaks, missing images, inverted mtime, stray images, truncated CSV):
  ```bash
    python3 scripts/device-level-sweep.py selftest
  ```

#### Files and output structure

- **Files on device:**
  * Request file: `/tmp/synthyboy-select-program` (decimal program index; 0 = Init, 1..160 = the factory presets, `program_list_length` 161).
  * Acknowledgement: `/tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack` (`<counter> ok <index> <name>`).
  * Status file: `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status.txt` (switched by `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status-on`, `output_peak` is the peak since the previous status write, the file is rewritten about once a second; `output_peak_total` is held max and not used. The sweep streams `output_peak` and `output_blocks` every 0.1 s over one ssh session while the hit is played; a write is identified by its `output_blocks` and counts for a hit only when it was first seen after the hit started (window: hit start to 3 s later, max over those writes), writes seen earlier are discarded, and before each hit the sweep waits for two silent writes so no tail leaks into the next number).
  * Play log: `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log.txt` (switched by `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log`, records note-on events and velocities; the sweep takes the new note_on lines of each hit and accepts the hit only when exactly one note_on, note 48 at the expected velocity, arrived).
- **Files in output directory:**
  * `<NNN>-<preset-slug>-full.png`: Host track view mixer meter picture after full hit (velocity 127).
  * `<NNN>-<preset-slug>-half.png`: Host track view mixer meter picture after half hit (velocity 64).
  * `preflight.png`: Initial host track view picture taken during preflight.
  * `sweep.csv`: Exactly 160 lines, one per preset (`index,name,full_peak_dBFS,half_peak_dBFS,diff,play_log_velocities,timestamp`).
  * `sweep.log`: Unattended execution log.
  * `verify-report.md`: Verification results table.

#### Device rules kept

1. **Lock directories:** Takes `/Users/szabi/Documents/Projects/Safian/.hw-local/mpc-tools/DEVICE-LOCK` (Key 61) or `/Users/szabi/Documents/Projects/Safian/.hw-local/mpc-tools/DEVICE-LOCK-XL` (XL) before touching device, released on completion, error, or Ctrl-C.
2. **Project Sbtest only:** Operates exclusively in the currently open project `Sbtest`; never saves, never reboots.
3. **Single touch per command:** Never touches anything except the peak-hold reset tap on the meter number (Key 61: UI (38, 698), the green readout under strip 1, via `/tmp/mtouch tap 38 698 90`; XL: UI (355, 698) via mirrored `/tmp/mtouch tap 924 101 90`).
4. **Note injection paths:**
   - Both devices: ALSA sequencer MIDI input via `seqnote 129 0` (note 48 = C3, the note of the offline level table, held eight tenths of a second) with `SEQNOTE_VEL=127` and `SEQNOTE_VEL=64`. Key 61 pads are not used: the Pad Perform maps every pad note (0x24..0x33 and the rest of 0..127 that reach the plugin) to MIDI 72..98 (`hwctl.sh raw "99 25 7f"` plays 72 = C5, 3 dB louder than C3 on basses), none to 48; seqnote 129 0 reaches the plugin as note 48 (play log) and the track meter.
5. **Retry & fault tolerance:** Up to 2 retries per preset on error (partial images cleaned before retry); after 5 consecutive failed presets, sweep halts automatically to protect against persistent device issues.

## Overlay (experimental)

Live pictures on seventeen tabs (the Play page's three sub-tabs included), drawn by the plugin itself on a display plane that the MPC firmware does not offer to plugins: the response curve of Filter 1 and Filter 2, the shape of LFO 1 - 4 with the cursor of the playing voice, (the oscillator pictures of this first overlay are gone: the OSC tab is now drawn entirely by the plugin, see "The OSC tab"), the live phase marker of Envelope 1 - 4 on top of the native envelope widget, and the spectrum of the plugin's output as a curve over the row of meters of the Macros tab. EXPERIMENTAL, off by default; proven on the MPC Key 61 (32-bit) and on the MPC XL (64-bit) with the same code. The skin alone cannot do this: the SDK widgets have no curve that follows the real filter, no LFO shape with modulation and smoothing, no oscillator output with warp and morph (the `Envelope` widget draws broken lines from a few stage values, see "Envelope tabs").

**What it is.** The panel is driven by a display controller with several planes: the whole UI of the application on one (the primary plane), a few the application uses for itself (a cursor, a small sprite) and some that are free and sit above the UI (on the Key 61: UI 33, cursor 35, free 38 and 40, CRTC 37; on the XL: UI 34, an application sprite 40, free 46, 52, 58, 64, 70 and 76, CRTC 82). The plugin lives inside the MPC process, which holds the display device (`/dev/dri/card0` on both) as DRM master. The overlay module (`shell-mpc/overlay/`, compiled in only with the CMake option `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OVERLAY`, default OFF) finds the CRTC and a free plane at run time ("Discovery") and puts an ARGB picture on that plane over the region of the view whose tab is on screen; the host's own UI keeps working underneath (it still gets every touch). Only one view is visible at a time (one plane), of ONE instance at a time: with several plugin instances (several tracks) the one whose page is on screen is drawn ("Several instances").
