| `LedList` (`led_list.h`) | 2 to 8 options (23 characters each), index, a mask of disabled options, an optional title | `Index` (the option) | a radio list, ALL options visible, one lit (dot + name, a faint flat tint on the lit row); the component chooses 1, 2 or 3 columns from its rectangle (the fewest where the rows are at least 56 px high and the columns as wide as the widest name needs: `LedList::columnsFor`); a tap on an enabled row selects it; a Q-Link step or the wheel moves over the enabled options without wrapping (the wheel up goes up); a disabled option is drawn dim and skipped. `sizeOk` is true when any arrangement fits |
| `MenuButton` | icon (down, gear, expand corners = full screen, none), label (up to 23 characters: `char label[24]`) and optional value (`char value[24]`) | `Request` | tap |
| `ListPopup` | names (the owner's array: the engine's choice labels), count, current, the box and the parent rectangle of its dimmed backdrop | `Index` (a tapped row), `Request` (the backdrop was tapped); turn moves the current item | tap on a row, vertical drag scrolls by whole rows, tap outside closes. A list longer than the box has a scroll zone of 44 pixels at its right edge: the thumb in it follows the finger (an absolute drag), a touch on the track above or below the thumb pages by a window less one row, nothing there selects a row. A `Stepper` with `listable` set sends a `Request` for a tap on its middle half (the name; the outer quarters still step) |
| `DetailButton` | icon (gear, bell, envelope, route) or label (up to 23 characters), optional main value (text of 15 characters, 0 to 1 or an integer range) | `Value` / `Index` for the main value; `Request` (details requested) on a tap | vertical drag and turn change the main value, a tap asks for the details |
| `RouteButton` | target text (3 characters), two indicator segments (their meaning is the owner's: an open question of the specification) | `Request` (route panel requested) | tap (a `DetailButton` without a value) |
| `PictogramSelector` | up to 10 items (`kMaxItems` = 10; pictogram id, label of 6 characters, enabled; an unknown id = a text cell), index | `Index` | tap on an enabled cell, turn steps through the enabled items; a row, or a column when the rectangle is taller than wide |

**LED controls in use (2026-11-16).** Voice (the five targets, 10x DETUNE, the LFO time mode), Filter (ENV 1, CLEAN, ROUTING, the OUTPUT popup destination), FX (every enumerated parameter: Off / On is a LED toggle, 2 named to 8 values a LED list, more than 8 the stepper with its popup; the rows of a page are as high as their cells need and the display gives up height before a list becomes a stepper) and the Mod slot page (INVERT, STEREO, SYNC). Not changed, with the reason: the filter MODEL (27) and STYLE (12) lists (more than 8: stepper with popup), F1 / F2 / F1+F2, the FX RACK (a 56 px header has no room for a list), the Mixer strip switch (a power glyph with the strip name is already one compact cell) and its destination (a list of 3 to 5 rows would take the height of the fader), the Mod matrix polarity and state cells (66 px wide, a LED toggle needs 96) and the ALL / BY SOURCE / BY DEST buttons (a tab row, a list would not be clearer), the Voice ROW selector (the grid has its row tabs), the Macros page (no toggle or short list).

**LED controls in use (2026-11-17, the rest of the pages).** Master (every switch, VOICE OVERRIDE, SAME NOTE, STEAL, MODE, TUNING, XYZ TO MACROS as LED lists; STEREO is a knob, being a percentage; the long lists SCALE and KEY stay steppers; the meters have their scale in a column of their own and the ramp of the Mixer; the first row of MAIN is OUTPUT and PITCH, the second VOICES (the widest) and GLIDE with its curve in the whole height), LFO (above), Env (BEATS and LEGATO in the cells of the knobs next to them, the dot and the name at the height of the dials), Macros (BANK 1 / BANK 2 a LED list in the bar, the targets of a card as tags without a button outline: the whole area is one touch target), the Mixer strip power (the glyph and the name, no box). Rule for all pages: the controls of a row are one height and the chips of a Q group stand on one line; a list that fits no cell as a LED list stays a stepper with its popup (the LFO MODE: six names of up to 16 characters).

The pictograms are a DATA table (`pictogram::kTable` in `base.h`: id, name, drawing function; sine, wavetable, granular, multisample, noise, sample, spectral and the sub shapes half sine, triangle, saw, square, pulse are in it): a new pictogram is a new row.

**Composite components** (built from the base components only; the ids of the children are given by the owner; a change comes out with the id of the child; `turn` goes to the primary child where there is one).

| Component | Made of | File |
|---|---|---|
| `TuneRow` | OCT, SEM, FIN, CRS number fields and the pitch tracking toggle; the vertical variant of the full screen is two columns of three rows (OCT SEM, FIN CRS, TRACK MORE) that fill the height of its block (never a row below 56 pixels); the noise variant is PITCH, FINE, KEY TRACK in three rows one above the other (the TUNE column beside the display of the sample noise) or, with `setNoiseRow (true)`, in ONE row of three equal cells (the generated noise's TUNE block); the short variant (OCT, CRS in one row) | `composite.h` |
| `TypeBar` | the strip under the header above the display: 1 to 4 slots from a preset table (`barpresets`): wavetable (FRAME, PHASE, RAND, MAP), granular (GRAIN, AMOUNT, LOOP, UNISON), sub (PHASE), multisample (ENV, VEL TRACK `ToggleNumber`, RAND), noise (STEREO, ENV 1), sample (PHASE, REGION, SLICE, UNISON), spectral (PHASE, FILTER, SCAN, UNISON). The filled slots are one Q group (`fillGroup` in `panel.h`) | `composite.h` |
| `UnisonBox` | the gear (`DetailButton`) and the up / down stepper | `composite.h` |
| `WarpSlot`, `WarpPair` | knob (amount, 184 px), type stepper (at most 300 pixels wide), optional extra value (VAR: dial 52 pixels, `kVarDial`); the slot is centred in the room it gets; dim and the knob without reaction when the type is OFF; the pair has two slots mirrored with the caption WARP (or stacked inside full-screen panel blocks) | `composite.h` |
| `KnobRandPair` | a main knob and the small RAND knob below, joined by a line | `composite.h` |
| `RoutePanel` | the popup of the route button: close button in a row of its own, target stepper, F1 / F2 balance knob (bipolar, enabled only when the owner says the target is the filter), BUS 1 and BUS 2 knobs; the four values are one Q group (`group`). The popup variant (`setPopup`) draws a dimmed backdrop over the parent rectangle and its own framed box; a tap on the backdrop is a `Request` change with the id of `setBackdropId` (close requested) | `composite.h` |
| `ToggleNumber` | a toggle and the amount it applies to (VEL TRACK); turn goes to the amount | `composite.h` |
| `KnobPanel` | a grid of parts from a preset TABLE (`presets::kWavetable`, `kGranular`, `kNoise`, full presets `kWavetableFull`, `kMultisampleFull`, `kGranularFull`, `kSampleFull`, `kSpectralFull`, `kSubFull`, `kNoiseGenFull`, and detail presets): the owner finds the parts by slot | `panel.h` |
| `DetailPage` | the page of the details of one control: a top bar with the back button and a title, an optional display area, the values by a `KnobPanel` preset (`kUnisonDetail` (wavetable): [MODE, STACK, WIDTH, RANGE] and [WT POS, WARP 1, WARP 2 spread]; `kUnisonDetailMultisample`: the same four and [WARP 1, WARP 2 spread]; `kUnisonDetailGranular`: the same four and [START, SPAN]; `kGrainDetail`: [AMOUNT, AMOUNT rand, SKEW, SKEW rand] and [SHAPE]; `kEnvelopeDetail` (multisample envelope, display area reserved above): [DELAY, A, H, D] and [S, R, OVERRIDE toggle]) in Q groups of four | `panel.h` |
| `ModulationsPage` | the modulations of one control: a row per entry (source name, depth knob, polarity toggle, bypass toggle, remove button), the back button, the Q group of the row touched last ([depth, polarity, bypass]) and the last row "add source" (a stepper over the sources the owner offers, names of 6 characters, and the add button: an `AddRequest` with the source id) | `panel.h` |
| `SourcePicker` | the source stepper with its dropdown button | `composite.h` |
| `TextEntry` | the keyboard page (`text_entry.h`, tab area 1280 x 628, minSize() is 1280 x 536): title, the text so far (cut to the limit in characters, the caret at its end), shift / caps, the letter layer and the digit / symbol layer, SPACE, backspace, CLEAR, CANCEL, OK; keys of 72 px height and at least 56 px width over the whole width | `Request` (the id of OK or of CANCEL; the text is `text ()`) | tap on a key (it acts when the finger lifts on it), a mouse click |
| `BasicControls` | the four values of the Q group "Basic" of an oscillator instance, one component for the compact view and the top band of the full screen (same members, `assignIds` gives the same ids): A, B, C = type pictograms, source (`SourcePicker`), PAN, LEVEL; Sub = waveform pictograms (sine, half sine, triangle, saw, square, pulse), OCT, PAN, LEVEL; Noise = noise type stepper, FILTER knob, PAN, LEVEL | `composite.h` |
| `OscillatorCompact` | the view of one third of the width (about 420 x 620 at 1280 x 800, proportional, `minSize()` 420 x 700): the header row (56 px: the source selector Sub / Osc A / Osc B / Osc C / Noise as a `Carousel` with a list: the arrows are an `Index` change, a tap on the name a `Request`), the display area (the rectangle shared by the three thirds, `play_rows.h`, reserved and reported) with its header band (the power toggle, the route button, not for Sub, the "full screen" button, a `Request`) and the plot area under it (`plotRect()`), the Basic group in one frame from the common controls line (stacked arrangement: the type cells, then ONE row: the table stepper with its list button / OCT, PAN, LEVEL); no tuning row, no type bar, no row of five tabs | `screen.h` |
| `OscillatorFullScreen` | the full screen of one instance on 1280 x 800 (other sizes in proportion), as a layout preset; there is no instance selector (the instance is the owner's: `setInstance (instance, kind)`): the top band (close button, on / off toggle, Basic in one row, the route button at the right end), below left the type bar under the header with the display below it (reserved and reported, the picture is the overlay views'; for Sub the display takes the full width), below right the tuning row in its vertical variant (full for A, B, C; absent for Sub; PITCH, FINE, KEY TRACK for Noise), the bottom band = the knob panel of the type as titled, bordered blocks side by side with a gap (`KnobPanel` presets with `PanelBlock` tables: wavetable and multisample UNISON \| WARP, the warp slots stacked inside their block; granular GRAIN \| PLAYBACK \| RANDOM; `presets::kWavetableFull`, `kMultisampleFull`, `kGranularFull`, `kSampleFull`, `kSpectralFull`; `presets::kSubFull` for Sub, `presets::kNoiseGenFull` / `kNoiseSampleFull` for Noise). Q groups: Basic, Type (the type bar, when shown), Tune (when shown), then the groups of the panel (wavetable and multisample: Unison [WT POS or TIMBRE, UNISON, DETUNE, BLEND] and Warp; granular: Grain [SCAN, DENS, LENGTH, WARP menu], Playback [OFFSET, DIR, PITCH], Random [LENGTH, PAN, LEVEL randomisation]; Sub: PITCH & MODE, PHASE & ROUTE, ROUTING; Noise: GENERATOR). Every Q group has a visible thin neutral outline and title (the type bar block, the tuning column titled TUNE, the panel blocks; Basic has its outline), the active group keeps its coloured frame and the 1 to 4 badges. Switching swaps only the type bar, the tuning row, the bottom band and the groups; a touch on a control of a group moves the Q frame to it; `turnQ` routes a hardware knob to the active group | `screen.h` |

**Q groups.** `QGroup` (`qgroup.h`) holds four slots, each a component; its rectangle is the union of the members' rectangles. `setActive (true)` marks the members (accent colour), `draw` draws ONE frame around the union and the index of every member (1 to 4: an index chip drawn by `Pen::qIndex` on the member's top left corner in the 16 pixel `kQLane` left above each member by layouts), `turn (knobIndex, delta)` routes the hardware knob to the member (nothing when the group is not active, the slot is empty or the index is outside 0 to 3). On a 16-knob device (MPC XL), idle mapped groups (blocks 1 .. 3) show knob numbers 5 .. 16 (`drawMapped`); on a 4-knob device (Key 61), chips show 1 .. 4 relative to the active bank. Frameless groups (`setFrameless`) are supported for separated controls (e.g. LFO rate + shape grid). A member may be a composite whose `turn` goes to its primary child (the unison box, a knob with its RAND pair). The members must be adjacent in the layout: `check (all, n)` returns how many components of `all` (the leaves of the layout, `collectLeaves`) are not members, not parts of a member and still overlap the union (the first one by pointer), so that a layout mistake is reported and testable.

**How to add a component.** Derive from `Component` (`core.h`), give it a `State`, `minSize`, `drawBody (Pen&)` that stays inside `rect_` and the `onDown`, `onMove`, `onUp`, `onTurn` gestures that return a `Change`; use the `Pen` helpers and the `TapGesture`; a composite derives from `Composite` (`composite.h`) and places its children in `layout ()`. Write the interface test first in `shell-mpc/ui/tests/`: every gesture gives exactly the documented change, clamps at the ends, nothing when disabled, the gesture is kept outside the rectangle, drawing inside the rectangle at the minimum size and at a large size. A new preset is a new table row, not new layout code.

**Tests.** `shell-mpc/ui/tests/ui_base_host.cpp`, `ui_composite_host.cpp` and `ui_screen_host.cpp` (built by `shell-mpc/accept.sh` under AddressSanitizer and UBSan; the pictures of the tests go to the directory in `UI_OUT`), and the demonstration program `shell-mpc/ui/tests/ui_demo.cpp` (the compact view and the full screen of the instances, the type bar presets, the detail pages, the route panel and popup, the knob in every modulation state, the pictogram selectors). The scene protocol additions are tested in `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/gpu_scene_host.cpp`. What the helper's shaders make of the new ops has not been compared with the CPU renderer on a device.

## Overlay look and drawing

The overlay's drawing was widened for a tech demo that should look good: thin lines of light, gradients, glow, proportional anti-aliased text, three looks. Everything below is in the overlay build only; the production (overlay OFF) build and the order of the 2707 parameters did not change.

**Scene protocol 6** (`shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/scene.h`, the GPU helper `gpu-helper/main.cpp`, the CPU reference `scene_cpu.h` + `draw.h`: one meaning, the tests compare the CPU picture; the shaders are checked on the device by reading the finished frame back).
- Cells: `kOpCellBegin` / `kOpCellEnd` (below, "Cached cells and layers"). Frame: `kMaxOps` 2048, `kMaxVertices` 65536 (the pool of the rows of all polylines of a scene), `kMaxCurve` 2048 (one polyline), `kMaxText` 8192 bytes. The shared block grew with them (336 KB); `writeScene` / `readScene` copy only the used part (the header, `numOps` ops, `numText` bytes, `numPoints` vertices), so a frame of a few hundred ops is a few kilobytes and the overlay thread stays free of allocation and locks (the seqlock is unchanged; the builder clears only the header). The counts of a torn read are cut to the limits before they are used.
- State ops: `kOpStyle` (the feather in pixels, round caps) and `kOpPaint` (four equally spaced colours along x, along y or along the angle of an arc; the alpha of the drawing op is a factor, so every curve has an alpha of its own). Line-like ops (Stroke, Line, Arc, Disc, VLine, RRect) take the feather from the style: below 1 px the plain 1 px anti-aliasing, above it a smooth step over that many pixels (the soft pass of a glow). A line of half width h is never stronger than 2h, so 0.5 px lines are fainter, not wider (`lineCoverage`).
- New shapes: `kOpRRect` (rounded rectangle, filled or an outline of a width inside the edges, signed distance field in the shader), `kOpBand` (the exact coverage of the strip between two polylines per pixel row: the min / max picture of a dense wave), round caps for Arc (a knob arc with round ends) and Line, an arc painted along its angle (`atan` in the shader).
- `kOpClip`: everything after it is limited to a rectangle (scissor on the GPU; the CPU reference puts back what the clipped ops drew outside), an empty rectangle switches it off; the Play view uses it to leave the strip of the host's Q-Link panel transparent.
- `kOpText`: a string from the text pool (any length, UTF-8, any size in pixels per em, tracking), proportional, drawn from a signed distance field atlas: the coverage is `clamp (0.5 + d * size / kEm, 0, 1)` on both renderers (`sdf_font.h`: glyph lookup, widths, `fitText` with an ellipsis, `layout`). The old 5 x 7 pixel font is gone from every view.
- `kOpGlowBegin` / `kOpGlowEnd`: the ops between them go to a layer of 1/2, 1/4 or 1/8 of the picture, which is blurred (two passes, 9 taps from 5 linear samples) and added over the picture: a bloom. `kOpFeedback`: the previous picture of a rectangle times a gain, under the new drawing (an afterglow); the helper copies the picture into a texture only for scenes that use it. The CPU reference draws neither (it skips the ops between the glow ops).
- Both glow ways (a wide faint extra pass per curve with a soft style; the bloom layer) are in the helper and were measured (trial 17 in "Observed on the device"): the soft pass costs per curve, the bloom is flat whatever the number of curves, so the bloom is the cheaper from about six curves (numbers in trial 17). The owner's taste decided the rest: NOTHING that carries a value is lit (signal lines, knob arcs, values, texts, selected items are crisp and anti-aliased); the only light is a faint back glow behind a display (`Look::backGlow`, bloom of its lines inside the display rectangle, 0.18 in look 1, 0 in look 2, 0.30 in look 3; the level of detail "reduced" has none).

**Cached cells and layers (scene protocol 8, `shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/scene.h`, `cell_plan.h`, the helper).** What the UI draws is mostly the same from one picture to the next (the blocks, the labels, the frames, the knobs at rest). The scene marks such parts as CELLS (`kOpCellBegin` with the rectangle and a hash of the content that `SceneBuilder::cellEnd` makes from the ops, the text and the rows of the curves inside, not from their places in the pools; `kOpCellEnd`): every leaf component, the under- and over-drawing of every composite, a Q frame with its tabs, the ground, the Env and LFO panels without their running point and the display with its block are cells. The helper keeps the cells of a scene in two LAYERS (textures the size of the picture): the cells before the first drawing op that is not in a cell are layer 0, the cells after such an op (the full screen button of the compact view, the popups) are layer 1 (they lie over what moves); a cell after a second such group makes the scene uncached (it is drawn whole). When the next scene comes, the cells are matched by rectangle and hash: a cell that is not in the layer yet or that is not in the scene any more is the damage, and the cells that lie over the damage are drawn again into the layer in a scissor box (their ops are built and drawn only for these). A picture is then made of one textured quad per layer and the ops that are not in a cell (the live read-outs). Cells must start and end with the default style and paint, may contain a bloom (GlowBegin / GlowEnd: it is added into the layer) but no Clear, Clip or Feedback, and keep inside their rectangle; the CPU reference ignores them (it draws the same picture), the tests `cell_plan_host` and `gpu_scene_host` check the hash, the plan, the damage and that equality.
**Partial pictures (buffer age).** The helper's EGL gives the age of the back buffer (`EGL_EXT_buffer_age`; what the Key 61 says is in "Observed on the device", trial 19): the buffer holds the picture from that many pictures ago. The helper paints into it only what changed since: the damage of the layers of this picture and of the age minus one pictures before, and the places of the ops that are drawn every picture (from their vertices, now and in the picture before), as at most four boxes in a scissor (the whole picture when the age is unknown, the clip of the scene changed, or the boxes cover more than seventy percent of it). `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-nopartial` switches it off (diagnostics); `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-egl.txt` (written once, after the 24th picture) has the EGL extensions of the display and the ages of the first 24 back buffers. The status file and the helper's statistics say how much was painted (`painted_px`, `full_frames`) and how much came from the layers (`cached_ops`, `redrawn`).
**The display while it changes.** The data of the display (the curves, the stack, the unison fan, the bloom) is a cached cell while it rests; while it changes every picture (a knob dragged along the wavetable position, a modulated position) it has changed within the last third of a second and is drawn with the picture instead (`PlayView`): a cell that is drawn again every picture only costs the layers under it too. The block of the display (gradient, keyline, grid) stays a cell. The live read-outs of the engine (read heads, grains, the phases of the sub, the read head of a sample noise) are always drawn over it (`display::drawLive`).
**The rate of the pictures.** The helper paces the plane updates on a grid of `stride` vblanks (`vblank_pacer.h`). The legacy plane update (SETPLANE) blocks until the vblank after the call; with stride 1 the grid computed the slot AFTER the vblank the previous update had just returned at, so every picture took two vblanks and 30 a second was the ceiling at any stride (trial 19 in "Observed on the device"). Stride 1 is now "no slot wait": the update follows the render at once. The plugin asks for it (`kMsgStride`): 60 a second on a GPU that is not of the Mali-T series (the XL), tried and kept only when the helper really presents 45 or more pictures a second while the picture moves (else back to 30 for two minutes); 30 on the Mali-T (the Key 61); `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-fps` (30 or 60) overrides. The scenes follow the stride (a scene a little before each picture). The status file says `gpu_rate_stride` and `gpu_rate_source`.
**The GPU clock.** The governor of the GPU (`simple_ondemand`, 100 to 600 MHz on the Key 61) lowers the clock when the load is low, so a picture that costs little at the highest clock takes several times as long at the lowest, and a change of the clock costs a stall. While pictures are made continuously (eight a second or more, three milliseconds or more each) the plugin raises the lowest clock the governor may use (`min_freq` of the devfreq node of the GPU) to the lowest available one of at least 300 MHz and puts it back when the picture has rested for three seconds, at the end and, from the file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-minfreq.orig`, at the next start after a run that died (`gpu_clock.h`); `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpuclock` with `off` leaves the clock to the governor. The status file says `gpu_clock_boost`.

**The font.** Titillium Web SemiBold (SIL Open Font License 1.1, the official release of the Google Fonts repository; `shell-mpc/overlay/fonts/TitilliumWeb-SemiBold.ttf` with `OFL.txt` next to it; the licence text is part of the repository, the font software keeps its own licence). The generator `scripts/gen-overlay-font.py` makes `shell-mpc/overlay/sdf_font_data.h`: an 8-bit signed distance atlas of 512 x 297 pixels (40 pixels per em, range +- 5 pixels, about 130 glyphs: ASCII, the Hungarian letters, the degree sign, a few typographic signs, and the arrows and triangles the UI uses, which the font does not have and the generator draws). It is generated from the font and the generated header is committed (the build does not need Python); `gen-overlay-font.py --check` and `scripts/tests/test_overlay_font.py` say whether the header is what the generator makes. The distance-field text is sharp from 12 to 40 pixels and for any fraction of a size on both renderers; there is no kerning.

**The theme** (`shell-mpc/ui/theme.h`): ONE table (`struct Look`) with every colour, gradient, line width, type size, radius and spacing the components and the views draw with; the components take their numbers from nowhere else. The type scale (title 28, value 24, label 15, item 22, small 12, minimum 9) is in the table. The colour of a modulation source (`sourceColour`: envelope orange, LFO green, velocity, key, wheel, pressure, macro, random) is the same on the depth arc of a knob, on its tab and on the modulation page; the instance colours are Sub orange, A cyan, B violet, C magenta, Noise green (look 1). The envelope and LFO gradients and the filter stops are in the table for the elements still to come. Next to the look there is the LEVEL OF DETAIL (`theme::Detail`, one table: the frames of the wavetable stack, the unison hairlines, the spectrum, the back glow, the afterglow): "full" is the designed picture, "reduced" (3 stack frames, 2 hairlines, no spectrum, no glow) is for a machine that cannot keep the full one within its frame time. The plugin chooses by measurement: while the GPU helper draws, the mean render time of the helper's pictures is taken once a second over the seconds with a moving picture (12 pictures or more); three such seconds in a row above fourteen milliseconds switch to "reduced" for the rest of the run; the CPU drawing (no helper) is "reduced" too. `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-detail` (`1` / `full`, `2` / `reduced`, anything else automatic) overrides it; the status file says `ui_detail`, `ui_detail_source` and `ui_detail_helper_render_ms`. The MPC XL (aarch64, stronger) is the primary target and gets "full" when it fits; the Key 61 is the cut-down machine and gets what its measured frame time allows. Three looks, three tables, no branches in the code: look 1 (`neon`) the reference palette, look 2 (`aurora`) calmer, thinner lines and little light, look 3 (`plasma`) the boldest: saturated, thicker lines, a lot of light, the instance colours dealt the other way round. The overlay reads `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-look` (a number 1 to 3) once a second; without the file it is look 1 (the status file says `ui_look`).

**The display** (`shell-mpc/ui/display.h`): the picture of the oscillator in its display rectangle. A wave is a thin crisp line along a gradient of the instance colour, over a faint area and a faint spectrum of the cycle; a wavetable shows seven frames of the table behind each other, the far ones fainter, and the frames near the position light up, so moving the position makes the highlight run along the stack; the unison voices are hairlines around the main curve (spread by the detune, as strong as the blend); a sample, multisample or granular oscillator is a min / max band with the loop range (the rest dimmed), the grain window and, when the position is known, the playhead. The compact view's envelope and LFO panels (`drawModule`) use the same language (ENV orange to red, LFO green to cyan, the running point and cursor). What the display does NOT show because the data is not there: the real playing position of a sample, the real positions and times of the grains.

## Q proxies and the plugin-drawn pages (overlay build)

The plugin draws some pages itself (the compact oscillator view on the Play page, the full-screen oscillator page and the detail pages above it; the drawing code is `shell-mpc/ui/`). This section is what sits under them: the hardware knobs, the page the knobs belong to, and the state of the pages.

**Q proxies.** Sixteen parameters "Q 1" .. "Q 16" after the last Play proxy (2703 .. 2718, 2719 parameters in all; overlay build only; nothing before them moves, "Parameter order (fixed)": Q 1 .. Q 4 are the old ones, Q 5 .. Q 16 were appended). The hardware knobs (Q-Links) of the host are mapped to them on the pages the plugin draws, Q-Link n = Q n on every plugin-drawn tab (skin: `Bank Direction "Row"`, all 16 Q-Links, `82_play.py` / `83_pages.py`; the host's own numbering stays, the SDK numbers the 4 x 4 grid from the bottom row). A Q proxy has no value of its own: the page sets the SLOTS, up to sixteen targets, each the table index of an engine parameter or "none" (`IInstanceView::setQGroup` in `shell-mpc/overlay/curve_source.h`; the logic is `QRouter` in `shell-mpc/q_router.h`, pure and tested on the host), and then
- reading Q n gives the normalised value of its target (0 when it has none), and its name, display text, label and properties are the target's (the name is the target's table name, e.g. "Cutoff Env Amount"; a macro is the exception: it reports the name the macro has been given, "Filter Sweep", "Macro 5" without one, cut at a character to the 64 bytes of the host's buffer, and the host is told by `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` when a rename or a loaded program / preset / chunk brings other names: "Macro names" below; a transpose that the Tune group splits into two knobs says "... Oct" and "... Sem", shows +n octaves / +n semitones and has the labels oct / st; a Q without a target, or with a virtual one of the LFO shape editor, is named "Q n" and shows nothing);
- writing Q n is the same write as the host's own write to the target (`writeRealParam`: mirror, protection, engine); a write of the value the target has (an echo of a report) changes nothing; a write to a Q without a target is ignored;
- a write inside the restore window (see "Project save and reload") is ignored: the chunk wins, the same rule as for the Play proxies;
- when the slots change, the display thread tells the host the sixteen new values (`audioMasterAutomate`, the mechanism of the Play proxies), so the knob display of the host follows, and then once `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` (`QRouter::poll`, the group-changed callback, once for any change of any of the 16 targets and never when nothing changed), because the names, units and value texts of the proxies are other now. **Measured on the Key 61 (2026-11-16, the host's Q-Link panel that appears while a knob is touched, the plugin told the host by a switch in a test build):** without any call the host KEEPS the names it read first (after the group changed from Macros 1-4 to 5-8 and back, and after a page change from the Play page's Sub group to Macros, the panel showed the names of an earlier group: "Sub Waveform ..." on the Macros page, "Macro 5 .. 8" for the group Macro 1-4); with `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` the panel shows the names, value texts and dial positions of the current group at once (the next time it appears, about half a second after the touch) and also after a group change while it is open (a tap on the BANK button 0.6 s after the touch: the panel was on the new names when it was photographed about 0.5 s later; the timing is approximate); `audioMasterIOChanged` alone changed nothing (stale names), so it is not used (it may also make the host re-initialise the plugin). `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` also makes the host read the program name again, and the host then shows the name exactly as the plugin reports it: the "*" that the host puts after the preset name of a project that was loaded ("Acid Saw Reso *") disappeared after the first call (measured: the header read "Acid Saw Reso" after the first group change and stayed so after knob turns). **The "modified" mark is gone by design (owner decision, 2026-11-19):** the earlier plugin appended " *" to the program name while a flag (`programModified`, set by a chunk restore or a real parameter change) was set, so that the host kept showing a mark after it had dropped its own; the owner found the mark misleading, and it was removed. No name the host reads carries a mark any more: `effGetProgramName`, `effGetProgramNameIndexed` (every `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PROGRAM_GROUPS` mode), the name in the chunk (`mpcProgramName`, always the preset's own name), the `.xpl` names and the content pack are checked to have no trailing "*" (`shell-mpc/tests/programs_test.cpp`, `shell-mpc/tests/play_host.cpp`, `scripts/tests/test_gen_xpl_presets.py`). The flag is gone with it; the only thing left is `PluginInstance::programNameRefreshDue`, set by a chunk restore, which makes the display thread call `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` once so that the host reads the plain name again (the call that drops the host's own "*", measured above). A project saved by an earlier version with "Name *" loads as "Name": `stripModifiedMark` (`shell-mpc/program_list.h`) cuts a trailing "*" and the spaces before it from the name read out of the chunk (`programNameFromChunk`) and from the name `resolveProgramInView` looks up. `effSetProgramName` is not supported (returns 0), so no name comes in by that route. Whether the host puts its own "*" after a parameter edit (it does not, after the call above, once the name was read again) was not re-measured without the plugin mark. What the host does with the project besides showing the text (a save prompt) was not observable without closing Sbtest and is not known. The knob displays of the XL were not measured. A target that changes by another route (its own page, a preset, automation, the plugin's own touch screen) is reported on its Q in the same way, and a host write of a Q makes the display thread report the REAL parameter too (its own page follows) but not the Q back to the host that wrote it; a value is reported only when it differs from what the host last heard (tolerance one millionth), so a host that sets every reported value back causes no loop;
- the Q proxies are NOT saved in the chunk (nor is the group) and are not offered for automation (`effCanBeAutomated` is 0). A host may still record an automation on a Q proxy: it replays into whatever group is active at the time, so what it moves is not what was recorded. Record on the real parameter instead (its own page, or the parameter of the plugin-drawn page), which is stable.
- Audio thread rule: `getParameter` and `setParameter` of a Q only use lock-free atomics (a read of the target, one step): no allocation, no lock (`shell-mpc/tests/q_router_host.cpp` counts every `operator new` on those paths and runs under ThreadSanitizer).

**The mapping of the 16 slots (one rule for both devices).** A page exposes its Q GROUPS in reading order (a group is four values: a block of a screen, a strip, a row, a bank). Slots 1-4 (Q 1 .. Q 4) are the ACTIVE group, exactly as before; slots 5-8, 9-12 and 13-16 are the next groups of the page in reading order after the active one, wrapping around, each group once; a page with fewer than four groups leaves the remaining slots without a target (name "Q n", value 0, writes ignored). `QRouter::mapGroups` and `QRouter::blockOfGroup` (`q_router.h`) are the rule, `overlay/q_publish.h` (`QSlots::publish`) is how a page hands its groups over; the router is told sixteen slots (`IInstanceView::setQGroup (targets, n)`, `n` up to 16; a page that only knows one group passes four, so Q 5 .. Q 16 have no target). The MPC XL has 4 x 4 knobs with a display each, so all four blocks are reachable at once, the host's own numbering (the active group on Q-Links 1-4 wherever the host puts them) is kept and no rows are remapped. The Key 61 has four knobs and a bank button (`Bank Direction "Row"`: bank 1 = Q-Link 1-4 = the active group, banks 2-4 = Q-Links 5-8, 9-12, 13-16 = the other groups). Every proxy reports its target's name, unit, value text and properties (the XL's displays and the Key 61's pop-up show the real names for all 16). The groups of each page (the "list"):
| page | groups in reading order (slot block 1 = the active one) |
|---|---|
| Play: oscillator panel (compact view) | Basic (one group: Q 5 .. 16 empty) |
| Play: envelope and LFO panels | the compact views have one group each (ADSR, RATE); the full screens publish all their blocks (rows Env, LFO below) |
| Osc A / B / C / Sub / Noise, full screen | the blocks of its panel in the order of the screen: Basic, Type / Tune, Unison / Playback / Random, Warp (as many as the oscillator has) |
| Osc detail pages (Unison, Grain, Envelope) | the groups of the detail panel |
| Osc / Filter modulation list (rows) | one group per row: depth, polarity, on / off of the row |
| Osc route popup, Filter output popup | one group |
| Mixer | the ten strips left to right (Sub, Osc 1 .. 3, Noise, Filter 1, 2, Bus 1, Bus 2, Main): Level, Pan, Bus 1, Bus 2 of a strip; the selected strip is the active one, the next three (wrapping) follow |
| Filter | MAIN, TRACK, OUT (and FORMANT with the Formant model) |
| FX | the rows of four values of the open effect that are on the editor's page (the first and the second row, or three rows without a display) |
| Mod: matrix | one group (the selected slot: row, source, target, amount) |
| Mod: slot page | AUXILIARY SOURCE, SHAPE, TIME |
| Mod / Macros targets list | one group per row |
| Macros | the two banks: MACROS 1-4, MACROS 5-8 (the shown bank is active, the other one is on Q 5 .. 8) |
| Master | the groups of the side that is up: OUTPUT, PITCH, LINK, VOICES, VOICE OPTS, GLIDE (main side) or SCALE, TUNING, MPE, MPE Y (tuning side) |
| Voice | STEP, RANDOM, RAND 10x, SCALING, TARGETS, NOISE |
| Env | ADSR, SHAPE, TIME (`EnvUi::publishQGroup`: three groups, Q 13 .. 16 empty) |
| LFO | RATE, TIMING (HZ / BPM / KEY list, PHASE, DELAY, MODE: the reading order of the block), MOTION (DIRECTION, SWING, STEREO, MONO), KEY FOLLOW (only in the key follow sync), SHAPE (SMOOTH, HOST); the random LFO: RANDOM (the style grid, RATE, HZ / BPM / KEY, STEREO) and TRIGGER (MODE, a group of one) in one block, KEY FOLLOW; the editor: EDIT, GRID, TRIGGER (MODE, PHASE, BEND ALL); the targets / modulations pages one group per row |
Tests: `shell-mpc/tests/q_router_host.cpp` (the rule, wrapping, fewer than four groups, the notification count for any of the 16 targets), `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/qslots_host.cpp` (the published list of every page above), `play_host.cpp` (count, order, names, the sixteen proxies against the real parameters), `scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_overlay.py` (the Q-Link maps).

**Stepped targets are driven relatively (Q router, 2026-11-16, reworked in round 6).** WHICH parameters: `shell-mpc/q_step_rule.h` (`stepCountOf`) - every parameter whose scale is `stepped` (toggles and lists of any length: a list of 1249 destinations cannot be reached with an absolute knob of 128 clicks) and the integer steppers of at most 32 values (voices, polyphony, bend ranges, octave, length: ten parameters, listed in `q_step_rule_host.cpp`); the fine-grained quantised amounts (grain density 1 .. 64, transposes, note and velocity ranges, key tracking: 63 parameters, the count is pinned by the test) stay absolute knobs. HOW: a stepped target is not driven by the proxy's absolute 0 .. 1 (a toggle needed half of the knob's travel, a short list a third: reported by the owner on the MPC XL). `QRouter::hostWrite (..., stepsOf)` takes the distance the host moved its own value with each write and makes ONE step of the target per `kStepTravelDefault` = 6 / 128 (owner, XL, 2026-11-16: the first 3 were too fine; both devices; override: the file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-q-step-travel`, 1/128 increments, read once a second), in the direction of the turn; a change of direction starts the travel again; the ends of a list do nothing. One host write makes at most `kMaxStepsPerWrite` = 4 steps and writes the target ONCE (the audio thread may be the caller); a move of more than four steps is a jump of the host (automation, a far report), no travel: it only re-bases. The distance of a write: the host's previous write and the last value we reported are both candidates and the one nearer to ONE increment (1/128) counts; a write that repeats the previous one exactly is the first write of a new touch (from the report). So the rule does not depend on whether the host adopts our reports: the test `q_router_host.cpp` simulates three hosts (adopts at once, never adopts, adopts one poll late) and asserts that 30 increments are 5 steps on a list of 57, and a toggle that is ON when its page is entered does not flip for three increments. A new target in a slot (the page changed its group): the slot word carries a generation (`setGroup`), the stepping state of the old target is dropped, the baseline is the value told to the host for the new one, and a write before the host was told only moves the baseline (one detent never jumps a list: tests R1 / R2 of `q_router_review_host.cpp`, which also covers the torn read of the slot, F7). The Q proxy of a stepped target is a plain knob of 1/128 for the host (`getParameterProperties`): with the target's integer range the host quantised its own increments (MEASURED, Key 61: a list of 5 jumped four entries for six increments, a list of 20 did not move for fourteen single increments). **MEASURED on the Key 61 (play log, `ov26-results/`):** the host writes 1/128 per increment; within one touch (a burst, increments 20 milliseconds apart) it goes on from its OWN value although we report the target's position in between; at the next touch it starts from the value we reported ("the host adopts the report at the next touch"), e.g. report 1.0, one increment down arrives as 0.9921875; six single increments (one per touch) flip a toggle, a burst of six does too; a burst can arrive as one write of several increments. After a step the poll reports the target's position, so a list of 57 does not run into the host's 0 / 1 limit. **Status file (to calibrate a quarter turn of a knob):** `q_step_travel: 0.046875 (6.00 increments of 1/128, default)` (`overridden` while the debug file is in use), then one line per knob of the instance on screen that has a target: `q_knob_N: target T, raw R (x/128), delta D (x/128), travel A (x/128 of 6.00 per step), turned S/128 in all` - `raw` is the value the host last wrote to Q N, `delta` the move of that write, `travel` the signed accumulation towards the next step, `turned` the signed sum of every delta since the plugin started. CALIBRATION of a quarter turn: read `turned` of the knob (the line of the Q it drives), turn the knob by a quarter of its rotation, read `turned` again: the difference is the number of 1/128 increments of a quarter turn. Tests: `q_router_host.cpp`, `q_router_review_host.cpp`, `q_step_rule_host.cpp`, `play_host.cpp`. Not measured: the quarter turn of a knob in increments (the `turned` counter has to be read on the device) and the MPC XL (the write behaviour above is the Key 61's).

**Chips on the screen.** The ACTIVE group keeps the yellow frame and the chips 1 .. 4 on its members. The other mapped groups (the blocks 1 .. 3 of the rule) show quiet chips on their members (`QGroup::drawMapped`, and `drawIdle` on the pages that always showed them: FX rows, Master, Voice; the rows of the list pages `ModulationsPage`, `ModTargetsPage`): on a device with 16 knobs the knob numbers 5 .. 16 (block * 4 + position), on a device with four knobs the numbers 1 .. 4, relative to the bank (the Key 61's bank button then steps to that group). A group that is not mapped (more than four groups on the page) shows no chip on the XL; the pages that always had idle chips showed 1 .. 4 on every other group, and still do on the Key 61. **How the plugin tells the devices apart (`overlay/q_device.h`, set once by `Service::ensureController`):** the panel is the same size on both (1280 x 800), so it tells nothing; the plugin uses the NAME the kernel gives the device (`/proc/device-tree/model`, "Akai Professional MPC Key 61" on the Key 61, read on 2026-11-16) and the architecture of its own build: a name with the word "XL" means 16 knobs, any other name four, no readable name: the 64-bit build means 16 (the 64-bit module is the one the XL loads), the 32-bit build four. `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_Q_KNOBS=4|16` overrides it. MEASURED on the MPC XL (2026-11-16, by the main session): `/proc/device-tree/model` = "Akai Professional MPC XL", compatible `inmusic,acvc` / `inmusic,az04` / `rockchip,rk3588`, `uname -m` aarch64, so the name rule holds there (the exact string is in `qslots_host.cpp`). The fallback on the architecture for an unreadable name is a GUESS (not measured on any device).

**Measured on the Key 61 (2026-11-16, package `q16-pkg`, new unsaved project, `.hw-local/mpc-perf/overlay-pkgs/q16-results/`).** Q-Link 1 .. 4 in each of the four banks (the bank button, `Bank Direction "Row"`: bank n = Q 4n-3 .. 4n) on 18 views (Play 1 .. 3, Macros, Mixer, Osc A / B / C / Sub / Noise, Filter 1 / 2, Env 1, LFO 1, Mod, FX, Master, Voice), the arrival group and one more group a tap brings up: 512 rows, every one as expected (a knob with a target changed exactly the target the page had published for that Q, 237 rows are slots without a target that changed nothing). Facts the table showed: the bank of the host's Q-Link panel is kept PER TAB (a tab comes up in the bank it was left in), and stays when the tab changes; Q 5 .. Q 16 reach their targets through the knobs like Q 1 .. Q 4. The idle chips on the Key 61 (Master page, tuning side) show 1 .. 3 / 1 .. 4 on the groups of the next banks. The table needs care with stepped controls and with controls that change the page (a type, a mode, a row select): the second part of the table re-reads the published slots for every bank. Not measured: the MPC XL (16 knobs at once, the numbers 5 .. 16 on the chips, the XL's device name).

The slots are set from the overlay thread; a debug hook lets the host tests do it without a UI: while the file `/tmp/synthyboy-q-fake-group` exists (`SYNTHYBOY_MPC_Q_FAKE_FILE` changes the path) its first line, up to sixteen table indices (-1 for none), is the slots of every instance in the process; it is applied when the line changes (`PluginInstance::qFakeGroup`, display thread).

**Which page carries the full-screen UI (decision and evidence).** The wish was a page of its own for the full-screen oscillator ("Osc Full"): no host widgets except the markers the overlay needs, Q-Links 1 to 4 = Q 1 to Q 4, reachable only from the plugin. The format does not allow it, so the full-screen UI is drawn OVER THE PLAY PAGE:
- Every tab of a skin sits on a function key (`fnKeyIndex`, `fnKeySubIndex` in `TUI.json`, `Tab` / `SubTab` in `Q-Links.json`) and is reached by pressing that key (again for the next sub-tab); the SDK documentation (`ui-reference.md`, "Screen geometry", the tab fields) describes no tab that has no key and no way to select one. A page of its own is therefore a sub-tab that the player steps through with the Play key (or a key of its own): it would be reachable from the keys, which is what was to be avoided.
- The plugin has no call to change the host's page: the host calls of the VST2 interface (`audioMaster...` in `mpc_vst2_abi.h`) are parameter reports, time, display update and the like, none selects a tab; `audioMasterVendorSpecific` is not documented for it and was not tried. Not measured on a device (there was no device in this work): "it cannot" rests on the documentation and the interface above, not on a test, and a host that the player does not see being driven by a call nobody documented would not be a design to build on.
- What the skin CAN do from the plugin is hide and show host widgets inside one tab through a parameter (the LFO tab shows Rate or Division by the sync choice, `additionalInvalidatingHandles`), and the Q-Link map is fixed per tab and sub-tab.
So: the full-screen UI covers the whole plugin area of the Play page, the plugin consumes the touches there, and the Q-Link map of all three Play sub-tabs begins with Q 1 .. Q 4 (`scripts/mpc-skin-extras/82_play.py`, `Q-Links.json`: the first bank of knobs = parameters 2703 to 2706), so the knobs act on the group of the page whichever sub-tab is on screen. The Key 61 has ONE column of four knobs and `Bank Direction` is `Column`: the banks are the columns of the 4 x 4 grid read TOP TO BOTTOM (bank 0 = Q-Link 13, 9, 5, 1; bank 1 = 14, 10, 6, 2; SDK ui-reference 8.2 / 8.3). On the Env and LFO sub-tabs bank 0 is Q 1 (top knob), Q 2, Q 3, Q 4 (bottom knob). On the oscillator sub-tab (the first one) the host honours only the bottom row of the grid and fills the rows above with its own program-mode parameters, so there the map has `Bank Direction` `Row` and Q-Link 1 to 4 are Q 1 to Q 4 (measured on the device with injected knob turns, `82_play.py`); the first version mapped Q-Link 1 to 4 (the bottom ROW of the grid), so only the bottom knob was a Q (the owner's hands found Macro 1, Master Volume and Macro 5 on the others: an unassigned Q-Link reads as the program mode's parameter). Bank 1 (Q-Link 14, 10, 6, 2, top to bottom) is the second row of the Env and LFO sub-tabs' knobs (slots 5 to 8 of the module); on the Play (oscillator) sub-tab it is free. The Env and LFO sub-tabs' slots 1 to 4 (the bottom row, Q-Link 1 to 4 until now) are therefore reached through the group: the UI of the Env and LFO thirds has to put them in the group when those thirds are in focus (the host widgets of the slots are still on the page, for the touch screen).

**The oscillator third.** On the Play page the left third has no host widget (no panel picture, no title, no selector) and the Play sub-tab no knob; the compact view is drawn over it by the plugin. The Env and LFO thirds are as they were. The older overlay view `PlayView` (`shell-mpc/overlay/play_view.h`) maps the five-instance selector to the engine's oscillators: A, B, C draw their cycle as before, Sub and Noise an empty panel with the title "SUB" / "NOISE" (it has no cycle picture for them); the panel titles of A to C are "OSC A" .. "OSC C".

**The rows of the Play page's three thirds (2026-11-17, owner request; `shell-mpc/ui/play_rows.h`).** The oscillator, the envelope and the LFO third have the same rows, top to bottom, at 1280 x 800 (a third is 620 px high, y 114 .. 734): (1) the top row, 56 px at y + 6: ONE selector component in all three, a `Stepper` with a list (the LFO's "< LFO 1 >" was the model): the arrows step, a tap on the name opens the list of all entries as a popup over the third (rows 56 px, the library's `ListPopup`, not a page of the stack; the LFO's 14 entries scroll). The oscillator's selector (Sub, Osc A, Osc B, Osc C, Noise) stands in the header row between the power button (56 x 56) and the full screen button (64 x 56) and the route button (64 x 56, not for Sub), about 200 px wide (272 px for Sub); it writes the Play Osc selector exactly as the five tabs did (`setPlaySelection (0, n)`; the source is no Q member, the Q groups are unchanged). The envelope's selector is an `EnvStepper` ("ENV 1" .. "ENV 6"): the small dot in the colour of the envelope stays (beside the name, and on the rows of the list) when the envelope has targets. The five source tabs and the six ENV tabs are gone. (2) The display, y + 70, **292 px high** (the wave, the envelope graph, the LFO curve: the same top, the same height, the same bottom: `playrows::displayRect`). (3) The controls line at y + 382 (`playrows::controlsLine`, the lane of the Q index chips lies above it, 16 px, then a 4 px gap to the display): the oscillator's Basic frame (type cells 56 px, a 16 px lane, then ONE 160 px row: the table stepper / OCT at the left, about 200 px wide and 76 px high, then PAN and LEVEL as 100 px wide vertical knobs with name and value under the dial; for the Noise the stepper row and the row of FILTER, PAN, LEVEL as before), the envelope's ADSR knobs (two rows of 108 px, side knobs, dial at most 96 px) and the LFO's SHAPE / RATE / RISE / SMOOTH block (its minimum, 232 px, is what sets the shared height: the display gets what the third leaves above it). A random LFO (not on the Play page) has two more rows and takes the room from its display. Tests: `ui_screen_host.cpp` (the header row, the selector, and "the three displays have the same top and height for each of the five sources on Play 1 / 2 / 3"), `ui_env_host.cpp`, `osc_ui_host.cpp`, `env_ui_host.cpp`, `lfo_ui_host.cpp` (the selector and its list).

**The header band of the displays (2026-11-18; `playrows::bandRect`, `plotRect`, `powerRect`, `routeRect`, `fullRect`, `labelRect`).** Every display of the three thirds has a HEADER BAND at its top, inside its frame, of one height (`kBandH` = 72 px: 4 px, the 64 px row of the buttons, 4 px) and the buttons stand in it, left to right: the power button (oscillator only, 64 x 56), the label text of the display (the LFO's "LFO 1 - SINE"; the oscillator's "OFF" tag and "content missing" line; the envelope has none), the route button (oscillator, not Sub; 64 x 64, its minimum size: at 56 px its two segments and the text would break the component's own minimum, so the band's row is 64 px for all three) and, rightmost in all three thirds at the same place, the full screen button (64 x 56, centred in the row). The PLOT AREA (grid, curve, fill, handles, the value bubble, the playhead and the cursors) is the display below the band with the same padding in all three (`playrows::plotRect`: 14 px at the sides, 10 px under the band, 20 px at the bottom for the envelope's time labels): the wave, the envelope curve and the LFO curve have the same top, bottom and height, and nothing of a curve is drawn in the band, so none runs under a button (the saw's peak and the end of the wave no longer lie under the full screen and route buttons). The oscillator gets the area through `display::Data::area` (`OscUi::plotArea`: empty on the full pages), the envelope through `EnvGraph::plotArea` (empty = the margins of the full page, unchanged), the LFO display gets it through `lfodisplay::Data::area` (`LfoUi::compactData` sets it from `playrows::plotRect` of the third's display; empty on the full page and the shape editor, whose `plotRect` is unchanged) and writes its label in the band. Tests: `ui_display_host.cpp` (the compact display: every kind of picture, a switched-off source, the live read-outs), `ui_env_host.cpp` (`testCompactPlot`: long times, no times, a selected handle), `ui_lfo_host.cpp` (`testCompactPlot`: with and without the lead strip, an envelope type, a trace, dim), `ui_screen_host.cpp` (equal plot areas, 56 px targets inside the display, the full screen button at the same place), `osc_ui_host.cpp`; the helper `dataOpsInto` of `ui_test.h` counts the ops of a scene that reach into a rectangle. Gallery: `play1_env_long`, `play1_lfo_lead` and the existing Play states.

**The oscillator third's corners, the grey look, the carousel selectors (2026-11-18).** (1) The power button (64 x 56, 4 px in) stands at the left end of the header band of the wave display, the full screen button at its right end exactly as on the envelope graph and the LFO display (the same size and inset), the route button (F1 ..., not for Sub) left of it (the header band of the display, see above; it stood in the bottom right corner for a day); the display's "OFF" tag and the "content missing" line moved away from the corners. The top row of the oscillator third holds only the source selector, like the other two thirds. (2) A switched-off oscillator is drawn in the INACTIVE look: `Pen::inactiveGrey` (core.h, one rule: every colour a grey of its own brightness at 70 percent, `Component::setInactive`, `QGroup::setInactive`), applied to the display picture, the whole Q block and, on the full pages (Osc A / B / C, Sub, Noise), the blocks and groups; the power button is red (`ToggleIcon::State::negativeOff`, the theme's negative colour) while off and in its active colour while on. Nothing is disabled: the controls stay operable (a patch can be prepared on a switched-off oscillator), as before. (3) The three top selectors are `Carousel`s (`ui/carousel.h`, docs/ui-components/carousel.md): the current entry in the middle, the neighbours at the sides (a tap steps, a drag steps once per 60 px, labels snap: no animation), a tap on the name opens the list; the three lists wrap, as the steppers did, so the neighbour of the first entry is the last. The envelope's carries the target dots (`EnvCarousel`). (4) The type of the compact oscillator view is a drop-down (`TypeSelector`: the PictogramSelector with `dropdown`; closed it shows the type's pictogram and name, a tap opens a list with pictograms, `ListPopup::State::icons`), side by side with the table / OCT stepper (200 px each: the table stepper keeps 140 px of text, so the two are not stacked), and PAN and LEVEL below as the side knobs of the envelope third (the same two rows of 108 px, dial at most 96 px): the Q block is two rows (type + table / OCT, PAN + LEVEL), the order 1 type, 2 table / OCT, 3 PAN, 4 LEVEL is unchanged and a Q turn steps the type through its list. The Noise keeps its stepper row and the row of FILTER, PAN, LEVEL. The full pages keep their type bar of cells.

**ONE Play page (2026-11-18).** The Play tab has no sub-tabs: Play / Play Env / Play LFO are one page, the view `play1` (the only view of the kind `play`; the layout file, the generated header, the skin pages and the skin generator `82_play.py` have no `play2` / `play3`). The three thirds' Q groups are mapped together (`PlayView::publishQ`): oscillator Q 1 .. 4 (type, table / OCT, PAN, LEVEL), envelope Q 5 .. 8 (ATTACK, DECAY, SUSTAIN, RELEASE), LFO Q 9 .. 12 (SHAPE, RATE, RISE, SMOOTH; STYLE, RATE, SYNC, STEREO for a random LFO), nothing on 13 .. 16. A touch on a third makes its group the active one (`highlight`): on the 4 knob device it moves to Q 1 .. 4 and the others follow in reading order (wrapping), the yellow frame marks it; on the 16 knob device nothing moves (below). The host's Q-Link map of the tab is Bank Direction Row, Q-Link n = Q n: on the Key 61 the host's bank buttons reach 5 .. 8 and 9 .. 12 as on every other page. The host PARAMETERS are unchanged (their count and order are pinned): the selectors "Play Osc", "Play Env", "Play LFO" and the 24 proxies stay in place and keep being saved (`mpcPlayOsc`, `mpcPlayEnv`, `mpcPlayLfo`, ...); the proxies of the envelope and the LFO have no host widget any more (the skin of the single page has none for any third), they are inert except for the old project's automation. A project that was on a Play sub-tab opens on the one Play page (the tab names are gone: the host falls back to the first tab of the key). The older code of the Controller that treats the Play views of a layout as sub-tabs of one stack stays (it is generic: it only sees one view now).

**Q groups on the 16 knob device (2026-11-18, one switch: `QGroup::setWideDevice`, `QRouter::wide ()`).** On the MPC XL all sixteen knobs are always there, each with its own display, so there is NO "active group": no yellow frame is drawn (the frame cell is not emitted), every member of a group that is on the knobs carries its slot number 1 .. 16 as a chip, all chips in the one style (the yellow Q chip, which is legible on every ground), the labels keep their normal colours (`setQ (false)`), and touching a control does not re-order the groups: a page of at most four groups keeps its slots (`QRouter::blockOfGroup` and `mapGroups` use the active group 0). Pages of MORE than four groups (the Master page with ten, the oscillator full pages with up to eight, the LFO full page with up to six, the lists of modulation rows, the FX editor's rows) keep the paging as today: a touch on a group rotates it to Q 1 .. 4 with the next groups after it; with no frame, the chips (1 .. 16) show which groups are on the knobs and a group that is not on them has none. On the 4 knob device (Key 61) nothing changes: the frame marks the active four, a touch makes a group the active one, the chips are 1 .. 4. Tests: `ui_cov_qgroups_chips_host.cpp` (no frame op, labels, chips numbers), `env_ui_host.cpp` (`testPlayPageQSlots`: both devices).

**One cut for a content name (2026-11-18).** A content name is cut ONCE: where the middle goes (`contentname::middleFit`) the text is drawn at the size the cut was made for (`Stepper::contentName`, the `content missing` line over the display, the notice of the effect page); drawn at a larger maximum the pen cut the end of the already cut text again (two ellipses). The three drawing sites measure and draw with the same size. Tests: `ui_cov_steppers_lists_host.cpp` (`test_stepper_content_name_is_cut_once`: widths, with and without caption, non-ASCII names), `osc_ui_host.cpp` (`testMissingContentLine`: the long name in the compact band and on the full screen).

**Text that did not fit, content names, missing content, Master quality (2026-11-17, from the layout lint and the renders).** (1) *Oscillator row of the Play page:* the table stepper has the whole left cell (about 200 px, 140 px of text) because the compact row has no list button any more (`SourcePicker::setCompact`; a tap on the name is the list / the browser as before, on the full screen the button stays); PAN and LEVEL are the vertical 100 px knobs with their value under the dial. The longest built-in table name ("Harmonic Series") is written whole at 13 px or more; there is no ellipsis for the 16 built-in names (`ui_screen_host.cpp` checks it). (2) *Names of files:* a file name is shown by one rule (`shell-mpc/ui/content_name.h`): the pack manifest's title when the content reference has one (`ContentFileRef::name`), else the file name without folder and extension, underscores as spaces; too wide for its box (`Stepper::contentName`) it loses its MIDDLE ("drum loop 1...20 bpm"), never its end. The layout lint treats an ellipsis in the middle of a text as a content name (intended) and an ellipsis at the end as a cut (a finding): the baseline stays empty. (3) *Choice labels* (`ui/choice_names.h`): the loop mode "Forward/reverse loop" is shown as "Ping-pong", the slice play modes as "Slice to end" / "One slice"; the style names of the eight classic filter models are the documented ones (`filterStyleNames`: 12 dB, 24 dB, Notch swap, Dual notch, Band peak notch, 24 dB plain / Shelf, Taps C .. E, Comb, Positive flange, Negative flange, Band comb, Band pos. flange, Band neg. flange, A-I-U-O, A-O-I-E, Vocal tract, Feedback, Inverted: docs/engine-spec/05-filters.md section 13; the Serum models keep the engine's names), and the style list pop-up is as wide as its longest name needs (at least 280 px) and as tall as its rows. (4) *Missing content:* `IInstanceView::missingContent()` (bits 0 .. 2 the file of oscillator 1 .. 3, 3 .. 5 the user wavetable, 6 the impulse of Convolve, 7 the sample of the noise) shows "content missing: <name>" in the warning colour at the top of the oscillator's display (`OscUi::drawMissing`, on the Play page and on the OSC pages) and in the header of the Convolve page right of the effect's name (`FxSlotEditor::notice`); the name comes from the state's content reference (the reference is there although the file is not); gallery state `osca_missing_content`. (5) *Master main:* the OUTPUT block has the Q group (VOLUME, SPREAD, STEREO, VOICE OVERRIDE: 120 px high) over a strip of three LED switches outside its frame (touch only): LIMIT (the plugin's output limiter, always a live control, shows the real state), and the QUALITY pair NO SMOOTHING (`quality.no_smoothing`) and OVERSAMPLE 4X (`quality.oversample_4x`), which have no Q slot because all ten group slots of the page are taken; the meters keep their column. OVERSAMPLE 4X is shown live; what it does per device profile is documented by the engine, the page does not say that it is ineffective on a profile. (6) *Noise page:* the generated noise has one grid in the bottom band: the TUNE block (PITCH, FINE / KEY TRACK) and the NOISE block (KIND, STEREO / ENV 1) side by side with the same top, height and rows (the display has the whole width above them, as on the sub page); the noise from a sample keeps the older arrangement (tune block at the right of the display, SOURCE and PLAYBACK blocks below).

**States of the plugin-drawn pages.** Per plugin instance (a slot of the overlay), `UiStack` (`shell-mpc/overlay/ui_stack.h`, held by the `Controller`): main (the compact views on the Play page) -> full(element) -> detail(page) -> detail ..., up to eight pages (one full page and seven detail pages; the page beyond the limit is refused). `Controller::openFull (slot, element)` (from main or from another full page, which replaces the stack), `openDetail (slot, page)` (only above a full page or another detail page), `back (slot)` (one page; the full page returns to main), `closeAll (slot)`. The ids are the UI's. The whole stack of an instance is closed when the page detector reports that the instance's Play page is no longer on screen: no view of the skin readable on screen, a view that is not a Play sub-tab (the player pressed Mixer, ...), or the page of ANOTHER instance of the track on screen; coming back always shows main. Not a reason to close: another sub-tab of the Play page (the stack stays), an instance picture that is blank or unreadable for a moment (the page is there, the instance is not told yet), a framebuffer that cannot be read, a sleeping screen, an ambiguous rotation. The stack is not saved anywhere and a slot given to a new instance starts in main. The views get it in `ViewInput::ui` (`shell-mpc/overlay/view.h`; the views of today ignore it) and the status file has `ui_state` (main, full, detail), `ui_depth` and `ui_auto_closes` (how many stacks were closed by a page that left). Tests: `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/controller_host.cpp` (the stack, the closing rules, two instances, a reused slot, the Q group handed to the instance), `shell-mpc/tests/q_router_host.cpp`, `shell-mpc/tests/play_host.cpp` (the Q proxies in the plugin, 2707 parameters), `scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_overlay.py` (the skin).

**Not done here (the plumbing step).** The rest is in "Oscillator UI".

## Oscillator UI

The oscillator of the Play page is drawn by the plugin (overlay build only): the compact view in the left third, the full screen over the whole Play tab, detail pages and the route popup, all from the UI library (`shell-mpc/ui/`, "UI components") and wired to the real engine parameters. The code is `shell-mpc/overlay/osc_ui.h` (`OscUi`, pure, tested on the host by `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/osc_ui_host.cpp`), held by the Play view (`play_view.h`), driven by the controller (`controller.h`).

**How it is put together.**
- The Play views (`play1` .. `play3`) now have the region of the whole Play tab (x 0, y 110, 1280 x 628; `IView::regionOverride`, the layout file's region stays what the skin generator and the markers use). The picture is one scene (`kKindPlay`, the same ops for the GPU helper and, through `scene_cpu.h`, for the CPU fallback): transparent where nothing is drawn; the Env and LFO panels at their old places (they are not drawn while a full screen is open); on top of them the oscillator UI. The markers and the instance picture of the view lie under the compact view, the selector and the LFO panel, as the old panels covered them.
- The state is the instance's `UiStack` (main = compact view; full = `OscillatorFullScreen`, element 1; detail pages 10 unison, 11 grain window, 12 multisample envelope, 13 route popup, 14 modulations of a control). Opening and closing are changes of the library components (the full-screen button, CLOSE, BACK, the backdrop of the popup); leaving the Play page closes everything (the controller, "States of the plugin-drawn pages").
- The picture in the display rectangle is the existing oscillator display (`OscCycle::computeDisplay`): the cycle of a wavetable (512 points), the min / max overview of a sample, multisample or granular oscillator (256 points each, with the loop markers and the grain window), the spectrum of a spectral oscillator, "NOISE" / "NO SAMPLE" for noise and an empty sample; the sub oscillator draws its waveform analytically (six shapes, phase).

**Opaque pages.** Under a full screen, a detail page, the popups and their backdrops the whole region is an opaque fill (`PlayView::build`), in the main state the oscillator third is: the host's panel picture, its Q-Link focus frame and the Env / LFO graphics of the host no longer show through (the composed pictures of round 14 did show them).

**The touch path.** `shell-mpc/overlay/touch_linux.h` (`LinuxTouchReader`, `TouchInput`) finds the touch node itself (direct, multitouch, `/dev/input/event0 .. 31`; opened read only and non blocking, never grabbed, never written), `touch_decoder.h` decodes protocol B for the FIRST finger (a second finger is ignored until the first lifts; a tracking id change without an up is an up and a down; SYN_DROPPED ends the finger and discards the rest of the packet; a finger that comes and goes inside one frame reports nothing) and maps the position: the ABS range of the device is scaled onto the panel's pixels (the Key 61 reports 0 .. 2048 on both axes for its 800 x 1280 panel, so the range is NOT the pixel count), then rotated with the inverse of `geometry.h`'s `uiPointToPanel` (a test checks that they agree for the four rotations). The panel size and the rotation are those of the overlay's discovery (`ITouchInput::configure` every step; nothing is hard coded). The overlay thread reads it (no audio thread, no allocation in the decoder): `Controller::pollTouch` once per step, about 80 times a second while a view of the Play page is on screen (`kTouchPollMs`), and gives the clock (`tick`) while a finger is down (the long press of a knob). A touch goes to `IView::onTouch` of the visible Play view; the application gets every touch as well (nothing can keep it from it), which is why the skin has no host widget under the compact view and hides the others while a full screen is open (below).

**The mouse path (a USB mouse as one finger; `mouse_input.h`, `mouse_linux.h`, `mouse_router.h`, `LinuxPointerSource` in `drm_linux.h`; tests `mouse_host`, `mod_ui_host`).** The owner could turn the knobs with a finger but a USB mouse did nothing on the plugin-drawn pages, although the host's pointer is visible over our picture. Now the left button is a finger, and the wheel steps the control under the pointer. **Reading the mouse:** `LinuxMouseReader` opens every `/dev/input/event0 .. 31` that has relative X and Y axes (`EV_REL` `REL_X`, `REL_Y`) and the left button (`EV_KEY` `BTN_LEFT`), read only and non blocking, NEVER grabbed (no `EVIOCGRAB`, no write: the host keeps getting every event), up to 8 at once; the nodes are looked for again every 2 s while a plugin-drawn page is on screen, so a mouse plugged in while the plugin runs is found, one that is unplugged is dropped (a button that was down ends with an Up), no restart; with no mouse nothing but that scan happens. While no plugin-drawn page is on screen the pending events are read and thrown away (`drain`: they must not arrive with the next page). `MouseDecoder`: `BTN_LEFT` down / up, a Move per report in which the pointer moved with the button held, a Wheel per report with the `REL_WHEEL` notches summed (positive: away from the user); `SYN_DROPPED` throws the half done report away; the other buttons and axes are the host's. **The position is NOT integrated from the relative movements** (the host owns the pointer and its acceleration): it is read from the host's pointer plane, the application's small plane (at most 128 x 128 pixels) on our CRTC that shows a framebuffer, found by scanning the planes the discovery listed (the pointer may appear after the discovery: a plane with no framebuffer is not on the CRTC), as the properties `CRTC_X` / `CRTC_Y` (signed) and `CRTC_W` / `CRTC_H`, `CRTC_ID`, `FB_ID` of the plane through a descriptor of the plugin's OWN with `DRM_CLIENT_CAP_ATOMIC` (a capability of that descriptor only: the atomic properties are not listed to a client without it, and the legacy `GETPLANE` gives no position; the application's descriptor never gets a capability). One `OBJ_GETPROPERTIES` call per read; the position is read when a button or wheel event arrives and, with the button held, at most once per picture (`Timing::frameEveryMs`, 33 milliseconds; 60 Hz on a GPU path that runs 16.7), and a move that changes nothing is not sent. The plane's top left plus the arrow's HOT SPOT is the panel pixel under the arrow's tip, and `panelPointToUi` of `geometry.h` (the rotation mapping the overlay already has) gives the UI pixel; the touch then goes the same way as a finger's (`Controller::touchEvent`: where the host's picture shows through, under the Q-Link pop-up's cut or outside our region, our pages take no mouse input, exactly as for touches). **The hot spot** is a pair of plane pixels per rotation: `defaultHotSpot` is the corner of the plane where the arrow tip lies when the UI's top left corner is mapped through the rotation (90: the bottom left `(0, 63)`, 270: the top right `(63, 0)`, 0: the top left, 180: the bottom right); the table `measuredHotSpot` (`mouse_router.h`) holds what was measured on a device; the debug file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-pointer-hotspot` (`x y`) overrides both. **Measured on the Key 61 (2026-11-16, read only, a small tool of its own on the device: `OBJ_GETPROPERTIES` with the atomic capability on its own descriptor, GETFB2 and a PRIME mapping of the plane's framebuffer):** the planes on CRTC 37 are 33 (the application's primary, 800 x 1280), 35 (type 2, CURSOR, 64 x 64, `AR24`: the host's pointer, `CRTC_X` / `CRTC_Y` read 398, 577 with the mouse standing still), 38 (the overlay plane of the plugin) and 40 (free); no plane has a `zpos`; the host's mouse is `/dev/input/event2` ("HORI CO.,LTD. TAC mouse Mouse": `REL_X`, `REL_Y`, `REL_WHEEL`, `BTN_LEFT`); `event3` and `event4` of the same mouse (system control, consumer control) are not mice (event4 has `EV_REL` but neither `REL_X` nor `REL_Y`: the rule excludes it). The picture of plane 35: the arrow fills a 20 x 13 pixel box in the BOTTOM LEFT corner of the 64 x 64 plane, drawn turned with the screen (the vertical edge of the upright arrow is the bottom row, 16 pixels long; the diagonal edge runs up and to the right); its tip is the outline pixel `(0, 63)`, which is exactly what the corner rule gives for rotation 90, so the measured pair `(0, 63)` is the Key 61 / rotation 90 default (`kKey61HotSpotX`, `kKey61HotSpotY`, status `pointer_hotspot: 0,63 (measured)`). The XL's pointer (plane 40) was not measured; rotation 270 uses the corner rule `(63, 0)`. **Not verified on a device:** the whole chain (the plugin's reader, the click and wheel on a page) was not run on the Key 61, because the install of the test build was refused by the permission system in this session; the mouse's button and wheel events were not injected through `/dev/uinput` (it exists, `crw------- 10,223`) for the same reason. The chain is tested on the host with fake nodes and a fake pointer plane (`mouse_host`), rotation 90 and 270. **The wheel** goes to `IView::onWheel` -> the page's `wheel (x, y, notches)` -> `ui::Component::wheelOver`: the control under the pointer takes it as steps of its own, the same step as a hardware Q-Link step makes on it: a knob and a bar `1/128` of the range a notch (`wheelUnit`), a number field, a stepper, an up / down stepper whole steps (positive: next), a list the other way round (the wheel up goes up the list, the current item moves), the modulation matrix scrolls a row a notch where no control of a row lies under the pointer; a button takes no step, a finger that is down on the page keeps the wheel from it, no press is needed. Status file: `mouse_input`, `mouse_devices`, `mouse_nodes`, `mouse_scans`, `mouse_hotplugs`, `mouse_events`, `mouse_downs`, `mouse_moves`, `mouse_ups`, `mouse_wheel_events`, `mouse_wheel_notches`, `mouse_no_pointer` (a click or wheel with no pointer on the screen: no touch), `pointer_plane` (id or `none`), `pointer_plane_xy` (the plane's top left on the panel and its size), `pointer_ui_xy` (the mapped UI pixel), `pointer_hotspot` (the pair and where it came from: `default`, `measured`, `file`), `pointer_source`, `wheel_events`, `wheel_taken`. **Behaviour (owner, MPC XL, 2026-11-16, kept on purpose): the mouse works like adjusting the factory UI. A press on a knob and a drag turns it relatively (the same law as one finger: vertical drag, whole range over 200 pixels); a press on the arc of a knob does NOT make the value jump to where the pointer is, and nothing may be changed to make it so. The XL counted 22 presses and releases and 176 moves on our knobs with the hot spot 63,0 (rotation 270, `default`); the wheel was not tried there.** **Not measured / known gaps:** the arrow tip of the pointer picture on the MPC XL (the hot spot of rotation 270 is the default corner 63,0; the Key 61's (0,63) is measured: `measuredHotSpot` has that one row; the XL's row and its test are for the main session, how: `ov26-results/xl-plane.sh` shows the plane, the picture of the pointer plane is read as on the Key 61); whether the running host writes `MPC.settings` at the moment the option "Show Q-Link Status When Touched" is flipped or only on exit, and whether the XL keeps the file at the same path (watch `qlink_status_option` while flipping it); with two mice the gesture belongs to the mouse whose button went down first (the other mouse's buttons do nothing meanwhile); the scan of `/dev/input/event0..31` every two seconds opens only nodes it has not seen or whose stamp changed (one `stat` for the rest).

**Review 2 (the look of the pages, no numbers).**
- The display shows the SHAPE of a wave, not its band limit (`display::smoothCycle`): the first and last samples (the first one sits in the middle of a jump) are left out, the rest is smoothed with a short Hann window and kept inside +-1. The stack of the table's frames is drawn only when the frames really differ (`framesDiffer`: a single cycle table such as the saw has none), over the same x range as the main curve. The unison voices are lines around the main curve over the same x range, higher / lower and wider with the detune, as strong as the blend; one voice has none. The noise has a picture of its colour (`Data::Kind::Noise`, nine deterministic shapes), faint while the source is off (no word OFF).
- ONE rule for the Q index chips: a chip on the top left corner of its member, on the line above it (`Pen::qIndex`); every layout leaves a lane of `kQLane` (16 px) above each member (the gap between stacked members, the title strip of a block, the strip above the top band). The screen and composite tests check that nothing lies under a chip.
- Two knob sizes: the large (the dial over the name and the value) and the medium (`Knob::State::side`: the dial at the left, the name, the value and the modulation chip in a column at its right; the WARP knobs and the knobs of the top band). Every knob says its value with its unit under / beside its name (`OscUi::formatValue`; PAN, LEVEL and FILTER keep the formats of the compact view). The modulation chip of a side knob lies in the text column, not on the dial.
- Names on every field: the type bar fields have labels (FRAME, PHASE, RAND, XFADE, STEREO ...), steppers have a caption (MEMORY, LOOP, MODE, STACK ...), the unison box has VOICES over the count and a gear button labelled MORE, the VEL TRK toggle and the switches show ON / OFF.
- Detail pages are titled blocks (one Q group each, `PanelBlock` with a fixed first row for the steppers): UNISON (MODE, STACK / WIDTH, RANGE) and VOICE SPREAD (WT POS, WARP 1, WARP 2); the grain window; the envelope (TIMES, SUSTAIN AND RELEASE). The unison and grain pages have the picture of the oscillator above the settings, the envelope page its curve.
