- The modulations page: rows 72 px apart, the depth is an `HBar` over the free width (both ways from the zero, the value as text, a horizontal drag, Q 1), the polarity switch says UNI / BI, the switch ON / OFF is the opposite of the bypass (on = active), the control's value is beside the title, a hint when nothing modulates.
- The noise full screen is its real parameters: top band TYPE (the noise source) FILTER PAN LEVEL and ROUTE; the tuning block PITCH, FINE, KEY TRACK; on the bottom panel one cell grid with medium side knobs where non-applicable sample controls are hidden and their room is used by the others (not greyed boxes): in generated noise mode one NOISE block (KIND, STEREO, ENV 1), in sample mode SOURCE (KIND, START, STEREO, ENV 1) and PLAYBACK (RANDOM, LOOP, BOUNCE). **Layout of the Noise page (2026-11-18):** the page has the standard margins of the other full oscillator pages (the lane of 22 px above the header row and the blocks down to the bottom edge of the tab; the empty bands in a rendered picture are the host's 110 px above and 62 px below the 628 px tab, the same on every oscillator page). The bottom blocks are ONE row each, at the maximum height of a row (`KnobPanel::kOneRowH` 128: the title strip 38, the row, the padding 6 = 172 px instead of 216), and what that frees goes to the display (340 px tall on the tab instead of 296). Generated noise: the display has the whole width; under it TUNE (PITCH, FINE, KEY TRACK) and NOISE (KIND, STEREO, ENV 1) side by side, three cells each in the same row (same top and height), so the chips 5, 6, 7 and 9, 10, 11 stand on one line and no block is less than half covered (layout lint rule g). Sample noise: the display at the left, the TUNE column beside it (PITCH, FINE, KEY TRACK in three rows that fill its height) and SOURCE and PLAYBACK in one row under them. The parameters, the ids and the Q slot order (Basic 1 to 4, Tune 5 to 7, Noise 9 to 11 or Source 9 to 12 and Playback 13 to 15, each group in reading order) are unchanged. Tests: `ui_screen_host.cpp`, `ui_composite_host.cpp` (`TuneRow`), `osc_ui_host.cpp`, `osc_page_host.cpp`. The sub oscillator display keeps about half height across full width (no thin single row); the bottom control area has every engine parameter in three labelled blocks with medium side knobs: TUNE (CRS, ENV 1, MONO), PHASE (PHASE formatted in degrees, FREE RUN), and ROUTING (DEST, BLEND, BUS 1, BUS 2).
- The content browser: the list has a header bar (the way up and the path, `ListPopup::State::header`), the roots are written alike (SD CARD, USB <NAME>, PLUGIN CONTENT).
- The status file has `output_peak` (the largest output sample since the last status write), `output_peak_total` and `output_blocks` (`shell-mpc/output_peak.h`, one pass in the audio callback, relaxed atomics).
- The GPU helper's layer cache and the plan of the partial picture are `gpu/cell_cache.h` (host test `cell_cache_host`: every buffer age). The GPU clock hint raises min_freq to the lowest clock that is at least 300 MHz and half of the highest (a devfreq node with `gpu` in its name; the XL's 300 .. 1000 MHz gives 500); a helper that ends (the plugin died) puts back a level the state file says was replaced. On a GPU that is not of the Mali-T series the full picture rate is kept unless a picture costs more than the rate check's limit (`chooseRate`: a vblank at 60 a second).

**Host widgets under a full screen (decision and evidence).** The full screen covers the Play tab; the host widgets of the Env and LFO thirds (the selectors under the panels and the eight knobs of the Env / LFO sub-tab) would react to the same touches. The skin's visibility mechanism is used: every widget the Play pages have in the Env and LFO thirds carries `RangedEnabling/0/18/33/Parameter <Overlay Instance>` (`scripts/mpc-skin-extras/82_play.py`, `additionalInvalidatingHandles`): visible while the film strip frame of "Overlay Instance" is 0 .. 18. While a full screen is open the instance reports frame 24 (`instance_slots.h` `kFullScreenFrame`; the plugin: `IInstanceView::setUiFullOpen`), a frame the skin shows as blank, so the widgets are hidden and cannot be touched; the controller keeps the view and the stack for the blank instance picture of that instance (the page is there by design). This adds no parameter (2707 stay, the order is the fixed one). See "Observed on the device" for the check.

**The host's Q-Link status pop-up (the key, `panel_key.h`, `controller.h` `updateKey`, `qlink_setting.h`; tests `panel_key_host`, `qlink_panel_host`, `qlink_setting_host`, `controller_host`, `page_detector_host`, `q_router_host`).** While a hardware knob is TOUCHED (the pop-up opens on the capacitive touch, so also without a turn and without any parameter write) or turned the host draws a panel over the right edge of the screen: a black line two pixels wide at its left edge (x 1024 and 1025), the dark fill right of it, the four knobs with their names and values, over the whole height. Measured on the Key 61 (`.hw-local/mpc-perf/overlay-pkgs/ov22-all-ui-results/screens/panel-*`): the panel is the same on a host-drawn tab (Osc 1), on Play and on every plugin-drawn page; the host draws no side buttons and no shadow in it on this 4 knob layout. The host draws everything, the panel included, into ONE opaque primary plane (no plane lies below the panel), so our picture cannot be stacked under it. It is KEYED: it behaves as if it lay under whatever the host draws over the right part of the tab. The host picture under our page is static (the skin's hidden widgets), so it is taken as a reference (a 16 row by 220 column grid of the strip x 840 .. 1280) when the page is settled: both markers seen for 600 milliseconds, no write of the host to a Q proxy for 800 milliseconds (`QRouter::writeCount`, `IInstanceView::qWriteCount`: a knob turn brings the panel), AND the strip has stood still for 250 milliseconds (`PanelKey::consider`: the first look is a candidate, every later look must equal it within the tolerance, else the candidate is the new look and the time starts again; a pop-up that slides in or out changes the strip all the time, so a reference is never taken from a strip with the pop-up part way in, which would hold the panel's black line at that place and cut the picture there; a touch writes nothing, so nothing but the picture can tell). Every tick while a view is on screen the strip is compared with it (`PanelKey::measure`, a tolerance of three levels per channel); a column in which most rows differ belongs to the band, the first such column is its left edge, the picture is cut two pixels left of it (`ViewInput::panelEdgeX`; the Play view and every `PageView` clip there, the CPU renderer and the GPU helper alike, `kOpClip`), and right of it the host shows through. So the cut follows the panel as it slides in and out with one tick of lag, and there is no yes / no flag. Our controls take no touch right of the cut (`Controller::touchEvent`). A view that has its left marker and instance picture but not the right marker (the panel covers it) is still the page when the band's edge is at or left of that marker (`Detection::partialView`, `key_holds`). The reference is dropped and taken again when the whole strip differs (the band starts at its left end: another host page, a new project, another skin, the standby dim; `key_reference_retakes`), when a band stays for 300 milliseconds while BOTH markers are seen (the time of band and markers TOGETHER, `bandWholeSinceMs_`; until 2026-11-16 it counted from the moment the band first appeared, so a pop-up that had been up for seconds was judged stale the instant it slid out and the reference was dropped at EVERY slide-out), and on every change of the view (another tab or sub-tab). While there is no reference (a page just entered, a reference just dropped) the key looks for the panel's own black line in the strip (`PanelKey::findLine`: the first sampled column in which most rows are black; the line is two pixels wide over the whole height, which no page of the host has) and cuts the picture there, every tick, and the page is held while that line or the panel's look (`Detection::partialLook`) is on screen (`key_blind_ticks`), instead of blinking off for as long as the pop-up is up. What is NOT keyed: a host rectangle that is no band over most of the strip's height (a popup in the middle of the tab); the page's markers decide then (it goes). The GPU path (the framebuffer imported as an EGL image, the key in the composite shader) was not built: the strip is read by the CPU through the same PRIME mapping as the page detector (`drm_linux.h`) and the key is one number, the x of the clip. The option of the host that switches the pop-up off (below) switches the key off entirely: with it 0 no reference is taken, nothing is read, nothing is cut. Status file: `qlink_panel`, `qlink_panel_changes`, `key_active`, `key_pixels` (the width of the host's part), `key_reference_taken`, `key_reference_retakes`, `key_holds`, `key_blind_ticks`, `qlink_status_option` (`1`, `0` or `unknown`), `key_enabled`, `qlink_status_option_reads`, `key_cost_ms` (average and maximum of one comparison), `view_hides` (pages that went off the screen: a blink).

**The flicker with the pop-up (reported by the owner on 2026-11-16, package `ov25-all-ui`; cause found and fixed in the code the same day, host tests only).** The report: on the MPC Key 61 the UI flickered while a Q-Link knob was turned; with the pop-up ON and turning, it looked right for a while, then the right strip flickered EMPTY (our picture cut away, the host's empty background showing) and stayed so until the tab was changed (Play -> Macros -> Play), which takes the key's reference again. On the MPC XL the host shows no such panel (its knobs have their own displays). **Cause (three faults of the key, reproduced by the replays of `qlink_panel_host.cpp` before anything was changed: the old code showed 341 ticks with our picture over the pop-up, 35 ticks with the page gone, 3 spurious reference drops and 4 references in one touch-only run):** (1) `bandWhileWhole` measured the time from `bandSinceMs_`, the moment the band first appeared, not from when band and both markers were seen together, so after a pop-up that had been up for more than 300 milliseconds the first look at the right marker while the pop-up slid out dropped the reference (`k.forget()`), at every slide-out; (2) with no reference the key did nothing, and a touch alone writes no parameter, so the write guard (`kKeyQuietMs`) did not hold the next reference back: the page was held only by the key, so the pop-up that came within the next second and a half was left uncovered by our picture and, as soon as it covered the right marker, the page went off (a blink) for as long as it stayed; with turning (a write at least every 800 milliseconds) the reference was not taken again until the knob was left alone, which fits what the owner saw (the replays reproduce the blinking and the uncovered pop-up; a state that lasts until a tab change is not reproduced as such: a tab change takes the reference again, and the fault ended with the knob left alone); (3) the reference could be taken while the pop-up slid in (nothing checked that the strip stood still), so it held the panel's line at the place where the slide was, and the cut stayed there after the pop-up left. **Fix:** the stale timer counts band and markers together; the reference is taken only from a strip that stood still for 250 milliseconds; with no reference the line is looked for in the strip itself and the picture is cut there (see the key above); the replays (a touch-only pop-up many times, the page entered at every moment of its first two seconds with a pop-up coming, the pop-up already up when the page is entered, a long turn, the pop-up coming back at every step of its slide-out, the reference taken with the line stuck part way, the dim, the dim with the pop-up) pass: the picture is never left cut while the host shows its plain page, never left over the pop-up once the line is seen, and the page never blinks. **Not verified on a device:** the owner says he switched the option off on his Key 61, so the pop-up cannot appear there (the settings file on the device, read at 19:52 on 2026-11-16, still said `val="1"`, file dated 04:48: the host had not written the change, or it was made later than the file); the fix is verified by host tests only (`qlink_panel_host`), the first run on a device with the option on and a knob touched is the check. **What the pop-up is, and the user-side way around it (2026-11-16):** it is the host's optional "Q-Link Status" pop-up (MPC Standalone OS User Guide v3.10.1): it opens on the right side of the screen whenever a Q-Link knob is TOUCHED (not only turned) and shows the current assignments and values. It can be switched off in Preferences > General > "Show Q-Link Status When Touched", or by holding the Q-Link button and tapping "Q-Link Status" in the Q-Links window; the host stores it as `<VALUE name="ShowQLinksStatusOverlay" val="1"/>` in `/data/Settings/MPC/MPC.settings` (an XML file of JUCE properties; 1 on the Key 61 and on the XL when read; the XL shows no such panel in either case). With it off no panel comes up over the plugin's pages. **The plugin reads the option** (`qlink_setting.h`, read only, the file is never written): when the overlay starts and again whenever the file's modification time or size changes (one `stat` a second; the file, about 77 KB, is read only then). With the value 0 the key is off entirely (no reference, no cut, no comparison, no cost); a missing file or key reads as `unknown` and the key stays on; the status file has `qlink_status_option` and `key_enabled`. The host writes its settings at its own times, so a change made in Preferences reaches the plugin when the host has written the file. This belongs in the user notes of a release for the Key 61: the pop-up is the host's option, it is switched off where it is not wanted.

**The host keeps a Q-Link map per track (cause of the "foreign" Q-Link assignments, measured on the Key 61 on 2026-11-15).** On the plugin-drawn pages the four knobs of Macros, Env 1, Master, FX and Mod wrote other parameters (the Osc 1 parameters 24, 19, 20 on Macros ...), Play, Mixer, Filter 1 (Q 1 .. 3), LFO 1 and every SubTab >= 2 were right. The host stores a SNAPSHOT of the skin's Q-Links.json in the project, per plugin track (`.xpj`, gzip + JSON: `data/tracks/N/program/customisable/mapping`, one entry `"parameterIndex": "<tab-1> <subtab-1> <q-link-1>"` -> `"automationIndex": <parameter>` per assigned Q-Link, 363 entries in the test project), made when the track got its plugin. When the project is opened the snapshot WINS over the file wherever it has an entry (a Q-Link it leaves free takes the value of Q-Links.json). The test project (`Sbtest`) was made with the FIRST skin (one tab per page, SubTab 1 only: Tab 2 was Osc 1, Tab 6 Osc 3, Tab 10 Master ...), so its snapshot is exactly that skin's Q-Links.json (checked entry by entry: 363 = 363), and every (Tab, SubTab 1) of today's numbering found the old map: Macros (Tab 2) the Osc 1 parameters on Q-Links 1 .. 3 and 5 .. 16 (only Q-Link 4 was free, so only Q 4 worked), Filter 1 (Tab 5) only on 9 .. 16 (free 1 .. 8: Q 1 .. 4 right), Play (Tab 1) only on 5 .. 16. SubTab 2 and up have no snapshot entry, hence clean. What did NOT matter (each tried on the device): the content of Q-Links.json (a file of 16 proxies, an empty map, a file for the product name or the layout name), TUI.json (widgets removed, bounds removed, a tab renamed), the plugin binary (the first one gave the same), `version.xml` (the plugin version; a higher `uiversion` makes the host refuse the plugin: "Please update MPC 3 Software"), a restart, the order of the tabs visited, the bank button. **A NEW project with SynthyBoy inserted: every page right in every bank** (Macros: the four Q proxies on all four banks; Filter 1: Q 1 .. 4). So this is not a defect of the skin or the plugin. Consequences: (1) the Q-Link table of a skin is tested in a NEW project (`.hw-local/mpc-tools/` `newproj` steps: Select Project > NEW PROJECT, the instrument list page 2, SynthyBoy, EDIT INSTRUMENT; never saved), never in a project made with an earlier layout; (2) a project that holds a track made with an older layout keeps the old map for that track (re-insert the plugin on a track to get the new one); (3) `scripts/mpc-project-qlinks.py PROJECT.xpj Q-Links.json` shows which tracks of a project are stale against a skin (exit 1; test `scripts/tests/test_mpc_project_qlinks.py`). Whether the host renews the snapshot for any change of the skin is not known (a plugin `version`, `uiversion` bump was tried: the version change did nothing, the uiversion one is refused).

**The Q path.** The hardware knobs are the Q proxies (above). `OscUi` computes the four targets of the ACTIVE group (the library's `QGroup`: the compact view's Basic group; on the full screen Basic, Type, Tune, Unison / Playback / Random and Warp, whichever the last touch or tap moved the frame to; the active group of a detail page; the route popup's [target, balance, BUS 1, BUS 2]; the modulations page's [depth, polarity, bypass] of the touched row = `modNN.amount`, `.bipolar`, `.bypass` of its slot) and hands them to the instance (`IInstanceView::setQGroup`) whenever they change (a tap on a group, a view or page change, an instance or type change). On the Env and LFO sub-tabs the Q-Links 1 .. 4 are Q 1 .. Q 4 too (skin), so the Env and LFO views (`EnvUi`, `LfoUi`) publish the group of their own page there; the Play view has no group of its own for them (the code that did was unreachable: the three Play views have the highlight 1, 2 and 3, and was removed).

**Writing and reading.** A `Change` of a component carries the id the owner gave; `OscUi` finds the binding of the id (key suffix of the engine table + a type) and writes through `IInstanceView::writeParam` (normalised; the plugin: `PluginInstance::uiWrite` = the host's own `writeRealParam`, then the display thread tells the host with `audioMasterAutomate`). Every `update` reads every parameter of the visible controls back (host automation, a preset, the host's own widgets show on the next frame). The selector of the compact view writes "Play Osc" (`setPlaySelection`, saved in the chunk and followed by the proxies).

**Modulation.** For each knob and number field the 64 slots of the matrix are scanned (`modsOf`: the slots whose `source` is set and whose `destination` is the knob's parameter, read with the parameter mirror; not on the audio thread; refreshed ten times a second and after every write): source id = the matrix source choice (envelope n = n, LFO n = 6 + n up to 8, LFO 9 = 33, LFO 10 = 34), depth = `modNN.amount` / 100, polarity = `modNN.bipolar`, bypass = `modNN.bypass`. The selected source is the Play page's LFO or envelope selector, whichever was set last (the plugin keeps it: `playLastSelector`; before any is touched the LFO). A drag on the knob's tab writes the depth, a long press opens the modulations page (depth, polarity, bypass, remove = the slot's source, destination and amount to 0, and the add row: the first free slot gets destination = the knob, source = the offered one, amount 0, polarity bipolar for a parameter with a negative range).

**The mapping (UI value -> engine key; A, B, C are `osc1`, `osc2`, `osc3`; every key and its range checked against `core/src/param_table.cpp` and `docs/params.json`).**

| UI control | Engine key | Conversion |
|---|---|---|
| on / off (all instances) | `<p>.on` | toggle |
| route button, popup target | `<p>.destination` | the seven choices (Filter 1, Filter 2, Filters 1+2, Effects only = M, Direct out, Filter blend, None); the button shows F1, F2, F12, M, DIR, BAL, OFF and the two filter segments |
| popup balance (F1 F2) | `<p>.filter_blend` | knob; enabled only for "Filter blend" |
| popup BUS 1, BUS 2 | `<p>.bus1_send`, `.bus2_send` | knobs |
| type pictograms (A, B, C) | `oscN.type` | Wavetable 0, Multisample 4, Granular 2; Sample (1) and Spectral (3) are the engine's, only on the host's own pages: no pictogram is lit, the full screen shows the type name, an empty type bar and an empty bottom band |
| waveform pictograms (Sub) | `sub.waveform` | six choices |
| noise source stepper (Noise Q 1) | `noise.sample` | the nine noise sources (NOT `noise.type`) |
| source stepper (A, B, C wavetable) | `oscN.wavetable`, and the table FILE of the content | the name of the built-in table (the engine's choice labels, 15), or the title of the file table when the oscillator plays one; the dropdown button and a tap on the name open the **wavetable browser** (below) on the full screen and on the compact view (the full screen opens first); the outer quarters step the 15 choices, or the files of the category of a file table |
| source stepper (A, B, C granular and multisample) | content, no parameter | the name of the loaded file ("NO SAMPLE", "LOADING ...", "LOAD FAILED"); the name and the dropdown open the content browser (below), from the compact view too (it opens the full screen first) |
| PAN, LEVEL | `<p>.pan`, `<p>.level` | knobs (PAN bipolar) |
| Sub OCT | `sub.octave` | -4 .. 4 |
| Noise FILTER | `noise.filter` | knob, bipolar |
| OCT, SEM (A, B, C) | `oscN.transpose` | OCT = whole steps of 12 semitones (-4 .. +4), SEM = the remainder (-11 .. +11), transpose = 12 * OCT + SEM; each write keeps the other part, the result is clamped to +-48. In the Tune Q group Q 1 and Q 2 are VIEWS of the transpose (`QRouter::kOct`, `kSem`: the target carries the view in the bits above 16), so they no longer do the same |
| FIN | `oscN.tune` | cents -100 .. 100 |
| CRS | `oscN.pitch_coarse` (Sub: `sub.tune`) | semitones, two decimals |
| TRACK | `oscN.pitch_track` | toggle |
| frame number (wavetable bar, read only) | `oscN.frame` | shown, not written |
| phase | `oscN.phase` (Sub: `sub.phase`, shown as degrees = 3.6 x percent) | degrees |
| RAND | `oscN.random_phase` | percent |
| WT POS | `oscN.frame` | knob |
| TIMBRE (multisample) | `oscN.multi.timbre` | knob, bipolar |
| UNISON count, gear | `oscN.unison.voices` | up / down stepper; the gear opens the unison page |
| DETUNE, BLEND | `oscN.unison.detune`, `.unison.blend` | knobs |
| WARP 1 / 2 type, amount (a tap on the type's name opens the list) | `oscN.warp.type`, `.warp.amount` / `oscN.warp2.type`, `.warp2.amount` | stepper over the engine's 99 choice labels (0 = off: the slot is drawn dim), knob |
| unison page: MODE, STACK, WIDTH, RANGE | `oscN.unison.mode`, `.unison.stack`, `.unison.spread`, `.unison.range` | steppers (choice labels), knobs |
| unison page: WT POS, WARP 1, WARP 2 spread | `oscN.frame_spread`, `.warp.spread`, `.warp2.spread` | knobs, bipolar |
| unison page (granular): START | `oscN.sample.uni_start` | knob |
| granular SCAN, DENS, LENGTH | `oscN.gran.position`, `.gran.density`, `.gran.length` | knobs (exponential parameters through their own skew) |
| granular OFFSET, DIR, PITCH | `oscN.gran.rand_offset`, `.gran.rand_dir`, `.gran.rand_pitch` | knobs (PITCH 0 .. 24 semitones) |
| granular LEN RAND, PAN RAND, LVL RAND | `oscN.gran.rand_length`, `.rand_pan`, `.rand_level` | knobs |
| granular WARP menu | `oscN.warp.type` | opens the list of the 99 warp types (`ListPopup`); a tap on a row writes it |
| granular XFADE | `oscN.sample.loop_xfade` | milliseconds, steps of 10 |
| granular loop mode | `oscN.gran.loop_grains`, `oscN.gran.manual` | One-shot (both 0), Loop grains (loop_grains 1), Manual (manual 1) |
| grain window button, page: AMOUNT, AMOUNT rand, SKEW, SKEW rand, SHAPE | `oscN.gran.window_amount`, `.gran.rand_window`, `.gran.window_skew`, `.gran.rand_skew`, `.gran.window_shape` | knobs, stepper |
| multisample VEL TRACK toggle, amount | `oscN.multi.vel_override`, `oscN.multi.vel_track` | toggle, 0 .. 100 |
| multisample envelope page: DELAY, A, H, D, S, R, OVERRIDE | `oscN.multi.delay`, `.attack`, `.hold`, `.decay`, `.sustain`, `.release`, `.override` | knobs (milliseconds, exponential), toggle |
| noise STEREO | `noise.stereo` | number |

**Disabled, because the engine has nothing behind them (drawn dim, no reaction).** (WARP Var and the phase memory stepper are no longer in this list: see "Every oscillator parameter has a control".) the source of the engine's Sample and Spectral types (only on the host's own pages); the source dropdown of the compact view for the wavetable type (the list opens from the full screen). Found in the engine and wired: `oscN.pitch_coarse`, `.pitch_track`, `.unison.mode`, `.unison.range`, `.unison.stack` (read in `core/engine-own/voice/voice.cpp` and `osc/lib_osc.cpp`, although the capability table of `core/src/engine_caps.cpp` lists them as unsupported), `sub.phase`, the granular `rand_*`, `position`, `window_*` and the multisample keys (`lk_` indices of `osc/lib_osc.cpp`). Not verified by listening: that every parameter that is read changes the sound the way the label says.

**Every oscillator parameter has a control (osc-coverage).** The five engine types have a key each in the type selector (WAVE, MULTI, SAMPLE, GRAIN, SPEC: `oscN.type` 0, 4, 1, 2, 3; the Sample and Spectral types have their own full screens now, they were only on the host's pages before). The 143 parameters of an oscillator are all on the pages: `coverage_host` finds 143 controls and 143 Q members for each of A, B and C, and its table of known gaps has no oscillator row. The full screen of a type shows what a player reaches for first; seven detail pages (`shell-mpc/overlay/osc_spec_page.h`, data driven: a table of blocks and controls, one component lays any of them out) hold the rest. A page is a titled set of blocks; every block is ONE Q group of up to four controls in one row (reading order, the same top for all, so the chips of a group sit on one lane); a control that does not apply in the current state is hidden and its room is used by the others (a block with nothing to show is left out); values are under or beside every knob; a stepper over a long list opens the list popup from its name; the display of the type (at least 240 px) is above the blocks.

| Type | Reached from | Page | Blocks (controls) |
|---|---|---|---|
| WAVE | type bar MAP | FRAME MAP | FRAME REMAP (on, curve slot, rise, fall: the last three while the remap is on); SPECTRAL MORPH (type, amount, spread) |
| SAMPLE | type bar REGION | SAMPLE REGION | REGION (start, end, root note while key tracking is on, key tracking); LOOP (mode, loop start, end, crossfade: the last three while a loop mode is on); LOOP OPTIONS (relative, link length, exit on release: while looping); EDIT (fade edges, normalize, reverse) |
| SAMPLE | type bar SLICE | SAMPLE SLICES AND SCAN | SLICE (mode, threshold in Auto, slice select in Manual); PLAYBACK (play mode, slice root: when slicing); SCAN (range, reverse); TEMPO (lock to tempo, length to BPM, length in beats while either is on) |
| GRAIN | type bar GRAIN | GRAIN ENGINE | DENSITY (mode, division in BPM sync, grains in Grains); LENGTH (mode, division, percent); SPAWN (pattern, max grains, jump start, reverse); RANDOM WARP (warp 1, warp 2); POSITION (manual, key track, crossfade); Y AXIS (axis, Y position while an axis is set) |
| SPEC | type bar FILTER | SPECTRAL FILTER | BAND (low, high, smooth edges, band after warp); FILTER (cut, mix, frequency shift); FILTER FROM WAVETABLE (use, table and frame while on) |
| SPEC | type bar SCAN | SPECTRAL SCAN | SCAN (manual position, key track, phase lock, transients); SPECTRAL MORPH (type, amount, spread); Y AXIS (axis, Y position) |
| all five | tune column MORE | OSCILLATOR OPTIONS (no display: the room goes to six blocks) | OSCILLATOR (on, env 1, smooth interpolation, pitch tracking); PITCH (mode, bend tracking, fixed pitch while tracking is off, transpose snap); PHASE (memory, legato, ratio source); KEY ZONE (low, high, fold, warp); VELOCITY ZONE (low, high, override); UNISON SHAPE (detune curve, blend shape, law, stack repeat) |

On the full screens the knob under the type bar is WT POS (WAVE), TIMBRE (MULTI), SCAN (SAMPLE, `sample.scan`), SCAN (GRAIN), POSITION (SPEC, `spec.position`); the unison box, DETUNE and BLEND and the two warp slots are the same for all. The old VAR knob of a warp slot (it had no parameter) is the warp's PHASE (`oscN.warp.phase`, `oscN.warp2.phase`), and the Warp Q group is now one group per slot (type, amount, phase). The unison detail page of SAMPLE, GRAIN and SPEC has START and SPAN (`sample.uni_start`, `sample.uni_span`). The PHASE MEMORY stepper (`oscN.phase_memory`) left the wavetable bar and is on the options page. **What the six options of that page do in the own engine** (`core/engine-own`, test `core/tests/test_own_osc_options.cpp`; with every one at its default the render is bit for bit what it was before, checked by a checksum): *Fixed Pitch* (`fixed_pitch`): with pitch tracking off the oscillator plays this MIDI note; 0, the default, is the oscillator's own C3 (Serum plays C-2 for the wavetable type; the default stays C3 because a changed default would change every saved sound). *Transpose Snap* (`transpose_snap`): a scale mask as Vital's transpose quantize: bits 0 .. 11 are the allowed semitones of the octave, bit 12 snaps the whole played note instead of the transpose alone; the nearest allowed note wins, a tie goes down; 0 is off. *Key Fold* (`map.key_fold`): a note outside the key range plays as the note an octave or more away that lies inside (a range narrower than an octave cannot take it: the note stays out of range and silent), the pitch follows. *Key Warp* (`map.key_warp`): out of its key or velocity range the oscillator keeps running as the source of the warps of the others, it is not heard and not in the mixer strips. *Phase Memory* (`phase_memory`, wavetable type): All Voices = PHASE and RAND for every note (the default); Contiguous = a note continues with the phases the previous note has now; Per Voice = every note starts with the phases captured from the previous note when the mode was entered (the first note after it captures its own). *Phase Legato* (`phase_legato`, or the global `voice.phase_legato`): a note that starts while the key of the previous note is still held takes that note's phases instead of starting at PHASE and RAND. **Not done:** Serum's default for a stolen voice (the phase does not reset) is not what the own engine does (it resets at every new voice); the engine's default stays.

**Empty state.** A type that plays a file (SAMPLE, GRAIN, SPEC, MULTI) with no file loaded shows the LOAD SAMPLE (MULTI: LOAD INSTRUMENT) button over its display, on the full screen and on every page of the type: one tap opens the content browser. The display of a sample draws the start and end of the region (the parts outside dimmed) and the loop range; the map page draws the remap curve of the slot (`osc_marks.h`).

**Rule for power switches.** A module's `.on` is the power switch in the header of its page (touch only: a knob turn that switches the sound off by accident is worse than a hardware step it saves). Where the module has an options page the switch is also its first member (a Q group) so that the parameter is reachable by hardware: the oscillators A, B, C have it on OSCILLATOR OPTIONS. The other modules keep their switches as touch only controls; `coverage_host` lists them (`sub.on`, `noise.on`, `filterN.on`, the effects' `.on`) in its table of known gaps as that class.

**LED controls and the transpose snap editor (osc-coverage).** The toggles of the detail pages are `LedToggle`, the short lists (up to 8 options, names that fit: remap curve, pitch mode, phase memory, ratio source, unison law, stack repeat, slice mode, scan range, density mode, length mode, spawn pattern) are `LedList` with all options visible (the title sits in the lane of the Q chip); the long lists (loop mode, fade edges, slice play, divisions, morph type, axes, filter table) stay steppers with the popup. A block takes the height its lists need (56 px a line; a list uses as many columns as its names allow). The transpose snap (`oscN.transpose_snap`, 0 .. 8191) is a `NoteMask` (`shell-mpc/ui/note_mask.h`): twelve LED note cells laid out as a piano (the five black notes C# D# F# G# A# in an upper row over the gaps between the seven white ones), every cell at least 56 px, a tap flips the note (bit 0 = C .. bit 11 = B), and a `SNAP NOTE` toggle for bit 12 (snap the played note instead of the transpose). Q: the mask editor is the Q member of its block (Q 1 = the parameter, a knob sweeps the value as a number, 64 steps a detent: coarse, the editing is by touch); the toggle writes bit 12 of the same parameter and has no Q knob of its own, so the parameter has one Q member. `voice.phase_legato` is read: it is the same switch as `oscN.phase_legato` for all oscillators, and Phase Legato now means (docs/ui-design/13-new-keys.md A86) that a note that starts while the key of the previous note is still held takes that note's phases (memory All Voices).

**LED switches on the main pages of the oscillators (osc-led).** Every on / off control of the Sub, Noise and Osc A, B, C pages is a `LedToggle` named by its function only (the dot is the state, no ON / OFF in the label): the toggles of a `KnobPanel` preset (ENV 1, MONO, FREE RUN of the sub page; ENV 1, RANDOM, LOOP, BOUNCE of the noise; OVERRIDE of the multisample envelope), the `TRACK` of the tune column (`KEY TRACK` on the noise page) and the `VEL TRK` of the multisample type bar. A switch takes the WHOLE cell of its Q slot (no 96 px cap, so its index chip stands where the chips of the knobs beside it stand) and its dot and name stand at the height of the dials of the row: ENV 1, MONO and FREE RUN of the sub page are on the baseline of BUS 1 and BUS 2 of the ROUTING block; on the noise page KIND, STEREO and ENV 1 are one line. Buttons that open a page stay buttons (MAP, MORE, the gears). The power switch of an oscillator is the power glyph (the rule for power switches above). **An oscillator that is switched off** shows the picture of its current content (the table, the sample, the spectrum) under a dark veil with a small `OFF` tag in the corner of the display (`ui::display::Data::dim`; `OscDisplay` computes the display once more with the switch forced on, `kind ()` stays `off`), never an empty display with the word alone; the noise keeps its own faint picture. **Warp slots:** the PHASE knob of a slot is a small dial (52 px) with its name and value at the right (the side variant with `Knob::State::dialMax`), the whole height of the slot, so that the names and the values of WARP 1 / PHASE and of WARP 2 / PHASE stand on the same two lines. **Play page:** the sub oscillator has no route button in the header (its routing is the ROUTING block of its page), the full screen button takes its place at the right edge; the five source tabs SUB A B C NOISE are one width and one text size (the size the longest name needs). **Mixer strips:** the PAN and send sliders and the destination dropdown have no box (the name, the value and the track, the name with its chevron over a hairline); the faders, the power glyphs and the strip blocks are as before, every cell is still 56 px. **Housekeeping:** the pictures the UI tests write go to `$UI_OUT`, else to `synthyboy-ui-images` in the system's temporary directory (`uit::outDir ()`), never into the working directory; the 79 pictures that a run in `shell-mpc/` had put into the repository are gone. `ui-screenshots/*.png` (the juce render test's output, restored after the tests by the earlier reports) is still committed: owner decision.

**The content browser (a sample for the granular oscillator, an instrument for the multisample one).** The source of these two types is a file, not a parameter. The name on the source stepper and the dropdown open the list popup as a browser (`shell-mpc/overlay/content_browser.h`, `ContentBrowser`): the first row names the folder and goes one up, then the folders (with a triangle), then the files, sorted by name, hidden files and everything that cannot be loaded left out (a text file, a FLAC: the engine reads WAV and SFZ only); at the top the roots the plugin offers (`IInstanceView::contentRoots`: SD CARD = `/sdcard`, PLUGIN CONTENT = its own `Content` folder, USB <name> for every drive under `/media` that is not an internal partition; only the ones that exist). It opens in the folder of the file that was loaded last. A tap on a file is `IInstanceView::requestContent (oscillator, kind, path)` and the popup closes; the plugin's display thread does the load (`PluginInstance::contentPoll`; reading, decoding the header, the content store, `engine->setContent`; the overlay thread never waits for it, the chunk and the content of the engine are changed under `contentMutex`): kind 1 (granular) copies the WAV into the content store (`user.samples`, content addressed, the root note from its name or the WAV chunk goes to `oscN.sample.root`); kind 2 (multisample) references an SFZ file where it lies (its folder is a pack of the store, the samples it names are read from there; the SFZ must lie in a folder of its own and its samples below it, no `..`), and a WAV becomes an instrument of one zone over the keyboard (stored with an SFZ the plugin writes). The source then says the name, the display shows the min / max picture of the file and the grains / heads, the chunk keeps the reference. What a failed load says stays for four seconds ("LOAD FAILED"). Not done: FLAC files (the engine has no decoder for them), instruments whose SFZ names samples above its folder.
**The wavetable browser (categories, packs, the player's own files; `shell-mpc/overlay/wavetable_browser.h`, `core/include/synthyboy/wavetable_catalogue.h`, `wavetable_file.h`).**

*What existed before (read from the code, 2026-11-18).* `oscN.wavetable` has 15 choices (`kNumWavetableChoices`, `core/include/synthyboy/params.h`, names in `core/src/param_table.cpp`): six one cycle tables (Sine, Saw, Square, Triangle, Pulse 25 percent, and "Saw Down" appended as choice 14), eight morph tables of 64 frames (choices 5 to 12) and choice 13 "Preset table" (`kWavetablePresetTable`). The built-in tables live in the process-wide bank (`core/engine-own/osc/wavetable_bank.cpp`), built lazily off the audio thread (section "Start-up time"). "Preset table" is the only way to play a file: the `content.wavetables` entry of the state (`ContentWavetable`: oscillator 1 to 3, `ContentFileRef` = pack id + path below the pack + SHA-256 + name, frame count, `core/include/synthyboy/content_state.h`). `IEngine::setContent` / `setContentStore` decode it into a `ContentSet` (`core/engine-own/osc/content_set.cpp`) on the calling thread (never the audio thread), and publish it with one pointer; the voice reads `userTable[osc]` for choice 13. The store (`UserContentStore`, `core/src/user_content.cpp`) looks a reference up as `<Content>/<pack>/<file>`, then in every `addPackRoot` directory as `<root>/<pack>/<file>` (the plugin adds `Content/packs`), and checks the SHA-256 when the reference has one. A reference that does not resolve leaves `userTable` empty: the saw plays and `IEngine::missingContent()` bit 3 to 5 is set; the pages write "content missing: <name>" over the display (`OscUi::updateMissing`). The oscillator never switches table by itself: the plugin's `applyState` builds the built-in tables the new values name before it sets them (`readyContentFor`) and sets the content afterwards. What loaded a table file by hand before: nothing in the plugin-drawn UI (the list button of the wavetable was only a list of the 15 choices; the content browser handled samples and instruments only); the Juce shells import tables through `attachStoredWavetable`.

**Which WAV files the engine accepts.** (one parser now: `parseWavetableWav`, `core/src/wavetable_file.cpp`, used by the own engine's `ContentSet::build`, the catalogue and the MPC shell's loader; the table editor of the JUCE shells (`wavetableFromWav`) and the Vital adapter keep their own reading). RIFF/WAVE with PCM 16, 24 or 32 bit integer or IEEE float 32 bit (also in an extensible header), 1 channel (a stereo file: the left channel is the table), any sample rate, at most 16 MiB (`kMaxWavetableFileBytes`; 256 frames of 32 bit float are 2 MiB). The frame size is 2048 samples (the common "wavetable WAV": N frames one after the other) or, when the file has a `clm ` chunk of the "<!>2048 10000000 wavetable" kind, the number written right after the three characters (64 to 8192; another size than 2048 is resampled to 2048 per frame, cubic). Frames: length / frame size, at most 256 (more: the first 256, as the engine always did). A file whose length is not a multiple of 2048 and that has at most 4096 samples is ONE cycle (AKWF style: 600 or 1024 samples) resampled to one frame of 2048 (before this change the engine took the first 2048 samples or zero padded); a file shorter than 16 samples, a longer one that is not a multiple of 2048 (a sample), a `clm ` chunk with a silly size, 8 bit or ADPCM or 64 bit float, more than two channels, a truncated or damaged data chunk, an empty file and a file above 16 MiB are refused with a reason (`not a wavetable file: <reason>`). The parser walks at most 256 chunks, checks every chunk size against what is left of the buffer, ignores chunks it does not know (odd sizes included) and never reads past the buffer; a file of the convention (2048 per frame, no chunk we use) decodes to the same samples as before, so every existing preset renders sample identical. The files are read through `IContentStore::readFileBounded`, which stops reading at the limit (a 200 MB file is never read to its end).

*The catalogue (`WavetableCatalogue`, built when the browser opens, never stored).* Categories, each with tables: (a) **Basic** (Sine, Saw, Square, Triangle, Pulse 25 percent, Saw Down) and **Morph** (the eight morph tables), the built-in tables under their own names ("Preset table" is not a table of its own: it is what a file table is); (b) every wavetable pack found in `<Content>/packs/<pack>/contentpack.json` (kind `wavetables`; a samples pack is not listed), grouped by the optional `category` of the manifest entry, default the first folder of the file's path (`content/factory-wavetables/<Category>/<file>.wav` gives the category `<Category>`), a file in the root of the pack: the pack's `name`; the row shows the optional `title` of the entry, default the file name without `.wav` (underscores read as spaces); older manifests without the two keys read as before (`ContentPackFile::category / title`, at most 63 bytes each, `schema/contentpack.schema.json`, `tools/contentpack/contentpack.py` keeps them when it rewrites the list); (c) **the player's folders** (below). Categories with the same name (any case) are merged between the packs and the folders; the order is Basic, Morph, the others by name, User last; the tables of a category are the built-in ones in their order, then the files by title. Limits (`wavetable_catalogue.h`): 32 packs, 1024 files per manifest, 512 directory entries read per folder (the first 512 the file system gives, then sorted), 64 category folders per root, 96 categories, 4000 files in all; `truncated` says a limit was reached. Listing reads names and sizes (`stat`) only, never the content of a wavetable file; the manifests of the packs are the only files read (at most 512 KiB each). A file that is empty or above 16 MiB, or whose name could not be kept in a state (`:`, a back slash, more than 255 bytes), is listed with its reason in the row and cannot be chosen.

*The player's folders (extending the set by copying files).* `SynthyBoy/Wavetables/` below each of the places the content browser offers: the SD card (`/sdcard/SynthyBoy/Wavetables/`), every USB drive (`/media/<drive>/SynthyBoy/Wavetables/`) and the plugin's own `Content` folder (`<plugin folder>/Content/SynthyBoy/Wavetables/`, which on a machine with the plugin on the internal drive is the way to add files without a card). A `.wav` file directly in that folder is in the category **User**; a sub folder is a category named like the folder, its `.wav` files the tables of it (one folder level; deeper folders are ignored; names with a dot at the start are ignored). No index file: the browser lists what is there each time it opens, so a file copied to the device shows the next time the browser opens (no restart). The internal "MPC Documents" tree is not a root of the plugin's content browser and is not scanned; copy the folder to the SD card, a USB drive or the plugin's Content folder. A preset that uses such a file stores the reference `pack "SynthyBoy/Wavetables"` + `file "<Category>/<name>.wav"` (or `"<name>.wav"`) + the SHA-256 computed when the file was loaded; the store finds it below whichever root has it (the plugin adds the drives as pack roots when a state names such a file, `PluginInstance::ensureUserWavetableRootsFor`, and when the browser loads one), so the preset works on another device that has the same file under any of the roots, and falls back as below when it has not.

*The browser (`WavetableBrowser`, `OscUi::openWavetableBrowser`, the list popup as the content browser uses it).* The table stepper of Osc A, Osc B, Osc C (the full page and the oscillator third of the Play page): a tap on the name or on the list button opens the list popup over the full screen (from the compact view the full screen opens first). Two levels like the path style list of the content browser, on 56 px rows: the categories (a triangle after the name, header "WAVETABLES"), and the tables of one category (header = the category name with the back arrow, a tap on it goes up). The list opens in the category of the table the oscillator plays, with that table marked (a built-in table by its choice, a file by its pack and path). Not done: a waveform thumbnail per row (the list component has no thumbnails and a file's content is never read while listing). A tap on a built-in table writes `oscN.wavetable` exactly as the stepper does and closes the list; a tap on a file calls `IInstanceView::requestWavetable (osc, ref)` and closes it; a tap on a refused file changes nothing (the row says why). The plugin's display thread does the load (`PluginInstance::loadWavetableFile`, under `contentMutex`): it reads the file bounded through the store, parses it with `parseWavetableWav`, computes the SHA-256 (`prepareWavetableRef`, `core/src/wavetable_catalogue.cpp`), puts `content.wavetables[osc]` (pack, path, SHA-256, title) into the content, gives the content to the engine (the table is built there; until it is published the oscillator keeps playing the table it had) and only then sets `oscN.wavetable` to "Preset table" (so the oscillator never plays the saw in between). The latest request wins (a newer tap replaces one that was not started). While the request waits or is loaded the amber loading bar of the pages shows (`IInstanceView::tablesLoading ()` is true for a built-in table in the making or a wavetable file load). A refused file leaves the sound and the state as they were; the display shows "not a wavetable file: <reason>" for four seconds (the line over the display of "content missing", `OscUi::updateWavetable`) and the row of that file in the browser says the reason from then on (`WavetableBrowser::markRefused`).

**The selector and the stepping.** With a built-in table the selector shows its name and the arrows and the Q knob step the 15 choices as before (the ends do not wrap), except that "Preset table" is skipped (13: the saw would play) while the oscillator has no file table. With a file table loaded (the parameter is "Preset table" and the content has a file for the oscillator) the selector shows the TITLE of the file, and the arrows and the Q knob step the previous / next FILE of the same category (the files that can be loaded, in the order of the list): the file is requested like a tap in the browser (the latest request wins, so a quick series of steps lands on the last one). At the first and the last file nothing happens (no wrap, the arrow is dimmed once the catalogue has said where the file stands; before the first step both arrows are lit); a file that is in no category (a preset whose pack is not installed here) steps the built-in tables as before. The way back to the built-in tables is the browser. The Q knob: `PluginInstance::qWrite` (the Q router's write of a table index) does not move the parameter while the oscillator plays a file table: it leaves the step in an atomic mailbox (`wtStepMail`, no lock, no allocation: the host's thread or the audio thread may call it), and the overlay (`OscUi::updateWavetable`, every frame) turns it into the file step. Host automation of `oscN.wavetable` and the host's own widgets write the parameter directly: they still select built-in tables.

*State and missing files.* The file choice is content state, not a parameter: the host parameter list (2719, order unchanged) does not change; the chunk and the presets keep `content.wavetables` with the reference. A missing file (not found, another SHA-256, refused by the parser) is the saw with the flag and "content missing: <title>" (`missingContent` bit 3 to 5): the policy of section 6 of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Tests: `core/tests/test_wavetable_file.cpp` (frame conventions, `clm `, bit depths, single cycles, refusals, odd chunks), `core/tests/test_wavetable_catalogue.cpp` (the catalogue from a fake file system: built-ins, packs with and without categories and titles, the manifest keys, user folders, the limits, neighbours, `prepareWavetableRef`, a user table through the state and the missing file fallback), `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/osc_ui_host.cpp` (`testWavetableBrowser`, `testWavetableRefusedFileAndCompactView`: the browser from the compact view and the full screen, built-in and file choices, stepping, the ends, the Q mailbox, the refusal) and `osc_page_host.cpp` (Osc A and Osc C), gallery states `osca_wavetable_categories`, `_category`, `_file_selector`, `_file_marked` in `ui_gallery_extra_host.cpp`.

**Live read-outs.** The engine hands the display more than the first read head (`LiveValues`, `core/include/synthyboy/readout.h`; written by the audio thread at the end of a block into fixed size relaxed atomics, no lock, no allocation; the voices are the newest eight, newest first): `voicePos[oscillator][voice]` (the sample type's read head, the head of the zone the multisample type plays, the play head of a granular cloud, 0 .. 1 of the file, -1 = not playing), `grainPos` / `grainLife` of up to 32 grains of the voice that is heard (where each reads and how far it has run), `subPhase[voice]` (the phase of the sub oscillator) and `noisePos` (the read head of a noise whose source is a sample). The display draws a line per read head (the newest voice the brightest), the grains as bars whose height and brightness follow the window of the grain (`display::drawLive`), dots on the wave at the phases of the sub, a progress line for a sample noise. Engine tests: `own_osc_read_outs_per_voice_and_the_sub_phase`, `own_osc_granular_read_out_has_the_grains`, `own_osc_multisample_read_out_has_the_position` (`core/tests/test_own_osc_types.cpp`).

**Tests.** `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/osc_ui_host.cpp` (every control of every instance and type has a parameter or is disabled; both directions of the values; the pages; the Q groups; the modulations; the drawing in one scene; the CPU renderer), the touch tests (`shell-mpc/overlay/tests/touch_host.cpp`, `run_touch_tests.sh`, with `fixtures/key61-touch-capture-arm32.bin`: a real `cat` of `/dev/input/event0` of the Key 61 while `mtouch` injected six gestures) and the controller test (events of a fake touch input open the full screen, drag a knob, close with the page). The cached cells, the plan of the layers, the damage and the part to paint for the age of the back buffer: `cell_plan_host.cpp`; the rate of the pictures (stride 1 / 2, the renderer name, the file): `gpu_backend_host.cpp`; the GPU clock hint on a fake devfreq directory: `gpu_clock_host.cpp`; the content browser and the loading request through the oscillator UI: `osc_ui_host.cpp`; `tour_dump.cpp` writes the table of every control (position, id, parameter, kind of value) the Key 61 function tour reads (`.hw-local/mpc-perf/overlay-pkgs/ov17-osc-ui-results/scripts/tour17.py`).

## Envelope UI

The envelope of the Play page is drawn by the plugin too (overlay build only): the compact view in the middle third, the full screen over the whole Play tab, the targets page, the list that picks a new target and the modulations page of one control. The parts are library components (`shell-mpc/ui/env_graph.h`: the graph, `shell-mpc/ui/env_screen.h`: the two views and the selector, `shell-mpc/ui/mod_targets.h`: the targets page); `shell-mpc/overlay/env_ui.h` wires them to the `envN.*` parameters like `OscUi` does for the oscillator, `shell-mpc/overlay/ui_bind.h` holds what the element UIs share (table lookups, writes, value text, the modulation matrix seen from a parameter and from a source). Tests: `shell-mpc/ui/tests/ui_env_host.cpp` (the geometry and the touch of the graph, the layouts), `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/env_ui_host.cpp` (the wiring, both units, the Q groups, the targets page, the Play view's routing between the elements), pictures: `shell-mpc/ui/tests/ui_env_demo.cpp` and `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/env_ui_demo.cpp`.

**The element and the stack.** The `UiStack` of an instance is shared by the elements: the full screen of the envelope is element 2 (`EnvUi::kElementEnv`; the oscillator is element 1), its detail pages are 30 targets, 31 the list of groups and parameters, 32 the modulations of a control. `PlayView` gives each UI the stack it may read: the oscillator UI sees an empty stack while another element's page is open, a touch goes to the element whose page is open (else to the oscillator's or to the envelope's third), and when a full screen opens or closes every element publishes its Q group again (`forgetQGroup`: the router keeps what the last page set). The envelope's third is opaque in the main state (the host's selector, title and the eight knobs of the Env sub-tab are gone from the overlay skin: `PLUGIN_DRAWN_PANELS` in `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/82_play.py`; their Q-Links are Q 1 .. Q 4 and nothing else).

**The graph (the Serum 2 manual, "ruler scaling below the envelope", Serum2-Manual.txt lines 5405-5470).** The x axis is TIME. LOCKED (the default, the LOCKED button at the top left of the graph): the envelope always fills the plot (a continuous fit, not steps), so a change of the times changes only what the ruler under the graph stands for; UNLOCKED (FREE): a horizontal drag along the ruler band under the plot zooms (to the right in, to the left out, one e-fold per 160 pixels), the scale is then the zoom, the envelope may run past the right edge and the handles beyond it are not offered; unlocking keeps the picture as it is (the zoom starts from what the fit was), locking again fits again. The compact graph (under 520 x 260 pixels) is always locked and has no button. The ruler is `shell-mpc/ui/time_ruler.h` (`makeTimeRuler`: round 1-2-5 marks in ms / s, or in beats when the envelope is in BEATS mode: the axis then counts beats, `envN.*_beats`) and its marks are also the vertical grid lines. A stage of 0 takes no width (the curve starts at the origin, attack runs on into decay, handles of zero-length stages share the joint: a touch on it or up to 3 pixels right of it takes the attack, further right the hold; drag right to give it time); a stage with time takes its share of the width on the one common scale but never less than `envgraph::kMinStageW` = 14 pixels (the 13 pixel handles of two neighbours never overlap; the scale that makes the sum exact is found by bisection, `EnvGraph::solveWidths`); the curve handle of a stage narrower than 30 pixels is not offered. Sustain is a level, not a time: the decay curve ends at the sustain point and release begins after a short fixed marker gap (16 px). ENV 1 (the amp envelope) lies faintly behind ENV 2 to 6 on the SAME axis (the same scale; its times are converted with the tempo when its units differ from the shown envelope's). The stage names are written over the handle in hand (the value bubble); there is no legend. The curve is the engine's own (`own::powerShape`). Handles (touch targets of at least 56 by 56 pixels): the ends of delay, attack, hold, decay (two dimensional: time and sustain level at once) and release, and a small handle on the middle of attack, decay and release. A dragged time handle is under the finger: LOCKED the picture refits at every step, so the time that puts the end of the stage under the finger is looked for on the refitted picture (bisection, `EnvGraph::onMove`); the release handle (always at the right edge when locked) and everything in the unlocked state follow the finger's distance at the scale the drag began with. A curve handle follows the finger vertically (`envgraph::curveForMiddle`); a double tap on a handle is its default. The value of the handle in hand is written over it. The graph hands the owner an `Edit` (handle, normalised time, level, curve; the lock and the zoom are view state of the graph itself: `kLock`, `kZoom`, no parameter); `EnvUi::applyEdit` writes the parameters. Tests: `ui_env_host.cpp` (locked fit to the right edge, minimum widths, apart handles, ruler, lock and zoom, the drag under the finger, beats), `ui_time_ruler_host.cpp`.

**Units and the live dots.** `envN.units` decides which parameters the knobs, the fields, the graph and the Q group use (`attack` or `attack_beats` ...); the sustain and the three curves have one parameter each. The ghost behind envelopes 2 to 6 is envelope 1 (full detail only). The running dots come from `LiveValues::envVoiceStage` / `envVoiceFraction` (new, `core/include/synthyboy/readout.h`: the fine stage and the fraction of every envelope in the newest voices, newest first, written without locks at the end of a block like `subPhase`); an engine that fills only `envFineStage` gives the one dot. The newest dot is drawn bigger. The reduced level of detail has no ghost, no stage borders, no area under the curve, at most three dots and no value texts under the axis.

**Q groups.** Compact: ADSR (ATTACK, DECAY, SUSTAIN, RELEASE), active on the Env sub-tab. Full screen: ADSR, SHAPE (A CURVE, D CURVE, R CURVE) and TIME (DELAY, HOLD, BEATS, LEGATO: the two switches, LED toggles drawn at the height of the dials beside them, are in the TIME block, not in the top bar, so that a value is in one group only). What the envelope modulates is written as tags in the top bar between the selector and TARGETS (a dot and the name with the signed depth in percent, "name +45", as many as fit, then "+n"; text, not buttons: TARGETS opens the list); there is no side panel, the graph has the whole width with or without targets. Every block on the page is a Q group of at most four members in reading order. Tapping any block (or any control in it) makes it the active group with the yellow frame or frameless chips; inactive groups show idle chips (`QGroup::drawIdle`). Returning to the page restores the default group (ADSR).

**What the envelope modulates.** The TARGETS button opens the modulation matrix seen from the source (`ModTargetsPage`: a row per slot whose source is this envelope, with the target's name, the depth, the polarity, on / off and remove; paged when there are more than six). ADD TARGET opens the list of the groups of the modulatable parameters, then the parameters of the group; the new slot starts at half depth (bipolar when the parameter swings around zero). A long press on a knob or a field opens the modulations of that control, as on the oscillator pages.

## LFO page and the LFO Editor (overlay build)

**The page.** `shell-mpc/ui/lfo_screen.h` (`LfoFullScreen`), `shell-mpc/overlay/lfo_ui.h`. Top bar: CLOSE (not on a tab page), the fourteen LFO / RND cells (60 x 56 px each, one bar), and at the right end EDIT (over the shape column) and TARGETS. The sync is ONE control, the HZ / BPM / KEY list (a LED list of three, `SyncSwitch::mode`): it stands in the TIMING block (the RANDOM block of a random LFO) and is Q member 1 of that group, a knob step on it is a step of the parameter `lfoN.sync` (Free, Tempo, dotted, triplet, Key follow: the stepped Q rule above) and a tap on a row selects it. TRIP and DOT are LED toggles under the division stepper in the RATE block (`RateControl::trip`, `dot`), shown only in the BPM mode where they act (`SyncSwitch` is only the logic: `nextSync (part, change)` turns a change of the list or of a switch into the sync value). The controls are not individually framed: DIRECTION is a LED list (Forward / Reverse / Ping pong, rows of 56 px, in the left column of MOTION with MONO under it, SWING over STEREO at the right), SMOOTH and HOST (under the shape grid), MONO and the random LFO's STEREO are LED toggles; the trigger MODE of an LFO (six long names) stays a stepper with its list, that of a random LFO (Retrigger / Free-run) is a LED list. One medium knob size on the page: RISE, SMOOTH, PHASE, DELAY, SWING, STEREO are the side variant in a cell of 114 px. The chips of TIMING and MOTION read top to bottom (the list and the knob beside it on one line, then the next control), the group frame is the block. The random LFO page has no second STYLE control: the style grid is Q member 1 (the group is frameless, its chips mark the quad); its RANDOM block is one row (RATE, HZ / BPM / KEY, STEREO, MODE) and the display takes the width. The LFO third of the Play page (`LfoCompact`): the HZ / BPM / KEY list is part of the rate control (its member), RATE, RISE and SMOOTH are the same dial size, and the quad is 248 px high (the room goes to the display). The modulation chip of a knob ("MAC 2", "LFO 1") is in the knob's label row, right of the name, and never covers the dial (`Knob::Geom::tabBox`); a number field puts its chip at the left of its label row. Tests: `ui_lfo_host`, `lfo_ui_host`, `ui_base_host` ("knob chip").

**EDIT opens the editor for every shape.** `LfoUi::openEditor`: an LFO with a built-in shape starts from that shape as points (`lfo::ShapeModel::fromBuiltin`), a DRAWN shape from its points in the content state, a DRAWN shape without points from a triangle. What the engine's shape model allows (`core/shapes`, `ui/lfo_model.h`, exactly what `ShapeData::evaluate` plays): up to 128 points, x ascending with the first at 0 and the last at 1, y 0 .. 1, one curve (power -20 .. +20, `powerScale`) per segment, ONE loop point, ONE smooth flag for the whole shape (the cosine S-curve of every segment), vertical steps as two points at the same x. So: sine = 3 points + smooth (exact), triangle, saw up / down, square, pulse, step up / down and random (8 steps) are exact; EXP UP / EXP DN / HALF are sampled by 16 segments (within about 0.01 of the engine's function). The shape parameter becomes DRAWN (12) at the moment the editor opens (the preset shape is replaced by its points, so what plays and what is edited are the same data); the original shape is one tap away in the picker. Not in the editor: curves between points other than a power per segment, more than one loop point, busses for the modulation of points (one source and one amount per value, as the content format has it).

**The editor page** (`shell-mpc/ui/shape_editor.h`, `ShapeEditorPage`; design and the table of the capabilities of the reference editor against ours: `docs/ui-design/lfo-editor-capabilities.md`, user guide chapter 5.9). Top bar: BACK, the seven tools (POINT, FLAT, RAMP+, RAMP-, SELECT, SOFT, BEND), SHAPES, MORE, UNDO, DELETE, MOD. The canvas (956 x 540) with the grid, the points, the curve handles and the marker; at the right three Q groups: EDIT = POINT (which point is selected, 1 .. n; with several selected "n SEL"), X (its time), Y (its level), CURVE (of the segment after it; with several selected: SCALE, the height of the selection) on Q 1 .. 4; GRID = GRID X (columns of the cycle, 2 .. 32), GRID Y (rows of the height, 2 .. 32) and SNAP on Q 5 .. 7; TRIGGER = MODE (`lfoN.sync_type`, the six trigger types, a list), PHASE (`lfoN.phase`) and BEND ALL (the mean curve) on Q 9 .. 11 (the next groups of the page; on the Key 61 the banks). LOOP and SMOOTH are LED toggles under the groups. The Q targets are the virtual targets 0 .. 7 of the router (`QRouter::kNumVirtual` is 10: POINT, X, Y, CURVE, GRID X, GRID Y, SNAP, BEND ALL; absolute 0 .. 1: POINT picks the n points over the knob's travel) and the two real parameters; 11 of the 16 slots. The page of the point modulation (MOD) has one group of two knobs, Q 1 = MODULATE and Q 2 = AMOUNT (virtual 8 and 9), published while it is open; the editor's slots return with BACK (details: `docs/ui-design/lfo-editor-capabilities.md` section 3). The grid is drawn as the canvas background (vertical lines at the columns, horizontal lines at the heights the points snap to), used by SNAP (a toggle instead of a modifier key) and by the cell tools (`gridX` columns, `gridY` rows).

**Gestures (touch; no mouse modifier is needed).** POINT: a tap on empty ground adds a point, a touch on a point selects and drags it, the middle handle of a segment drags its curve; a double tap on a point deletes it (never an end point, never the point the first tap of the pair has just added: a double tap on empty ground adds one point), a double tap on a curve handle sets the curve straight, a long press still deletes; DELETE deletes the selection. FLAT / RAMP+ / RAMP-: one drag across the columns draws the whole staircase or saw in ONE undo state, every column between two events is drawn (the height of the finger interpolated, snapped with SNAP; a fast finger leaves no gap). SELECT: a rectangle on empty ground selects the points inside, a tap on a point adds it or takes it out, a tap on empty ground clears; the selected points move together (clamped as a whole so that the order and the form stay), DELETE removes them, the fields X / Y / SCALE move and scale them. SOFT: dragging a point moves the selected points, or every point within 25 percent of the cycle, with a linear falloff (rainbow colours show the shares). BEND: dragging a curve handle bends all curves by the same amount. Every operation is one undo state (the state before it; UNDO restores it exactly, the loop point included); the model keeps its 128 point limit (a full shape takes no more points).

**The marker is the PHASE (read from the engine, `core/engine-own/voice/voice.cpp` `Voice::lfoValue`, `core/engine-own/env_lfo/lfo.h` `LfoCore`).** The voice does not read the stored loop index of the shape: the loop and sustain position of the trigger types is the PHASE knob (`lfoN.phase` / 360 of the cycle): type 0 Retrigger and 2 Envelope: where the cycle starts (START); 3 Sustain envelope: the run waits there while the key is held; 4 Loop at point: after the end the run jumps back there; 5 Loop and hold: while the key is held the run loops from 0 to there (LOOP); 1 Free-run: a phase offset (the marker is dimmed with the hint "FREE-RUN: PHASE SHIFTS THE CYCLE"). The editor therefore draws a marker at the PHASE (a vertical line with a 56 px handle at its top), draggable with the tools POINT, SELECT, SOFT, BEND: in a loop mode it snaps to the nearest point and the loop point of the model (`ShapeModel::loop`, stored as `loop_point`) becomes that point, in the START modes it is free (a point within 14 px takes it, SNAP puts it on the grid). The loop point is a bookmark that follows the marker: while the PHASE and the loop point say the same, moving the loop point (the fields, the canvas, a flip, a shift, UNDO) moves the PHASE with it; LOOP sets the bookmark at the selected point (and the PHASE in a loop mode); loading a shape with a loop point sets the PHASE in a loop mode. MODE and PHASE are on the page (and on the knobs) so that a loop can be tried without leaving the editor.

**SHAPES: the shapes on the card.** A list (`ListPopup`): FACTORY SHAPES (the 12 built-in shapes as editable points), MY SHAPES, SAVE AS ... (the name keyboard `TextEntry`), REPLACE A SAVED SHAPE, RENAME A SAVED SHAPE, DELETE A SAVED SHAPE (REPLACE and DELETE ask once: two rows, the action and CANCEL; SAVE AS on a taken name asks the same). A load replaces the model in ONE undo state, takes the grid the points lie on and shows the result for three seconds. Storage: `SynthyBoy/LFO Shapes/<name>.lfoshape.json` below each root the instance offers for the player's own files (`IInstanceView::wavetableSources` userRoots: the SD card, the USB drives, the plugin's `Content` folder: the same places and rules as `SynthyBoy/Wavetables/`), the file of `synthyboy::serializeLfoShapeFile` (the format the desktop and the other clients of the core read; `parseLfoShapeFile` reads it) plus two optional keys that the parser skips, `"loop_point"` and `"point_mod"` (the modulation of the points: `{"point","target":"time|level|curve","source":"<text id of the mod matrix>","amount"}`, bad entries skipped on load, `version` stays 1); a plain `.json` of that format is listed too. New files go to the first root that has the folder, else to the first existing root (the folders are made); the list merges every root (a name two roots have is listed once, from the first). The modulation of points is stored in `"point_mod"`, loaded in the same undo step as the shape. Files are listed, read and written on the overlay thread when the list opens or a button is tapped (`shell-mpc/overlay/lfo_shape_files.h`: `ICatalogueFs` for the listing, `safefile::writeReplacing` for the writes, `mkdir`), never in `draw`, never on the audio thread. The user library of the core (`UserLibrary`, `<userDir>/library/lfo_shapes/`) is a desktop concept: the MPC plugin has no user directory.

**MORE: the actions.** A two level list: EDIT (SELECT ALL, FLIP HORIZONTAL, FLIP VERTICAL, SHIFT LEFT, SHIFT RIGHT by one grid column with wrap-around, RESET ALL CURVES, CLEAR LOOP POINT), GRID (4 x 4, 8 x 8, 12 x 12, 16 x 16, 32 x 32, HALVE, DOUBLE, THE GRID FOLLOWS THE POINTS), COPY (COPY TO LFO ... a list of the other nine LFOs: the shape, SMOOTH, the loop point, the modulation and the grid go to that LFO and its `lfoN.shape` becomes DRAWN; OSC A / B / C CYCLE TO THIS LFO: one cycle of the oscillator as at most 128 points, `convert::frameToLfoShape`, the error below 0.2 percent of full scale; THIS SHAPE TO OSC A / B / C: the shape as one frame of 2048 samples, a WAV file `SynthyBoy/Wavetables/LFO <n> Shape[ k].wav` that is requested like a file chosen in the wavetable browser, a new file every time). The flips and the shift are exact (a power p of a segment cut by a shift becomes p t and p (1 - t); with SMOOTH on the pieces take the power that gives the same middle level).

**MOD: the modulation of points.** `PointModPage` above the editor: a stepper TIME / LEVEL / CURVE, the board of the modulation sources (a tap gives the source to the value of the selected points at half depth), AMOUNT (-100 .. 100 percent) and REMOVE; several selected points get one assignment. The model holds it (`lfo::Point::mx / my / mc`), `LfoUi::contentOf` / `modelOf` keep `ContentPoint::modX / modY / modCurve` (before this work an edit in the editor dropped the point modulation of a shape), the engine evaluates it (`core/engine-own/voice/own_content.h`), the canvas draws a bar along a modulated value.

**Tests.** `shell-mpc/ui/tests/ui_lfo_host.cpp` (the model operations, every gesture of the canvas: quick delete, step drawing with no gap, selection, soft, bend, marker; the page with its three Q groups; `PointModPage`), `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/lfo_ui_host.cpp` (the marker and the PHASE, the quick delete and the selection through the touch path, the library: save then load gives an equal model, the lists SHAPES and MORE with every action, the copies, the point modulation; `testShapeLibrary` on a temporary directory), `qslots_host.cpp` (the three groups), `layout_lint_host.cpp` (states editor, editor-loop, editor-pointmod, editor-shapes-list, editor-more-list), gallery states `lfo_editor_*` in `ui_gallery_extra_host.cpp`.

**F2: the grid is stored with the LFO (state version 5).** Both the host chunk and the `.sbpreset` carry the state document, so the grid is in both. `kStateVersion` is 5 (`core/include/synthyboy/state.h`). The entry of an LFO in `content.lfo_shapes` is
`{"lfo":3,"name":"Drawn","smooth":true,"loop_point":1,"grid_x":16,"grid_y":4,"points":[{"x":0,"y":0,"power":0},...]}`
with `grid_x` (divisions of the cycle) and `grid_y` (divisions of the height), each an integer 2 .. 32, written only when stored (0 in `ContentLfoShape::gridX / gridY` = not stored: nothing is written), between `loop_point` and `points`. A document of version 4 or older has no such keys and loads with the grid 0; the editor then takes the DEFAULT grid: the coarsest of 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32 whose lines all the points of the shape lie on (`lfo::inferGrid`), else 8, the same for both directions. A value outside 2 .. 32 in a document is read as "not stored" and the section stays valid; `validateContent` (so the writer) accepts only 0 or 2 .. 32. A reader of version 4 reads a version 5 document as `newer_version` (the host keeps the project) and skips `grid_x` / `grid_y` like any unknown key: MEASURED with the reader of commit a9251b1b (`kStateVersion` 4, built from `git archive` of core/src) on a version 5 document with a grid: status `newer_version`, document version 5, content ok, the shape read in full (3 points, smooth, loop point, name). The grid is part of the editor's data: a change of GRID X / GRID Y or the first open of a shape writes the content (`setLfoShape`) like an edit of the shape. Tests: `core/tests/test_lfo_grid.cpp` (round trip, the exact text, not stored when 0, a version 4 document, values that are no grid, a later version with unknown fields, a preset), `lfo_ui_host.cpp` (the grid reaches the content, two independent grids, SNAP and POINT as Q targets).

## Plugin-drawn pages (overlay build)

**The OSC tab (pages of the oscillator UI).** The five tabs of the OSC function key (`Osc A`, `Osc B`, `Osc C`, `Sub`, `Noise`; views `osca`, `oscb`, `oscc`, `oscsub`, `oscnoise` of `overlay_layout.json`, kind `oscui`, the view's index is the number of the Play selector: 2, 3, 4, 1, 5) are pages the plugin draws entirely, like every other tab: `OscPageUi` (`shell-mpc/overlay/osc_page.h`) in a `PageView`. It is the full-screen oscillator UI that the Play page opens with its full-screen button (`OscUi`, `osc_ui.h`, one class) in its page mode (`OscUi::setPage`): the tab names the oscillator, so the Play selector is neither read nor written, the full screen has no CLOSE button (the name of the oscillator is in its place), and everything else is the full view: the Basic group, the type bar, the tuning column, the knob panel with its Q groups, the detail pages (UNISON, grain window, envelope, modulations), the route popup, the lists and the content browser. The page re-flows the type bar under the header with the display below it and tuning on the right, ensuring the display is at least 240 px tall on the OSC pages (and proportionally on the Play third). The picture of the oscillator in its display rectangle and the live read-outs are `OscDisplay` (`osc_display.h`, shared with Play third): the wave is scaled to at least 80 percent of display height from its own peak, with bipolar fill toward the center zero line (a saw reads as two triangles meeting at the zero crossing), the vertical edge of the saw/square drawn at the cycle boundary, and unison hairlines rendered only as a thin spread around the main curve over the same x range without fanning (off at UNISON 1). Tables with multiple frames keep the 2D view of the current frame in front, with an optional 2D stacked depth view behind. The page lists the four readout targets of its own oscillator (A, B, C; none for Sub and Noise), so the readout holds 68 values with all views. The old picture views of the three oscillators (`OscView`, the layout kind `osc`, the pink curve over a region of a host tab) and the host's own tabs Osc 1 .. Osc 3, Osc1 Shp .. Osc3 Shp and Sub/Nois are removed from the layout, the generator, the skin, the controller and the tests (the production skin keeps them: `20_osc.py` does nothing in the overlay variant). The host widgets of the five kick pages stay under the page, hidden while it is up (`83_pages.py`), and the four Q-Links are Q 1 .. Q 4. Tests: `osc_page_host.cpp` (the oscillator named by the tab, no CLOSE, the Q group, the writes, the detail page, the scene), `page_view_host.cpp` (the stack, the selector not written, the readout targets), `osc_page_ui_demo.cpp` (pictures).

Besides the Play tab, the plugin draws these tabs entirely (a `PageView<Ui>` per view, `shell-mpc/overlay/page_view.h` over the generic `page_view_base.h`, the aliases and the factory `SynthyBoyPageFactory` in `pages.h`; the controller opens the element page of the tab with `View::pageElement()` and keeps one `UiStack` group per tab, so a change of tab closes the detail pages of the previous one). A page has the whole screen (1280 x 800 region, opaque, the markers at the top, the host's Q-Link side panel stays visible), its own Q groups (`Q proxies and the plugin-drawn pages`) and its readout targets (`pages::make(view)->targets`).

| Tab (overlay variant) | View kind (`overlay_layout.json`) | Element | Page id |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Play | `play` | oscillator / envelope / LFO elements in `PlayView` (ONE page since 2026-11-18) | - |
| Filter 1, Filter 2 | `filterui` | `FilterUi` (`filter_ui.h`) | 4 |
| Mixer | `mixer` | `MixerUi` | 5 |
| FX | `fx` | `FxUi` | 6 |
| Mod | `mod` | `ModUi` | 7 |
| Macros | `macros` | `MacroUi` | 8 |
| Master | `master` | `MasterUi` | 9 |
| Voice | `voice` | `VoiceUi` | 10 |
| Env 1 - Env 4 | `envui` | `EnvUi` in page mode | - |
| LFO 1 - LFO 4 | `lfoui` | `LfoUi` in page mode | - |
| Osc A, Osc B, Osc C, Sub, Noise | `oscui` | `OscPageUi` (`osc_page.h`: `OscUi` in page mode + `OscDisplay`) | 1 |

Master and Voice are no longer members of `PlayView`. All elements bind to engine parameters with `ui_bind.h` (`uibind::`: key lookup, plain and normalised writes, `formatValue` with the unit of the parameter, the modulation slots of a parameter, `DestIndex`: the grouped destination list) and open the modulations of a control (long press) and the sources' target list with the shared `ModulationsPage` / `ModTargetsPage`.

**Skin.** `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/83_pages.py` (overlay variant only) hides every host widget of a page tab (except `Overlay Marker *` and `Overlay Instance`) while the plugin's page is open (`RangedEnabling` on the overlay parameter, as `82_play.py` does for Play), sets Q-Link 1 .. 4 to the Q proxies "Q 1" .. "Q 4" and `Bank Direction` to `Row`. `scripts/overlay-skin/mpc-ui-pages-overlay.json` has 24 tabs (the limit of the host is 32; the Model tabs, Mod 3-4, Mod Amt, the single effect tabs and the six host oscillator tabs and Sub/Nois are gone because the plugin's pages hold their controls: the OSC key has the five pages Osc A, Osc B, Osc C, Sub and Noise).

**Filter UI.** A real response curve (log frequency grid, dB scale, the response computed from the parameters: `FilterResponse`), the live cutoff from the readout, a drag bubble with cutoff and resonance, the formant pad with its X / Y / TRANSPOSE / SPREAD, a list of the models and a stepper / list popup of the styles.
