The style selector offers only the styles the selected filter model actually has (`filterModelStyleCount(model)`, classic models 6/6/6/6/2/3/1/2 styles, Serum models 8..26 their own counts), with names matching each style's response type. When the filter model changes and the current style index is above the new count, the style clamps to the last valid style (`count - 1`) and writes the parameter once through the standard user change path so both engine and host see it. A model with exactly one style shows the style control dim and as a plain label (no stepper chevrons, not listable, no popup list). The Q knob and mouse wheel step only through the valid styles without wrapping. ROUTING, three blocks MAIN / TRACK / OUT, one Q group each. In dual F1+F2 mode, the display shows both filters' response curves (in palette colours with F1 / F2 labels) along with the combined response curve computed from actual routing (serial: product of magnitudes; parallel: sum of the two paths with blend weights), with interactive cutoff handles on both curves. Curves clip cleanly to the -48 dB display frame bottom edge without running flat along it. Controls not applicable to the current filter model (such as COMB when not a comb model, CLEAN on Comb/Formant, and VAR on Formant) are consistently dimmed including their value text. The TRACK row (KEYTRACK, ENV 1, VAR, COMB) uses uniform 100 px slots and a uniform toggle look (`knobLike = true`).

**Macro names.** Each of the eight macros has a name (`content.macro_names` of the state and the preset, at most 16 characters, default "Macro n"; format, cleaning rules, compatibility and the choice of the limit: `docs/ui-design/macro-names.md`). The title of a card of the Macros page is the name (capitals, underlined: a button; `MACRO n` without one); a tap on it opens the keyboard page (`ui::TextEntry`, a detail page of the stack, `MacroUi::kPageKeyboard`; no Q group; OK stores the trimmed name through `IInstanceView::setMacroName`, CANCEL / BACK / a tab change leave it; a drag that starts on the name still turns the knob). The Mod page's board, pickers and rows use the name, the chips on knobs the short form of at most six bytes ("MAC n" without a name, "Filter" for "Filter Sweep"; `shell-mpc/overlay/macro_names.h`, the names of the instance on screen are made active by the page view for everything it does, `NamesScope`); a chip of a macro keeps the colour of the macros. The Q proxy of a macro reports the name (`macroParamName`, `shell-mpc/macro_names.h`). Host tests: `core/tests/test_macro_names.cpp`, `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/macro_names_ui_host.cpp` (pages and keyboard), `shell-mpc/tests/macro_names_chunk_host.cpp` (chunk, Q proxy names, the host told; built by `accept.sh` against the overlay build; the rename of a page is the debug file `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_MACRO_NAME_FILE`, a line `n name`, read only when the variable is set). Pictures: `scripts/render-ui-gallery.py` renders the states `Named` and `NamedMods` and the keyboard pages (`Named_keyboard_*.png`).

**Mod matrix and macros.** Tabs ALL / BY SOURCE / BY DEST, rows of 72 px (source, target, depth bar, UNI/BI, ON/OFF, MORE), the slot detail page (auxiliary source, shape, time; delete with a confirmation), the grouped lists, the macros as 8 cards with their targets and the live spectrum strip. The selected row Q group maps Q1 = row select (virtual Q target), Q2 = SOURCE, Q3 = TARGET, Q4 = AMOUNT (chips 1..4 left to right). With 0 rows in use the page replaces the empty list with a quick-add board (`ModSourceBoard`): the 57 sources the engine has as chips in 5 groups (ENVELOPES, LFOS, MACROS, PLAYER, SOUND); a tap on a chip opens the shared target picker (`ModTargetsPage` with the grouped destination list on top) and makes the row in the engine; with rows present the board is gone and the matrix list is displayed as normal. `+ ADD` stays available in all states.

**Mixer and FX.** Strips with slider bars, a fader and a meter fed by `stripPeak`. The Q group of the Mixer page is the strip you touched: a tap anywhere in a strip selects it, the one Q frame and the chips move to that strip (they are published at the touch, not at the next refresh) and the routing line at the top follows it; Q1 to Q4 are LEVEL, PAN, B1 and B2 of that strip. The engine has PAN and the two sends for SUB, A, B, C, NOISE, F1 and F2 only; BUS 1, BUS 2 and MASTER have a level and nothing else, so on those strips PAN, B1 and B2 are left out (`MixerStrip::setPresent`, `MixerFullScreen::setStripMembers`): the controls are not drawn (no dim ghost), take no touch, and have no chip and no place in the frame, while the strip keeps its height and the remaining controls their places. Test: `testMixerQPerStrip` in `mixfx_ui_host.cpp`. The FX page is the chain bar plus the slot editor with a display per type (EQ and compressor are drawn from the textbook biquad and threshold / ratio models, not from the engine's measured response; delay, FX filter and distortion from their parameters), pages of knobs and a Q group per row. With an empty chain the panel replaces the empty state hint with a gallery of the 16 effects the engine has (`FxGallery`: the 13 types of the pool and the 3 splitters): a card per effect with its name and a procedural icon of its transfer curve, frequency response or bands in the look's palette; one tap on a card adds that effect to the end of the Main rack and selects it, with the slot editor replacing the gallery. The pages of the effects are driven by tables ("FX editors" below). `readout.h` publishes `stripPeak[10]` (a level on a 60 dB scale with a release that lets a peak fall within a fraction of a second, atomics, no lock) and `voiceStep[kReadVoices]`; engine tests `own_mixer_strip_peaks_follow_the_sources_and_decay` and `own_voice_control_read_out_has_the_step_of_every_voice`.

**Master and Voice.** Master: two sides (MAIN: OUTPUT and PITCH in the first row, VOICES and GLIDE in the second; and TUNING & MPE: SCALE with keyboard, TUNING, MPE). The switches and the short lists are LED controls (see "LED controls in use"). OUTPUT: the meters (the dB scale in a column of its own, the ramp of the library), the clip indicator (`ClipLabel`: small red CLIP label above the meters that lights when the engine's safety limit acts on sample magnitude > 1.8, holds for 2.0 s, tap clears; independent of host meters), VOLUME, SPREAD and STEREO (three knobs of one size; `master.stereo_routing` is a continuous percentage) and VOICE OVERRIDE (a LED list). PITCH: TUNE (the medium knob), TRANSPOSE, BEND UP, BEND DOWN in one row, LINK under the bend fields. VOICES (three rows): POLY, LEGATO, POLY LEG, PHASE LEG = the group VOICES; VEL TRACK, SAME NOTE, STEAL = VOICES 2 (in reading order); PAD PRESS, PAD % and the voice meter. GLIDE: the two knobs over ALWAYS and SCALED at the left, the curve at the right in the whole height. The note selector keyboard is drawn in palette with dark keys, thin outlines, shorter and narrower black keys positioned above white keys, white keys labeled C..B, in-scale and selected root notes accented, and every key touch target >= 56 px. Every block is a Q group of at most four members in reading order (10 Q groups total across both sides). Inactive groups display quiet idle chips (`QGroup::drawIdle`); tapping any block or member activates its group with an accent frame, and returning to the page restores the default group (OUTPUT on Main, SCALE on Tuning). All 33 parameters of `master.*` and `voice.*` are covered by dedicated controls (including `master.velocity_track` added to VOICES). The live voice count meter displays a row of POLY-many cells lit for active sounding voices beside the voice count text. Voice: 6 Q groups total (STEP header with virtual step/row selectors, length and value; RANDOM 1 with four spread knobs and RANDOM 2 with 10x DETUNE; SCALING with two time knobs and mode stepper; TARGETS 1 with OSC A..SUB toggles and TARGETS 2 with NOISE). The TARGETS block cells fill the full block height (width 74, height 190 px) leaving no empty lower half. Inactive groups display idle chips. All 62 parameters of `voicectl.*` are covered across the controls, grid, and Q groups.

**Review 2 rules (all pages).** One Q group is marked by ONE yellow frame (`QGroup::draw`) and the index chips 1 to 4 on the members' top left corners; a member never draws a keyline of its own (`Pen::control` ignores the Q flag), so a block, a row or a card carries no inner frame. When the members lie inside cards the frame runs around the cards (`QGroup::draw (sb, &around)`: the Macros page frames the four cards of the bank). The quad that is not the active one of a page shows quiet chips (`QGroup::drawIdle`: the second row of an FX page). A switch in a row of knobs has the cell of a knob (`ToggleIcon::State::knobLike`: Filter ENV 1, Env BEATS / LEGATO). Nothing is shown twice: the envelope page has no value boxes (the times are on the knobs and under the graph) and the target block only when the envelope modulates something; the envelope tabs in both the Play view and the full Env page mark envelopes that have modulation targets with a small filled dot (no red underline); the LFO page shows HOST under the shape column and SMOOTH once, as the LED on the label of the SMOOTH time knob; in the key follow sync TRANSPOSE and TUNE stand in the RATE block where the rate knob stood (no fourth block, no dead dial), its instance bar has the ten LFOs and R1 .. R4 in one row (no pager cell), the 13-cell shape grid is Q1 in the RATE group (the redundant shape stepper is removed), TIMING has MODE stepper + PHASE + DELAY (the redundant sync stepper is removed, keeping the header HZ / BPM / KEY buttons), and MOTION has DIRECTION + SWING + STEREO (the redundant MONO switch is removed, keeping the header MONO toggle); RATE knob is large with the value, and secondary knobs use a uniform medium knob size family with no empty block halves. The filter curve is cut at the frame (the plot runs +30 to -48 dB; a stretch under the floor leaves through the bottom edge instead of lying along it). Live displays are never empty boxes: the macro spectrum and the meters of the mixer and the master have a scale in dB (the meters: the scale runs from the floor to full scale, with a mark every 12 dB or at 0 / -6 / -12 / -24 / -48 dB) and a faint base line at rest, the master meter a peak hold, the voice grid a handle at every step of the length (also at zero) and a tint on the sounding step. The mixer strip reads top down LEVEL 1, PAN 2, B1 3, B2 4 and its routing bar draws only the selected strip's path with its destination lit; the FX chain bar has wide cards with quiet "+" places after the add card; the WAVESHAPER display fills its table (grid, scales, the present input level of the rack as a dot, from the bus meters before the racks and the master meter for the main rack). The macro names are not editable: the engine stores none (only the importers read them).

**Review 4 rules (polish).** A Q group whose members lie apart (the LFO page's shape grid and its RATE block) is marked without a frame: `QGroup::setFrameless` draws the four active chips and the members' Q colour, no frame around everything between them, and the adjacency check does not apply. The medium knobs of the LFO page (RISE, SMOOTH, PHASE, DELAY, SWING, STEREO) are one size, a cell of 122 px (the dial at the left with name and value beside it when the cell is 172 px wide or more); RATE is the large knob; the stepper of TIMING and MOTION is at the top of its block. The random LFO page has the same RATE knob (the whole height of the RANDOM block, on the left) with STYLE, SYNC and STEREO beside it. The LFO shape graph is one clean cycle unipolar from the floor; PHASE moves a start marker on the graph, and with RISE > 0 or DELAY > 0 a separate strip above the graph displays the delay / fade-in envelope on a time scale. The Master page has two large knobs only (VOLUME, SPREAD: tall cells), PITCH and GLIDE are a row of four cells (the Q group) with LINK / the curve under it, and every toggle has the look of LEGATO. The Mod source board shows the LFOs as LFO 1 .. 10 and then RLFO 1 .. 4 (display order only: the chips carry the engine's source numbers). The Env graph names the decay time under DECAY and the sustain level under SUSTAIN (the Decay handle's bubble keeps both).

**Tests and images.** `filter_ui_host.cpp`, `mod_ui_host.cpp`, `mixfx_ui_host.cpp`, `mastervoice_ui_host.cpp` (all in `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/`), `page_view_host.cpp` (the real layout, opening, groups, status naming, readout targets) run in `shell-mpc/accept.sh` (the last ones also under AddressSanitizer); the `*_ui_demo.cpp` programs (shared `demo_common.h`) write the demonstration pictures into `$B/ui-images/<element>/look-N` (the collected set is in `.hw-local/mpc-ui-plans/komponens-kepek/`). The old 17-view table lives in `tests/fixtures/legacy_layout.h` for the tests of the controller that do not need the new pages.

**FX band racks of the splitters (`ui/fx_band_board.h`, `fx_ui.h`, `core/src/fx_rack.cpp`).** The engine has ten racks: Main, Bus 1, Bus 2 and the seven band racks 3 .. 9 (L/H low, L/H high, L/M/H low, mid, high, M/S mid, side: `fxl::bandRack / bandOfRack / bandName / rackName`). Only the 13 modules of instance 0 that are no splitters sit in a band rack (the slots of the pool and the splitters have three racks: `fxiNN.rack` has three values, a splitter in a band is repaired to Main by the engine); `synthyboy::fxMove` takes such a module into any of the ten racks and reorders inside one (the positions the band's modules hold are permuted: `core/tests/test_fxrack.cpp`). An effect in a band works only while its splitter is on (`test_wp7.cpp`). The page of a splitter shows ONE LANE PER BAND in the place the display of other effects has (`FxBandBoard`): a tile (LOW / MID / HIGH or MID / SIDE, what the band is: "below 200 Hz", "200 Hz to 3.0 kHz", its number of effects or BYPASS or SPLITTER OFF) and the chain of the band as cards (type picture, power, name) and + ADD. A tap on a card, on the tile or on + ADD OPENS THE BAND (`FxUi::scope_`): the chain bar then holds the cards of that band (every module that sits in the rack, a bypassed one dim), the BACK card first (the splitter and the band: the way up to the splitter's page, where the bar is the main chain's again), and the page of the selected effect is the SAME table page as in the Main chain (the same rows, Q groups on 1 .. 16, REMOVE). The place stands over the name of the effect: "MAIN CHAIN > SPLITTER L/M/H > MID BAND". + ADD in a band offers the types whose module is free (off, and not parked in a band; a module is in one place only): the module is moved to the end of the band and switched on; an empty band shows the gallery of the free types; a drag of a card reorders (in the bar and in a lane); the power of a lane card switches the effect without opening the band; REMOVE of a module switches it off and takes it out of its band (rack Main). The RACK switch of a module has the ten racks (the page follows the effect into its band or back to the buses), the one of a splitter or a slot of the pool three. For this reason the picture of the frequency split (`FxDisplay::Kind::Split`) is gone: the tiles carry the crossover frequencies. Tests: `mixfx_ui_host.cpp` (`testBandRacks`: main -> splitter -> band -> effect -> back; `testEveryBandRack`: all seven racks, all 13 modules to all ten racks by the rack switch and the power of the card writing the module's own keys; `testBandLaneDrag`), `ui_fx_editor_host.cpp` (`testBandBoard`), the lint (`layout_lint_host`: the three splitter pages with lanes and every band page), `ui_gallery_host` (`*_fx_splitter_*_lanes / _band1 .. / _band1_empty`). Parameter coverage (`coverage_host`, FX module): no control 0 before and after; control but no Q 112 before and after (the chain-level keys `.on`, `.rack`, `fx.order.N`, `fxiNN.type / on / rack / order` are operated by the cards' power, the rack switch, the drag of a card and + ADD / REMOVE, not by a knob).

**UI library regression checks (the nine findings of the first coverage round).** `SliderBar` and `Fader` take one wheel notch as one Q-Link step (1 / 128, not the whole range), a `Fader` takes no touch outside its rectangle and both report `capturing ()` (they had a `captured_` of their own that hid the one of `Component`); `MixerStrip::kMinH` (456) is the height its layout needs; `FxChainBar` shows only the cards of the slots it has before it is given any; `ListPopup` takes no release to the left of its rows; `sdf::fitText` gives no text (width 0) to a box too narrow for the ellipsis; `setText` with a capacity of 0 writes nothing; `ModRowSelector` reports a change only when the row changed; the `PictogramSelector` out-of-range turn is by design (a step down from index 5 of 3 cells starts at the last cell). The checks live in the `ui_cov_*` tests (the findings file is gone).

**UI gallery renderer.** `scripts/render-ui-gallery.py` renders every plugin-drawn page of the overlay build at 1280x800 for offline visual reviews without a hardware device. Driven by `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/ui_gallery_host.cpp` through the CPU reference renderer, it captures the Init state and factory presets across all views discovered directly from `overlay_layout.json` (Play 1-3, Macros, Osc A/B/C/Sub/Noise with unison and mod details, Mixer, Filter 1/2, Env 1-4 with/without targets, LFO 1-4 and random LFO, Mod matrix empty/populated, FX gallery and all 16 effect editors (13 types, 3 splitters), Master sides, and Voice). Output images and an `index.html` contact sheet are placed into the `--out` folder (default: under the build tree). Usage: `python3 scripts/render-ui-gallery.py [--build] [--host-bin BIN] [--out DIR] [--presets FILE...]` (pass `--build` to build or rebuild `ui_gallery_host`, or `--host-bin` / `SYNTHYBOY_UI_GALLERY_HOST` to name an existing binary; without `--build` no build is started). Tests: `scripts/tests/test_render_ui_gallery.py` and `ui_gallery_host --test`.

**FX editors: one table per effect (`shell-mpc/ui/fx_layout.h`, `fx_slot_editor.h`).** Every effect the FX page shows has a TABLE of its controls in rows of four cells, named by their engine keys: the 13 types of the pool (`kFxTypeChorus` .. `kFxTypeConvolve`) and the three splitters (`fxl::kKindSplitterLh / Lmh / Ms`, modules 13 .. 15 of the fixed chain, no type of the pool; they are added from the + ADD list and the gallery by switching the module on and moving it to the end of Main). A row is a block of the effect and ONE Q group in reading order (the rows of a page on Q 1 .. 16: four rows a page without a display, two or three under one, as the table says; the table also says the height of the display). The name, range, unit and choices of a control come from the parameter table; control number p of a type of the pool is its parameter p, the controls the type's own list does not have (the splitters, the reverb's `force_enable`) follow. ONE rule (stated in `fx_layout.h`) for what does nothing in the state of the effect, from the `Live` condition of each control (a parameter of the effect and the values it is live under): (1) a cell can hold two controls that exclude each other (RATE / DIVISION, TIME / DIVISION, PRE-DELAY / DIVISION, COMB OFFSET / FORMANT X): it shows the live one; (2) a control that is not live is HIDDEN when no live control follows it in its row (the row closes up, its Q group has fewer members, a row with nothing live is gone and the rows below move up, the pages are made of the rows that are left), else it is drawn DIM in its place, takes no touch and has no Q member. The conditions come from the engine (`fx_compressor.h`: the bands modes, the limiter latency; `fx_reverb.h`: room in Nitrous, feedback in Nitrous and Basin; Distortion filter order; EQ mid 2 mode; FX filter model; Utility mono bass; the sync of the delayed effects). A control kind follows its values: a continuous value is a knob, a whole number (VOICES, UNISON) a NUMBER FIELD (`NumberField::maxBox`: the drawn field is limited and at the left of its cell, the touch zone is the cell), an on / off value a LED toggle, 3 to 8 values a LED list where the cell has the room, else (and for longer lists) the stepper with its popup. The splitters have chain-card icons and, in the place of a display, the RACKS OF THEIR BANDS (next paragraph). Tests: `ui/tests/ui_fx_editor_host.cpp` (the tables against the engine, the rule, the pages, the number kind), `mixfx_ui_host.cpp` (every effect in every state of its conditions: one control per cell, no page with fewer than three live controls, the closing-up rule, the splitters), `coverage_host` (every FX parameter of the engine has a control: the FX gap rows with no control are 0, they were 14: the three splitters and `reverb.force_enable`).

**Names of laws and types (`shell-mpc/ui/choice_names.h`).** The engine's choice lists carry the working names of the models they were measured against ("Vital", "Serum"); the screen shows what the choice does, read from `core/engine-own`: EQ law (`eq.law`, label BAND SHAPE): State variable / Biquad (the Digital filter's sections / the textbook biquads); Convolve law (label SCALING): Relative / Seconds (size 0.25 .. 4 x the impulse and decay a share of its length / size 0.1 .. 10 x and decay up to 40 s, the impulse normalised); oscillator unison law (`oscN.unison.law`, label VOICE LAW): Equal power / Fixed gain (the blend weights normalised to equal power / a measured gain per voice count); reverb algorithm (`reverb.type`, label ALGORITHM): the first one is Dense (the sixteen line network); the third compressor mode: Peak (the peak compressor). `ui::shownChoice (key, index, engineName)` and `ui::shownLabel (key)` are the one place; the oscillator pages call them for `oscN.unison.law` (no oscillator page shows it yet: it is an open item of the oscillator pages).

**Modulations of one control.** A control nothing modulates opens a page that is the BOARD of every source (`ModulationsPage::board`, the same `ModSourceBoard` as the empty matrix, filled by `uibind::fillSourceBoard`): a tap on a chip makes the modulation of THIS control from that source (an `AddRequest`, the owner's existing handler), no stepper to wade through. With rows present the board is gone and the compact add row stays under them. The Env, Filter and LFO pages fill the board; a page that does not (the oscillator page, until its owner calls `uibind::fillSourceBoard (mods_.board)` before `mods_.load`) keeps the compact add row.

**Knob with a LED on its label (`Knob::State::led`).** A knob can carry the on / off switch of what it sets as a LED in front of its name (SMOOTH on the LFO page: the smoothing and its time are ONE control): off = the knob is drawn dim and takes only the switch, a tap on the text column (`Knob::ledHit ()`, at least 56 px) switches (an `Index` change 0 / 1 with the knob's id). The Q member of the switch parameter is the undrawn `LedSwitchProxy` beside the knob's chip (LFO group SHAPE), so `lfoN.smooth` stays reachable from the Q-Links. Test: `ui_led_host.cpp`.

**Knob touch precision and numeric entry.** Any drag movement of at least 1 px changes the parameter value (no dead zone; tap-vs-drag threshold at most 2 px so a tap is still a tap). Drag sensitivity adapts dynamically based on movement speed: finger movement below 80 px/s engages fine mode (0.1 dB per 2 px for dB parameters, or 1/1000th of range per 2 px for non-dB parameters); faster movement uses standard coarse mapping (200 px for full range). Speed transitions are smooth and applied per-delta without value jumps. Tapping directly on the value text opens numeric entry (`TextEntry` on the Symbols layer) pre-filled with the current value; confirming with OK sets the value clamped to parameter range and rounded to 0.1 dB, while Cancel leaves the value unchanged.

**LFO key follow.** In the key follow sync the rate is the note's: the rate knob is not on the page, TRANSPOSE and TUNE stand in the RATE block (their own Q group right after RATE, no fourth block), and the display shows the SHAPE at full height: `LfoDisplay::compute` smooths in time only where there is a rate (the picture used to apply the smoothing at the rate of a made-up note: 45 percent of the height, shifted). Test: `testKeyFollowCurve` in `lfo_ui_host.cpp`.

**Voice and Filter (led-pages).** The Voice ROW is a LED list of the six rows (56 px rows, two columns of three, `VoiceScreen::rowList`) in a block at the left of the grid; it is Q2 of the header group (frameless: its members lie apart). The Filter page's F1 / F2 / F1+F2 is the LED list CURVES in the top bar: which curves the display draws (F1, F2, or both and the resultant) and the filter the knobs edit; it is not the ROUTING list (Parallel / Series) and not the Filter 1 / Filter 2 tabs of the host, which only choose where the page opens.

## Browser tile

The host displays a graphic tile in the Sounds > INSTRUMENTS plugin selector when the package contains `Plugin Skins/browser_images/soundsmode.png`.

- **Dimensions**: exactly 270 x 110 pixels PNG. The host does not downscale larger images; it crops them to the top-left 270 x 110 pixels.
- **Design**: custom vector and gradient drawing with the SynthyBoy wordmark, analog hybrid synthesizer description, multi-layer glowing waveforms, and status indicators. It contains no third-party branding, stock elements, or vendor marks (no Akai, AIR, or NI trademarks).
- **Generator**: `scripts/gen-mpc-tile.py` draws the tile at 4x supersampling using Pillow and downsamples with Lanczos filtering. It honors font licensing by defaulting to the repository's open font assets or Titillium Web OFL if present.
- **Packaging**: `scripts/mpc-skin-extras/98_tile.py` copies the generated asset into the skin folder during skin generation. `scripts/build-mpc.sh` ensures the file is present in the final package.
- **Tests**: `scripts/tests/test_mpc_tile.py` checks that the tile exists, matches the 270 x 110 PNG dimensions, contains valid non-empty procedural artwork, and is copied into the skin package.


## Start-up time

The first open of the plugin on the Key 61 (32-bit Cortex-A17, 1.6 GHz, slow storage) looked frozen; later program changes were immediate. Where the time goes, from the code (cost classes are estimates for that CPU, `cheap` < 10 milliseconds, `tens` 10 to 100 milliseconds, `hundreds` 100 milliseconds to 1 s, `seconds` > 1 s; the status lines below replace the estimates with numbers).

The start-up measurement of the host (`dlopen`, `VSTPluginMain`, effOpen) is in "Observed on the device" above, as "Start-up of the plugin".

| # | When | Thread | What runs | Cost class (before) | Now |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | dlopen | host | static initialisers: the oversampling decimator tables (Kaiser design, namespace scope), `OwnEngine` key tables are lazy | tens | unchanged |
| 2 | `VSTPluginMain`, `PluginInstance()` | host | output stage, `createEngine` (own): parameter tables, `ParamIndex` (resolved once) | tens | unchanged (`engine_create`) |
| 3 | same | host | content store object + pack root: no scan, no hashing; content files are read and SHA-256 verified only when a state names them (`UserContentStore::readFile`). The 11.5 MB content pack costs nothing at start | cheap | unchanged |
| 4 | same | host | `scanPrograms`: 136 preset files of about 50 KB each (6.9 MB): every file was read whole (64 KB buffer) to take the name and category from its first 200 bytes. Cold storage: one seek per file plus 13 blocks each | seconds cold, tens warm | first 4 KB per file: hundreds cold (136 seeks), tens warm (`scan_presets`) |
| 5 | same | host | default parameter values into the engine and the mirror (about 1100 `setParam`) | tens | unchanged (`default_params`) |
| 6 | `VSTPluginMain` | host | AEffect, `startLive` (a thread), `startOverlay`: before, `Controller::readoutTargets` (now `SynthyBoyPageFactory::targetsOfAllPages`, `overlay/pages.h`) constructed all 24 pages and `Service::attach` constructed the controller with all 24 views again, then attached | hundreds | slot assignment only; the readout list is built by the display thread after its first report, the views by the overlay thread's first tick (`vst_main_rest`, details `start_live`, `start_overlay`, `overlay_readout`) |
| 7 | effOpen, effSetSampleRate, effSetBlockSize | host | store only | cheap | unchanged |
| 8 | program / parameter queries: effGetProgramNameIndexed (137 or 161 times), effGetParamName/Label/Display, effGetParameterProperties, effCanBeAutomated (about 1100 each) | host | table lookups, string copies; names of programs from memory | cheap each, tens together | unchanged |
| 9 | effGetChunk / effSetChunk / effSetProgram (first) | host | JSON of the state (about 50 KB, 1100 parameters), `applyState`, `setContent` (content files named by the state are read and hashed), then `readyContent` | tens; content files: hundreds per MB read on slow storage | plus: the built-in tables the state names are built here if not built yet (see 11): hundreds each for a morph table, once per process |
| 10 | effMainsChanged (on), first | host | profile selection (reads `/proc/device-tree/model`, writes the report file once), `engine->prepare` | seconds | see 11 and 12 (`engine_prepare`) |
| 11 | in prepare | host | the built-in wavetable bank: 16 tables, eight of them 64 frames: about 520 frames x 18 mip levels, one double precision complex FFT each (sizes 256 .. 2048), taper and spectrum copy, 29 MB, up to 3 helper threads | seconds | the 7 one cycle tables (about 20 milliseconds) and the tables the current parameters name; any other table is built when it is first chosen, by a low priority thread (`open_ms_detail_wavetables` is the build time summed over threads) |
| 12 | in prepare | host | the voice pool (36 voices: 3 wavetable oscillators with 56 KB morph buffers, 3 library oscillators with 80 sample, 16 grain and 4 spectral voices each, filters) and the effects (pool and three racks, every module built per rack: reverb and delay buffers) | hundreds | unchanged: needs the status numbers (`open_ms_detail_prepare_voices`, `_fx`) before it is reduced; a design is below |
| 13 | overlay thread, first tick | overlay | `buildViews` (24 pages' scenes), page detector, the GPU helper's start (`gpu_helper_startup_ms: 584` measured), font atlas and SDF data (constant tables: no work), first frame | seconds | now off the host's thread already; `open_ms_total_ui` is the time to the first frame |
| 14 | display thread | display | LFO cursors, spectrum analyser (allocates once), Q / Play proxies, polls every 100 milliseconds idle | cheap | unchanged |
| 15 | first `processReplacing` | audio | one block; the tables the state names are ready; per block the engine picks each oscillator's table (below) | cheap | unchanged |

### Wavetables: how a table gets to the audio thread

Each of the 16 built-in tables is built at most once per process and then kept (immutable). A build is the expensive part (about 64 frames x 18 mip levels of double precision FFTs for the eight morph tables); the cost therefore exists only for the first use of each table.

- **Program or state load** (effSetProgram, effSetChunk, the first prepare): `applyState` calls `IEngine::readyContentFor (values)` BEFORE it sets the parameters: the tables the incoming values name are built on the host's thread, then the parameters are set. Index and table take effect together: the engine derives each oscillator's table from the same snapshot of the parameters the voices read, once per block (`TickGlobals::wtResolved`), so a voice never sees the new index with a table that does not belong to it. A table another thread is making is waited for, never built twice.
- **A table index moved live** (knob, Q-Link, automation; tables are not modulatable): `setParam` stores the request in the oscillator's slot (an atomic store, lock free, audio thread safe). A low priority builder thread builds the LATEST request of each of the six slots (osc 1 to 3 wavetable, osc 1 to 3 spectral filter table); a table a knob swept past is not built. Until the table is finished the oscillator KEEPS PLAYING THE TABLE IT HAD (`wtHeld`); there is no substitute and no saw. When the builder has finished, the table is published with one release store and the engine takes it at the start of the next block. The audible behaviour is: the old table continues for the length of the build (the first use of a morph table: a few hundred ms on the Key 61), then the new one starts. There is no cross fade beyond the engine's own per period smoothing.
- The saw fallback keeps its original meaning only: "Preset table" whose content is missing.
- **A table FILE** (the wavetable browser, below) is not built by the lazy builder: the display thread decodes it with the content (`IEngine::setContent`, `ContentSet::build`, FFTs of its frames at every mip level, tens to hundreds of milliseconds on the Key 61 for 64 frames) and publishes it with one pointer; the plugin sets `oscN.wavetable` to "Preset table" only after that, so the oscillator plays the table it had until the file table is there (the amber bar is on meanwhile).
- **Loading mark:** while `IEngine::contentPending ()` is true the plugin-drawn pages show it, see "Loading mark" below.
- **Close:** the builder is reference counted by the lazy engines. The destructor of the last one (effClose) sets the stop flag and joins; the builder checks it between two frames of a table and in its 10 milliseconds sleep, so it stops within one frame of the table in the making (a few ms on the Key 61) plus 10 milliseconds. A table in the making is dropped: it is never published, so nothing half built is read. The builder thread holds no pointer to an engine or a plugin instance (the tables live in the process-wide bank, which outlives every instance), so closing while a table is being built touches no freed memory. Test source: `own_lazy_engine_open_close_repeat` (open, change the table, close at once, 20 times).
- Offline renders and the other shells (`Quality::lazyTables` false) build all tables in `prepare ()` as before.

**Precomputed tables on disk (evaluated, not done).** A package step could write the 16 built tables (all mip levels and spectra) to `Content/tables/*.bin` and the plugin could map them. Reasons against: (1) size: about 33 MB on disk (32.6 MB for the eight morph tables, 63.6 KB per frame), 4 MB to read for one morph table, which on the Key 61's storage costs about what the build costs (the cold read and the FFTs are the same order), so the gain exists only with a warm page cache; (2) byte exactness: the build uses `std::complex<double>` FFTs and `cos`/`sin` from the C library; a tool run on the build machine (or under emulation) does not give bit-identical floats to the device's own build (different libm, fused multiply add on AArch64), and the rendered samples must stay identical; a tool that runs on the device at install time would be the only exact one, and that is the build we already have; (3) real time: a mapped table takes page faults on first touch, a hazard on the audio thread; it would need a read ahead and a touch of every page on the builder thread before the hand over (cost again). If the numbers (`open_ms_detail_wavetables`, and the time of the first use of a table) show the first use is too slow, the exact way to make it faster is to build the frames of a table on two builder threads (the frames are independent), not to cache.

**Voice pool and effect racks (design, not implemented).** The same principle (constructor and prepare short, heavy work on an interruptible background thread, the audio path on what is ready, lock free hand over, the host never waits) applies to the 36 voices and the three racks. Design: `prepare` builds the first `voiceCap + 4` voices (8 + 4 on tier low) and the effect modules of the effect types the program names; a builder thread builds the rest. Per voice and per rack an atomic `ready` flag, set with release after the object is complete; the audio thread allocates a note only to a voice whose flag is set (acquire load), so a note-on that finds no ready voice uses the ready ones (the voice stealing it already does when all voices are busy); a rack whose flag is not set passes the signal through unchanged (the bypass the engine already has), and the rack's module is taken over at a block boundary. The cap can be raised by `setQuality` without a prepare: the builder then builds the missing voices and the cap in force is `min (cap, ready voices)`. Once every voice is ready the output is identical to today's: the voices are the same objects built by the same code, only later. Not implemented because (a) it touches the voice allocation and the voice stealing order, which fix the sample output of every polyphonic test, and a change that must keep every sample identical cannot be verified without running the engine tests (this task writes code without building); (b) the numbers `open_ms_detail_prepare_voices` and `_prepare_fx` decide whether it is needed. Ready for implementation after those numbers, with a test that renders the same notes with all voices ready and compares.

### Loading mark (UI change for review)

While a wavetable the current parameters name is still being built, every plugin-drawn page (`PageView`, `shell-mpc/overlay/page_view.h`) draws a 4 px amber bar (`0xFFB000`) along the top edge of the tab, from the left edge to the Q-Link panel's cut (`edgeX_`), over the page. No text, no touch target, no layout change, nothing moves; it appears and disappears with the table. Plumbing: `IInstanceView::tablesLoading ()` (default false; `OverlayView` answers `engine->contentPending ()`, a relaxed read of the six table parameters and the bank's state), `PageView::update` compares it with the last value and marks the picture changed. The compact Play views, the filter / LFO / envelope views and the host's own widgets show nothing.

### The preset index

`scripts/build-preset-index.py` (run by `scripts/build-mpc.sh` after the presets are copied; usable on `content/presets`) writes `Content/presets/index.json`: a header (`index_version`, `count`, `listing_hash`) and one line per preset (relative path, name, category, browser group from `scripts/factory-preset-categories.json`, size, a FNV-1a 64 hash of the file's bytes as an integrity check (not security), container and state version). At start the plugin reads only this file. Stale-index rule: the index is trusted when (1) it parses, (2) its version is 1, (3) the listing hash in its header matches the hash of its own list, and (4) a directory listing of the folder (names and sizes through readdir and stat, no file is opened) gives exactly the same names and sizes (the same listing hash). Otherwise (missing, unparsable, another version, a file added, removed or changed in size) the plugin falls back to the scan (the first 4 KB of every file) and writes one line to the developer log (`startup`: "N programs from scan (<reason>)"). User presets outside the factory folder are not in the index and keep the scan. The order is one rule, `sortPrograms` (category, name case-insensitive, path), used by the scan and by the index, so the program numbers and the Sounds browser files do not move; Init is program 0 before either. A preset edited in place with the same size is not detected by the listing check: run the script again (the packaging does). Test source: `shell-mpc/tests/preset_index_host.cpp` (index order equals scan order for the repository's presets, stale and damaged index fall back).

### The status lines

In `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status.txt` (every build; in a release build with `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status-on` present):

- `open_ms_<step>` for `engine_create content_store scan_presets default_params vst_main_rest host_until_first_call host_until_resume engine_prepare host_until_first_audio`: the time from the previous milestone in time to this one; `n/a` = not reached yet. Time zero is the first entry into `VSTPluginMain` of the process (the record describes the first instance).
- `open_ms_total`: time zero to the end of the first processed audio block; the parts above add up to it. `open_ms_total_ui`: time zero to the first frame of our UI shown.
- `open_ms_detail_*` (inside the steps, not added): `start_live start_overlay overlay_readout engine_construct wavetables prepare_voices prepare_fx`.
- `host_call_max_ms_<call>: <longest> (<n> calls, <sum> ms in all)` for effOpen, effSetProgram, effGetProgramName, effGetProgramNameIndexed, effGetChunk, effSetChunk, effMainsChanged, effSetSampleRate, effSetBlockSize, `param_info_calls` and `other` (everything else except effProcessEvents).

The same milestones go to the developer log (category `startup`, `docs/ui-components/LOGGING.md`).

### First measurement on the Key 61 (profile low, test project, first instance) and what the new lines split

The status lines of that run (measured, in milliseconds) are in "Observed on the device" above, as "Status lines of the start-up run".

`host_until_resume` and `host_until_first_audio` are intervals between two of OUR stamps, so they contain the host's time and every call of the host into us that falls in them (effSetChunk with the project's state, which builds the tables the state names; program and parameter queries; the first effGetChunk). They are therefore not "host time" by themselves. The status file now splits them:

- `open_ms_ours_in_calls_before_resume`: the time we spent inside the host's calls before its resume call (the part of `host_until_resume` that is ours).
- `open_ms_ours_in_calls_resume_to_first_audio`: the time we spent inside ALL host calls (effMainsChanged excluded: that is `engine_prepare`) from the resume call to the first audio block.
- `open_ms_detail_first_block`: the first processed block itself.
- `open_ms_host_only_until_first_audio`: `host_until_first_audio` minus the two lines above that fall into it: the time in which the host calls nothing of ours, written as "(of <interval>, ours <x>)".

Reading: if `open_ms_host_only_until_first_audio` is close to 2401, the 2.4 s is the host's own (loading the project, its other plugins, its audio engine start) and nothing of ours waits in it; `host_call_max_ms_*` names which call of ours was the longest.

### The UI's way to its first frame

The first pictures, the GPU helper start and the `open_ms_at_*` status lines: the measured sequence is in "Observed on the device" above, as "First pictures and the GPU helper".

### What the host waits for

Every call of the host is answered on the host's thread. The longest work each can do now:

| Call | Longest work |
|---|---|
| `VSTPluginMain` | engine, store, preset names (136 x 4 KB), parameter defaults, two thread starts; no page construction, no helper start |
| effOpen, effSetSampleRate, effSetBlockSize, effGetVstVersion, effCanDo, effGetEffectName and the other string calls | a copy |
| effGetProgramName(Indexed), effGetParamName / Label / Display, effGetParameterProperties, effCanBeAutomated | a table lookup and a copy; one short mutex (`programMutex`) for the program name |
| effSetProgram | read one preset file (about 50 KB), parse, build the built-in tables the state names (`readyContentFor`; a morph table: hundreds of ms, once per process), apply the parameters, `setContent` (reads and hashes the content files the state names) |
| effSetChunk | the same, from the chunk |
| effGetChunk | save the state (about 50 KB) under `chunkMutex`; copy into one of four buffers |
| effMainsChanged (on), first and when the rate or block size changed | the engine's prepare: one cycle tables, the tables the parameters name, the voice pool, the effects. Hundreds of ms to a second or two (numbers: `open_ms_detail_prepare_*`, `host_call_max_ms_effMainsChanged`) |
| effProcessEvents, processReplacing | the audio thread: no allocation, no lock |
| effClose | waits for the display thread at most 1500 milliseconds and for the overlay's lock at most 2000 milliseconds (`kLiveJoinMs`, `kDetachWaitMs`); the last instance also waits for the overlay thread to end, at most 2500 milliseconds; normally a few ms |

The display thread's calls into the host (`audioMasterAutomate`, `UpdateDisplay`) are made only after the host has called us once, and never after effClose began.

## Memory on the Key 61

Measured 2026-11-20/21 on the MPC Key 61 (armv7, MPC OS 3.10.1.12, develop `bf18c706`). The device reports `MemTotal` 4,113,388 kB (4 GB, not 2 GB) and has no swap (`SwapFree` 0): the OOM killer is the only limit.

**Engine probe** (`synthyboy_test_own_memory` built static for armv7 with `scripts/build-arm32-test.sh`, run on the device; the Mac gives 67.2 MB and 2515 allocations for tier low). One instance of tier low: 4761 allocations, 72.38 MB heap (peak 105.6 MB), the prepare times are in the probe output (`.hw-local/mpc-perf/`); four instances: 289.5 MB (peak 322.8 MB). The tiers xl and high print the same bytes as low (not understood), and the probe's `rss_kb` column is not usable. The built-in 33.1 MB wavetable bank is warmed before the counters start and exists once per process.

**The real host** (test project Sbtest, one Spectral preset (Spectral Vowel Freeze) per SynthyBoy track, tier low, one note held, readings 8 to 20 s after the preset load, nothing saved):

| SynthyBoy tracks | MPC VmRSS | VmHWM | MemAvailable | MemFree | VmRSS per added track | threads |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 996,484 kB | 1,095,532 kB | 2,707,872 kB | 1,193,072 kB | | 53 |
| 2 | 1,077,256 kB | 1,095,532 kB | 2,627,048 kB | 1,112,248 kB | +80,772 kB | 55 |
| 3 | 1,166,656 kB | 1,166,656 kB | 2,536,968 kB | 1,022,152 kB | +89,400 kB | 57 |
| 4 | not measured: the host app crashed while the 4th track was being added (see below) | | | | | |
| Sbtest reopened from disk after the restart (one track) | 938,436 kB | 1,063,400 kB | 2,722,884 kB | 1,206,692 kB | | 53 |

About 80 to 90 MB of host RSS per added SynthyBoy track (a little above the 72 MB heap of the probe). No `VmSwap`, no OOM or kill line in `dmesg` at any reading. Extrapolation (NOT measured): a fourth track would leave about 2.45 GB available. Memory did come back to the one-track level after the reopen.

The host app (`MPC`, pid 15978) crashed at 00:27:46 (a Sentry crash envelope was sent) while the plugin list was being scrolled for the fourth track, with the dialog of track 8 open; it restarted as pid 7135 (uptime 2 days: not a reboot). MemAvailable was about 2.5 GB just before, so memory is not the cause. The journal gives no cause; whether it is reproducible, or specific to the flick gestures of that run, is open (see the open items list in the repository root).

## Logging in the plugin

Every file the plugin and the GPU helper write, and which thread writes it. "Host / audio" says whether a write can ever block the host's main thread or the audio thread.

### Default package build (debug hooks OFF, callback statistics OFF, `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEV_LOG` ON: what the measuring packages write; NOT the release package, see "What a release package writes" below)

| File | When | Size | Thread | Blocks host / audio? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status.txt` | about once a second and at view changes (`statusDue_`), replaced atomically (temp file + rename) | 6.9 kB, 182 lines | overlay thread | no / no: the host waits on the overlay thread only in effClose (bounded 2 s) and the write happens outside the controller's lock |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-profile.txt` | once, at the first `profileInForce ()` (first effMainsChanged on) | 248 B | host thread, in prepare | the host's thread for the length of one small write; not the audio thread |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-status.txt` | only when a poll fails (`PollGuard`), up to 8 lines | < 2 kB | display or overlay thread | no / no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-egl.txt` | once per helper start | 865 B | the GPU helper process | no / no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu.log` | the helper's stdout and stderr, a few lines per helper start and error; trimmed to 128 KB at each start | 402 B | the GPU helper process | no / no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-minfreq.orig`, `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-zpos-rest` | state the plugin needs (GPU clock's original setting, the plane's rest value), written when they change | tens of bytes | overlay thread | no / no (not logging; kept) |
| `/sys/class/devfreq/...` | the GPU clock raised and put back | - | overlay thread | not logging |

Read every second by the overlay thread (not logging): `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-off`, `-rot`, `-nogpu`, `-detail`, `-fps`, `-look`, `-gpuclock`.

### Debug-hooks build (`SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEBUG_HOOKS`), in addition

| File | When | Size | Thread | Blocks? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log.txt` | while `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log` exists; the ring is filled by any thread (the audio thread included: no allocation, no lock), written by the display thread every tick | up to 512 KB | display thread | no / no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log-chunk-get.json`, `-chunk-set.json` | a copy of each chunk, written on the next tick | up to 8 MB | the host thread copies (allocates), the display thread writes | the host's thread for a copy; not the audio thread |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log-dump.txt` | on request file | tens of kB | display thread | no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-curve.txt` | on request file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-dump` | kB | overlay thread | no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-frame.raw` and `.txt` | while `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-dump` exists, once a second at most (a readback of the frame) | the frame, 1 to 4 MB | the GPU helper | no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-select-program` | on request file | tens of B | display thread | no |
| `/tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack` | written after program selection or error | tens of B | display thread | no |

### Remote program select hook (`SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEBUG_HOOKS` only)

To enable automated scripts (such as an overnight acceptance run stepping through all 160 factory presets) to switch programs without touch screen interaction, builds with `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEBUG_HOOKS=ON` support remote program selection through `/tmp` files:
- **Compiled out entirely otherwise:** when `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEBUG_HOOKS` is OFF (the default and for release packages), the hook is `#if`-ed out completely; no `/tmp` file is checked, and no strings exist in the binary.
- **Thread model:** polled on the non-audio display thread (`liveLoop`) every tenth of a second (zero heap allocation when idle, never polled on the audio thread).
- **Thread-safe handover while audio runs:** the program switch invokes the exact code path `effSetProgram` uses. State content, wavetables, samples, parameters, and output limiter are loaded into the engine using the same thread-safe atomics and engine mutexes that protect host UI thread program changes against `processReplacing`.
- **Request file:** `/tmp/synthyboy-select-program` (environment variable `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_SELECT_PROGRAM_FILE` overrides the path for tests).
  * Format: a single line containing either a 0-based decimal program index (in `effGetProgramNameIndexed` order, 0 .. `numPrograms - 1`) or an exact program name (e.g. `Init` or `Space Wobble Bass`).
  * As soon as detected, the file is read and immediately deleted (`unlink`).
- **Host update:** after selection, `programNameRefreshDue` is set and `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` is sent so the host PRESET display pop-up and field follow.
- **Acknowledgement file:** `/tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack` (written atomically via temporary file and rename):
  * Success format: `<counter> ok <index> <name>` (e.g. `1 ok 0 Init`, `2 ok 1 Space Wobble Bass`).
  * Error format: `<counter> error <reason>` (e.g. `error out of range`, `error unknown name`).
  * `<counter>` is a monotonic integer counter starting at 1 incremented on every written ack line.

Example commands:
```bash
  # Step by program index:
  echo 0 > /tmp/synthyboy-select-program
  cat /tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack   # 1 ok 0 Init

  echo 1 > /tmp/synthyboy-select-program
  cat /tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack   # 2 ok 1 Space Wobble Bass

  # Step by exact preset name:
  echo "Space Wobble Bass" > /tmp/synthyboy-select-program
  cat /tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack   # 3 ok 1 Space Wobble Bass

  # Error handling:
  echo 9999 > /tmp/synthyboy-select-program
  cat /tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack   # 4 error out of range

  echo "Unknown Preset" > /tmp/synthyboy-select-program
  cat /tmp/synthyboy-select-program.ack   # 5 error unknown name
```

Option builds: `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_CALLBACK_STATS` writes `/tmp/synthyboy-callback-stats.txt` (and `.2` ..) every 5 s from its own low priority thread; `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OVERLAY_BENCH` (helper) writes `/tmp/sb-bench-stats.txt`, `/tmp/sb-pace-trace.txt`.

### What a release package writes (`scripts/build-mpc.sh --release`, `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEV_LOG` OFF, checked against the code 2026-11-19)

- Writes NO periodic file: no `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status.txt` (`controller.h` `writeStatus`, `#if ! SYNTHYBOY_MPC_WRITE_FILES`: returns unless the switch file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status-on` was there at the last look, read once a second), no `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-profile.txt` (`device_profile.h`), no `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-status.txt` (`poll_guard.h`: counts and lines stay in memory), no `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-egl.txt` (helper `main.cpp`), no `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu.log` (`helper_transport_linux.h`: the helper's output goes to `/dev/null`), no developer log.
- Writes a status file ONLY on request: `touch /tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status-on` makes the overlay thread write `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status.txt` again (once a second); removing the switch file stops it (the old file stays). A release package that was never asked has no status file, and that is the intended state.
- Still writes the two files that carry state the plugin needs (not logs): `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-minfreq.orig` (only while the GPU clock is raised, `gpu_clock.h`) and `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-zpos-rest` (the plane's rest zpos, `drm_linux.h`, removed again when the plane is given back). So `synthyboy-overlay-*` files may exist in /tmp of a release run only while those states are held.
- Switch files the plugin only READS (`-off`, `-rot`, `-nogpu`, `-detail`, `-fps`, `-look`, `-gpuclock`, `-status-on`) are unchanged. On-request dump files (`-dump`, `-curve.txt`) are written only when asked.

### What a public release should still write, and the option (the proposal this section implemented)

Proposal: nothing periodically. A status file only on request, through a switch file; no report, probe or error files; no log of the helper; no developer log. Failures stay visible in the host's own log through the plugin's refusal to load, not through `/tmp`.

The single build option is `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEV_LOG` (CMake, default ON so that today's builds and the device measurements keep working; `scripts/build-mpc.sh --release` sets it OFF, the release flips the default). OFF: `MPCKIT_DEV_LOG` 0 (no developer log code or strings) and `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_WRITE_FILES` 0 (`shell-mpc/write_files.h`): the periodic status file is written only while `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status-on` exists (read once a second with the other switch files, on the overlay thread: the status write was never on a thread the host or the audio thread waits for), the profile report, the helper's probe file and the PollGuard report are not written, the helper's output goes to `/dev/null`. The debug-hooks build is separate (never in a package). The developer log itself is described in `docs/ui-components/LOGGING.md`.
